===== PAGE 5 =====
•
PON network adopts P2MP structure, single PON connect with multiple users , PON 
network protection mechanism will improve reliability of our network.

===== PAGE 6 =====
•
ITU-T G.984.1 (2008 edition) defines Type B and Type C GPON line protection 
mechanisms and retires the Type A and Type D protection mechanisms. The reason is 
that the Type A mode uses fiber backup, which has nothing to do with devices and has 
no practical value.  Type D uses a complex hybrid networking mode, resulting in high 
costs. However, the OLT dual homing mode is added to the standard. 
•
In Type B and Type C protection, multiple PON ports are configured as a protection 
group. A protection group manages the switchover relationship between member ports, 
records the status of members, and manages the configuration data and status that 
are relevant to the protection relationship between members. 

===== PAGE 7 =====

===== PAGE 8 =====
•
The left figure shows the typical network of GPON Type B single-homing protection, in 
which the active and standby PON ports on the OLTs and the active and standby fibers 
are protected. 
•
The right figure shows the typical network of GPON Type B dual-homing protection, in 
which the active and standby OLTs, active and standby PON ports on the OLTs, and 
active and standby fibers are protected.

===== PAGE 9 =====
•
Advantage: Networking, OLT/ONU management, and service provisioning are simple. 
•
Disadvantage: A faulty OLT may cause service interruption. Multiple fibers are often 
put into the same pipe. As a result, there is a high probability that 2 fibers are 
interrupted at the same time. 
•
Use scenario: Single-homing can  protect important services for customers, such as 
enterprise and eNodeB private line services. 
•
In a single-homing network, 2 PON ports on an OLT, one working as the active port 
and the other one as standby, cannot forward packets at the same time. When the 
active path is interrupted due to a fiber or PON port fault, the ONU quickly switches 
services to the OLT connected to the standby line. Automatic switching can be 
triggered when one of the following conditions is met:
▫
A backbone fiber cut occurs.
▫
The active PON port of an OLT is faulty.
▫
The line quality deteriorates.

===== PAGE 10 =====
•
The working port is in the active state. The protection port is in the standby state.
•
Upon detecting a loss of signal (LOS) alarm (generated due to the active fiber cut), 
the working PON port disables the transmission of its optical transceiver.
•
After detecting the LOS alarm of the active PON port, the protection PON port enables 
the transmission of its optical transceiver and performs ONU ranging. 
•
If the fiber of the protection port is normal and the ONU ranging is successful, the LOS 
clear alarm is reported. 
•
The working port is switched to the standby state, and the protection port is switched 
to the active state. Till now, the protection switching is completed.

===== PAGE 11 =====
•
The working port is in the active state. The protection port is in the standby state.
•
Upon detecting an LOS alarm (generated because all ONUs go offline), the working 
PON port disables the transmission of its optical transceiver. 
•
After detecting the LOS alarm of the active PON port, the protection PON port enables 
the transmission of its optical transceiver and performs the ONU detection. 
•
The OLT keeps checking for ONUs on the working and protection PON ports until it 
detects an ONU going online. 
•
When an ONU goes online and a protection port is detected, the PON port switching is 
performed. If a working port is detected, the working port continues to work. 

===== PAGE 12 =====

===== PAGE 13 =====

===== PAGE 14 =====
•
In a dual-homing network, 2 OLTs, one working as the active device and the other one 
as standby, cannot forward packets at the same time. Customers need to manually 
configure the same service data on the 2 ONTs. They must ensure that the ONU 
quickly switches services from the active OLT to the standby OLT, shortening service 
interruption time. When the active path is interrupted due to an active OLT line, PON 
port, or active OLT fault, services can be quickly switched from the active OLT to the 
standby OLT. 
•
Automatic switching can be triggered : The fiber on the active OLT is broken. / The 
active OLT is faulty./ The PON port of the active OLT is faulty. / The uplink of the 
active OLT is faulty (this condition triggers automatic switching only in the associated 
protection switching scenario). /The quality of the active line deteriorates.

===== PAGE 15 =====
•
The fiber is disconnected during the working of the PON port on an OLT. 
▫
The working port is in the active state. The protection port is in the standby state. 
▫
Upon detecting an LOS alarm (generated due to the active fiber cut), the 
working port disables the transmission of its optical module. 
▫
Upon detecting an LOS alarm on the working port, the protection port enables 
the transmit function of the optical module, and performs the ONU ranging 
operation. 
▫
When the fiber connected to the protection port is in the normal state and the 
ONU ranging operation is successful, the protection port reports an LOS clear 
alarm.
▫
The working port is switched to the standby state, and the protection port is 
switched to the active state. Till now, the protection switching is completed.

===== PAGE 16 =====
•
All ONUs connected to the OLT PON port go offline. 
▫
The working port is in the active state. The protection port is in the standby state. 
▫
Upon detecting an LOS alarm (generated because all ONUs go offline), the 
protection port disables the transmission of its optical module. 
▫
Upon detecting an LOS alarm on the working port, the protection port enables 
the transmit function of the optical module, and performs the ONU ranging 
operation. 
▫
The OLT keeps checking for ONUs on the working and protection ports until it 
detects an ONU going online. 
▫
When an ONU goes online, if the protection port is detected, the protection port 
takes over; otherwise, the working port continues to work.

===== PAGE 17 =====
•
If a BFD session has been configured on the OLT, the BFD session can be bound to a 
protection group for creating an association between them. If the CFM function has 
been configured on the OLT, the MEP session can be bound to a protection group for 
creating an association between them. In this way, when the upper-layer network of 
the active OLT is faulty, the active and standby OLTs determine whether to perform 
protection switching and notify the ONU after the switching. Services are restored. 
•
An OLT protection group is associated with the upstream Ethernet port status. In this 
way, when the physical-layer link of the active OLT is faulty, the active and standby 
OLTs determine whether to perform protection switching and notify the ONU after the 
switching. Services are restored. 
•
On the OLTs, the dual-homing protection groups are associated with the upstream 
Ethernet port status. When the Ethernet port associated with the protection group on 
the working OLT (OLT 1) becomes down, OLT 1 checks whether the OLT (OLT 2) on 
the protection side and the physical-layer link on the protection side are normal. If 
they are normal and no forced switching or locking operation is performed on OLT 1 
and OLT 2, and OLT1 data is synchronized to OLT 2, OLT 1 and OLT 2 determine 
whether to perform switching. 
•
OLT 2 starts to work. It enables the transmission of the optical module and performs 
ONU ranging. 
•
After the switchover, the ONU service data is sent to OLT 2 through the protection 
port, that is, service data is transmitted over the protection link. The changes on the 
OLT are as follows:
▫
OLT 1 becomes standby.
▫
OLT 2 becomes active.

===== PAGE 23 =====
•
Compared with Type B protection, Type C protection is more reliable. Redundancy 
protection is provided for the OLT (dual-homing scenario), ONU PON port, backbone 
fiber, optical splitter, and branch fiber. When a component is faulty, services can be 
automatically switched to another link. After the original faulty link returns to normal, 
services will be automatically switched back to it. 

===== PAGE 24 =====
•
On a single-homing network, two PON lines between an ONU and the two PON ports 
of an OLT, one working as the active line and the other one as standby, do not 
forward packets at the same time. When the primary line is interrupted due to a fiber 
or component fault, the ONU quickly switches services to the OLT connected to the 
standby line. An automatic switchover can be triggered when certain conditions are 
met. 
▫
LOS occurs in the input direction.
▫
The ONU is offline.
▫
The OLT or ONU hardware is faulty.
•
The following describes the protection switching in three different scenarios. The PON 
port on the OLT is working properly and the ONU is registered with the OLT. The user 
needs to issue the same PON line configuration data to PON port 1 and PON port 2 on 
the OLT to ensure that the services on the ONU can be restored after the switching.

===== PAGE 25 =====
•
Both the ONU and OLT check the link status and determine whether to trigger protection 
switching based on the link status. 
▫
If the OLT detects a link fault, the OLT automatically switches to the protection link and 
sends a message to notify ONU 1 of the switchover and reason. 
▫
If ONU 1 detects a link fault, ONU 1 automatically switches to the protection link and 
sends a message to notify the OLT of the switchover and reason. 
•
After the switchover, ONU 1 service data is sent to the OLT through the protection port, and 
service data is transmitted over the backbone fiber of the OLT. The protection switching on 
ONU 1 does not affect ONU N. The changes on ONU 1 are as follows:
▫
The working port becomes standby.
▫
The protection port becomes active, and service packets are transmitted through link B in 
Figure 1. 
•
After the switchover, services on ONU 1 can be automatically switched back to the working 
port. The OLT sends an automatic switchback message and the switchback time to the ONU. 
▫
If the OLT learns that the working port and working link of ONU 1 are functioning 
properly and the working link stays normal during the wait to restore (WTR) time, the 
OLT automatically switches back to the working link and notifies the ONU of the 
switchover and reason when the WTR time expires. 
▫
If ONU 1 learns that the working port and working link of the OLT are functioning 
properly and the working link stays normal during the WTR time, ONU 1 automatically 
switches back to the working link and notifies the OLT of the switchover and reason 
when the WTR time expires. 

===== PAGE 26 =====
•
Both the ONU and OLT check the link status and determine whether to trigger protection 
switching based on the link status. 
▫
If the OLT detects that all branch fibers on the working link are faulty, the OLT 
automatically switches to the protection link and sends a message to notify all ONUs of 
the switchover and reason. 
▫
If an ONU detects that all branch fibers on the working link are faulty, the ONU 
automatically switches to the protection link and sends a message to notify the OLT of 
the switchover and reason. 
•
After the switchover, the ONU service data is sent to the OLT through the protection port, that 
is, service data is transmitted over the protection link. The changes on the ONU are as follows:
▫
The working port becomes standby.
▫
The protection port becomes active.
•
After the switchover, services on the ONU can be automatically switched back to the working 
port. The OLT sends an automatic switchback message and the switchback time to the ONU. 
▫
If the OLT learns that the working port and working link of the ONU are functioning 
properly and link A stays normal during the WTR time, the OLT automatically switches 
back to the working link and notifies the ONU of the switchover and reason when the 
WTR time expires. 
▫
If the ONU learns that the working port and working link of the OLT are functioning 
properly and link A stays normal during the WTR time, the ONU automatically switches 
back to the working link and notifies the OLT of the switchover and reason when the 
WTR time expires.

Both the ONU and OLT check the link status and determine whether to trigger protection

===== PAGE 28 =====
•
The ports on the same GPON service board are 0/2/0 and 0/2/1. 
•
The link where port 0/2/0 port is located is the working link, and that on port 0/2/1 is 
the protection link. 
•
The ONU ID is 0. The ONU adopts SN authentication (SN: hwhw-10101500) and the

The ID of the line profile bound to the ONU is 10.

===== PAGE 29 =====
•
In a dual-homing network, two PON lines between one ONU and two OLTs, one 
working as the active line and one as standby, do not forward packets at the same 
time. When the active line is interrupted due to a fiber or component fault, the ONU 
quickly switches services to the standby OLT. An automatic switchover can be triggered 
when certain conditions are met. 
▫
LOS occurs in the input direction.
▫
The ONU is offline.
▫
The OLT or ONU hardware is faulty.
▫
The OLT's uplink is faulty (this condition triggers protection switching only in the 
associated protection switching scenario).
•
In the dual-homing networking, the PON switching processes vary in 5 different 
scenarios. Both OLT 1 and OLT 2 are working properly and the ONUs are registered 
with the OLTs. The user needs to issue the same PON line configuration data to OLT 1 
and OLT 2 to ensure that the services on the ONUs can be restored after the switching.

===== PAGE 30 =====
•
A branch fiber connected to an ONU is faulty. 
•
If ONU 1 detects a fault on link A, ONU 1 automatically switches to the protection link 
and sends a message to notify OLT 2 of the switchover and reason. 
•
After the switchover, ONU 1 service data is sent to the OLT through the protection 
port, and service data is transmitted over the backbone fiber of the OLT, which does 
not affect ONU N. The changes on ONU 1 are as follows:
▫
The working port becomes standby.
▫
The protection port becomes active.
•
After the switchover, services on ONU 1 can be automatically switched back to the 
working port. The OLT sends an automatic switchback message and the switchback 
time to the ONU. If ONU 1 learns that the working port, working link, and the uplink 
of OLT 1 are functioning properly and the working link stays normal during the WTR 
time, the ONU automatically switches back to the working link when the WTR time 
expires. 

===== PAGE 31 =====
•
If an ONU detects that all branch fibers on the working link are faulty, the ONU 
automatically switches to the protection link and sends a message to notify OLT 2 of 
the switchover and reason. 
•
After the switchover, the ONU service data is sent to OLT 2 through the protection 
port, that is, service data is transmitted over the protection link. The changes on the 
ONU are as follows:
▫
The working port becomes standby.
▫
The protection port becomes active.
•
After the switchover, services on the ONU can be automatically switched back to the 
working port. The OLT sends an automatic switchback message and the switchback 
time to the ONU. If an ONU learns that the working port, working link, and the uplink 
of OLT 1 are functioning properly and the working link stays normal during the WTR 
time, the ONU automatically switches back to the working link when the WTR time 
expires. 

===== PAGE 32 =====
•
The backbone fiber is faulty. 
▫
If an ONU detects a fault on the backbone fiber, the ONU automatically switches 
to the protection link and sends a message to notify OLT 2 of the switchover and 
reason. 
•
After the switchover, the ONU service data is sent to OLT 2 through the protection 
port, that is, service data is transmitted over the protection link. The changes on the 
ONU are as follows:
▫
The working port becomes standby.
▫
The protection port becomes active.
•
After the switchover, services on the ONU can be automatically switched back to the 
working port. The OLT sends an automatic switchback message and the switchback

of OLT 1 are functioning properly and the working link stays normal during the WTR 
time, the ONU automatically switches back to the working link when the WTR time 
expires. 

===== PAGE 33 =====
•
On an OLT, the protection group is associated with the status of the upstream 
Ethernet port. When the Ethernet port associated with the protection group on the 
OLT goes down, the OLT notifies the ONU of the upstream link status change. 
•
After receiving the switching instruction from the OLT, the ONU switches to the 
protection link. After the switching, the ONU reports messages about the switching to 
OLT 1 and OLT 2 through the working and protection links. 
•
After the switching, the ONU service data is sent to OLT 2 through the protection port, 
that is, service data is transmitted over the protection link. The changes on the ONU 
are as follows:
▫
The working port becomes standby.
▫
The protection port becomes active.
•
After the switching, services on the ONU can be automatically switched back to the 
working port. The OLT sends an automatic switchback message and the switchback 
time to the ONU. If an ONU learns that the working port, working link, and the uplink 
of OLT 1 are functioning properly and the working link stays normal during the WTR 
time, the ONU automatically switches back to the working link when the WTR time 
expires.

===== PAGE 39 =====
•
PON network adopts P2MP structure, single PON connect with multiple users , PON 
network protection mechanism will improve reliability of our network.

===== PAGE 40 =====
•
As broadband services are widely used, carriers require higher bandwidth and reliability 
for Ethernet links. 
•
Hardware upgrades can increase Ethernet link bandwidth but also incur high costs. In 
addition, hardware upgrades are less flexible than software upgrades. To cope with 
these problems, Ethernet link aggregation is launched. It features the following 
advantages: 
▫
The maximum bandwidth of a LAG is equal to the total bandwidths of all links in 
a LAG. Hardware upgrades are not required, which is more cost-effective. 
▫
Traffic in a LAG is distributed to member links by using the load sharing 
algorithm, implementing load sharing and improving link usage. 
▫
Member links in a LAG dynamically back up each other. When one of the links is 
disconnected, another link immediately takes its place. 
▫
Link aggregation functions only between interconnected devices and is 
independent of the network topology. 

===== PAGE 41 =====
•
Impact of manual aggregation on services: Interconnected devices do not fully 
negotiate with each other in aggregation. In this case, when the receive or transmit 
direction of a member link fails (for example, when the fiber connected to a receive or 
transmit Ethernet port is cut), or when the link is incorrectly connected, the service 
transmit end cannot detect the fault. As a result, services are interrupted. 
•
Impact of LACP aggregation on services: If an access device is used to aggregate 
multiple cascaded devices, LACP packets are exchanged frequently and therefore the 
system may get overloaded. 

===== PAGE 42 =====
•
The LACP protocol provides the data switching device with a standard negotiation 
mode to form an aggregation link. After the LACP protocol is enabled, the system 
automatically aggregates multiple links according to its configuration and enables the 
aggregation link to transmit and receive data. 
•
Member ports in a LAG send LACP packets to notify the peer device of the information, 
including the system priority, system MAC address, port priority, port ID, and operation 
key. 
•
After receiving the information, the peer device compares the information with the one 
saved on other ports to select ports that can be aggregated. The interconnected 
devices reach agreement on the ports that can transmit and receive data and 
determine links carrying traffic. 
•
The LACP packets are sent in any of the following modes:
▫
Event-triggered: A change in the state of the local device or in the local 
configuration triggers the generation and transmission of a new LACP packet. 
▫
Periodic: When an aggregation link works stably, the system periodically sends 
the current state to maintain the aggregation link. 
•
When an aggregation link works stably, the system periodically sends the current state 
to maintain the aggregation link. 
•
After aggregation links are generated, LACP is used to maintain link status. In case of 
an aggregation condition change, LACP is used to automatically adjust link 
aggregation. Aggregation condition changes include the following:
▫
Physical port status change
▫
Board fault
▫
Packet forwarding failures on ports

===== PAGE 43 =====
▫
Port status changes of the peer device

===== PAGE 44 =====

===== PAGE 45 =====
•
To ensure packets carried on member links are correctly received on the peer device 
and ensure load balancing over member links in a LAG, hash algorithms are used to 
allocate packets based on: 
▫
MAC addresses
▫
IP addresses 
▫
MPLS labels
▫
Packet quintuple (protocol, source IP address, destination IP address, source port, 
and destination port)
•
In a load non-sharing LAG, only some member links carry traffic and other links are in 
the standby state. Member links work in a hot standby mechanism
•
Switchover Reversion Process
▫
If an LACP LAG works in load sharing mode and the active port before 
switchover recovers, the original faulty link is enabled and traffic is reallocated by 
the load sharing algorithm. 
▫
If an LACP LAG works in load non-sharing mode, you can set the link revertive
mode to revertive or non-revertive. 
▪
When an LACP load non-sharing LAG is set to revertive, services are 
switched back to the link of a higher priority after this link is restored.
▪
When an LACP load non-sharing LAG is set to non-revertive, services are 
not switched back to the selected link after this link is restored, but are still 
transmitted on the current link.

===== PAGE 46 =====

===== PAGE 47 =====

===== PAGE 48 =====

===== PAGE 49 =====

===== PAGE 50 =====
•
Access network users, especially corporations and large residential communities, are 
posing stringent requirements on network reliability. If the upstream or cascading links 
of access devices are not protected, services carried over these links will be interrupted 
if the links break or the devices are malfunctioning. For the access devices that provide 
services to tens of thousands of users, carriers usually demand protection for the 
upstream or cascading ports. Specifically, services can be switched between links 
automatically or manually, depending on your requirements, for example, when the 
upstream or cascading link is broken. Such a protection group ensures that an 
upstream or cascading link is always available, improving reliability.
•
Access devices support Timedelay Ethernet port protection group. A Timedelay
Ethernet port protection group also switches services based on port status, but the 
protection port is disabled when the working port is functioning properly. If the 
working port is faulty, the protection port remains enabled for a predefined period of 
time. Services are switched to the protection port if this port is functioning properly 
during this period. If the protection port is malfunctioning during this period, the 
Timedelay protection group retries the working port while it keeps the protection port 
disabled. The protection group will repeat the preceding process until one port restores 
the normal state, and then switch services to it.

===== PAGE 51 =====
•
Protection Reversion
▫
The reversion enable command is used to enable the reversion function of a 
protection group.
▪
The priority for a working member in a protection group to carry the 
service is higher than the priority for a protection member in the protection 
group to carry the service. When a fault that occurs on the working 
member causes the protection switch, if the time for the fault of the 
working member to recover exceeds the WTR time, the switch reversion is 
performed. The protection switch refers the switch under which the 
working member switches to the protection member, and the switch 
reversion refers to the switch under which the protection member switches 
to the working member.
▪
If the switch of a protection group is initiated by a manual switch or a 
forced switch, the switch of the protection group cannot be reversed.
▪
If the cancel manual switch and cancel forced switch operations are 
performed and the working member is normal, the switch reversion 
operation is performed after the WTR time expires.

===== PAGE 52 =====

===== PAGE 53 =====

===== PAGE 54 =====

===== PAGE 55 =====
•
Principle of STP
▫
STP determines the topology of a network by transmitting a certain special 
message (configuration message as defined in IEEE 802.1D) between bridges. A 
configuration message contains sufficient information to enable the bridge to 
complete the calculation of the spanning tree. The following defines the 
designated port and the designated bridge:
▪
For a bridge (such as bridge A), the designated bridge is a bridge that is 
directly connected to bridge A and forwards data packets to bridge A. The 
designated port is the port in the designated bridge through which the data 
packets are forwarded to bridge A.
▪
For a LAN, the designated bridge is a bridge that forwards data packets to 
the LAN. The designated port is the port in the designated bridge through 
which the data packets are forwarded to the LAN.
•
Defects of STP
•
In case of topology change or link failure, a port has to wait double Forward 
Delay time before it can turn from the blocking state to the forwarding state. 
Therefore, in case of topology change, double Forward Delay time (at least 
scores of seconds) is required to restore the network connectivity.
•
The entire bridged LAN uses a single spanning tree instance. Therefore, when the 
network is large, a longer convergence time may be required or the topology 
changes frequently.
•
Principle of RSTP
▫
RSTP is an improvement on STP. The rapidness of RSTP relies on the greatly 
shortened delay for the designated port and the root port to turn into the 
forwarding state in a certain condition. 

===== PAGE 56 =====
This helps to shorten the time for stabilizing the network topology.
•
In comparison with STP, RSTP improves in the following aspects:
•
First improvement:
▫
The alternate port and backup port are set for rapid switching of the root port 
and designated port.
▫
When the root port fails, the alternate port quickly switches to the new root port 
and turns into the forwarding state without delay.
▫
When the designated port fails, the backup port quickly switches to the new 
designated port and turns into the forwarding state without delay.
•
Second improvement:
▫
In a point-to-point link connected with two switching ports, a designated port 
turns into the forwarding state without delay after one handshake with the 
downstream bridge.
▫
In a shared link connected with at least three bridges, the downstream bridge 
does not respond to the handshake request sent from the upstream designated 
port, and the designated port has to wait double Forward Delay time before it 
turns into the forwarding state.
•
Third improvement:
▫
A port that is directly connected to a terminal and is not connected to any other 
bridge is defined as an edge port. The edge port can directly turn into the 
forwarding state without delay.
▫
Because a bridge does not know whether a port is directly connected to a 
terminal, the edge port must be configured manually.

===== PAGE 57 =====
•
MSTP : Multiple Spanning Tree Protocol (MSTP)
•
Defects of single spanning tree: some VLAN data forwarding is blocked, load balance 
can’t be achieved, sometimes choose the sub-optimal path.
•
MSTP sets VLAN mapping tables (relation tables between VLANs and spanning trees) 
to associate VLANs and spanning trees.
•
MSTP divides a switching network into multiple regions. Each region contains multiple 
spanning trees, and each spanning tree is independent of one another. Each MST 
Instance has a MSTID, MSTID is a two bytes integer.
•
VRP platform support 16 MST Instance, MSTID ranges from 0 to15, by default all 
VLANs will be mapping into MST Instance 0 .
•
In  this situation, we will configure 2 MST Instances, VLAN 10 , 20 will be mapping into 
MST Instance 1, VLAN 30 ,40 will be mapping into MST Instance 2 .

===== PAGE 58 =====
•
MSTP divides the entire Layer 2 network into multiple spanning tree (MST) regions. 
These regions and the other bridges and LANs are connected into a single common 
spanning tree (CST). Multiple spanning trees are created in a region through 
calculation. Each spanning tree is defined as a multiple spanning tree instance (MSTI). 
MSTI 0 is defined as an internal spanning tree (IST). MSTP connects all bridges and 
LANs with a single common and internal spanning tree (CIST) which consists of the 
CST and the IST. Like RSTP, MSTP calculates the spanning tree according to the 
configuration message. The configuration message, however, contains the message of 
MSTP on the bridge.
•
Calculation of CIST
▫
Select a bridge with the highest priority within the entire network as the CIST 
root by comparing the configuration messages.
▫
In each MST region, MSTP creates an IST through calculation. Meanwhile, MSTP 
regards each MST region as a single bridge, and then creates a CST between 
regions.
▫
The CST and the IST forms the CIST that connects all the bridges in a bridge 
network.

===== PAGE 59 =====

===== PAGE 60 =====
•
Link type: RPL- Ring Protection Link ; common Link;
•
Protocol message:
▫
R-APS signal fail (SF)
▪
indicates a link failure. When detecting a link failure, a node sends an R-
APS (SF) message to notify other nodes on the ring. When receiving an R-
APS (SF), the RPL owner node unblocks the RPL owner port so that the RPL 
owner port forwards data. Other nodes determine whether to flush the FDT 
according to the DNF flag in the R-APS (SF) message.
▫
R-APS no request (NR)
▪
indicates a link recovery. When detecting a link recovery, a node sends an 
R-APS (NR) message to notify other nodes on the ring. When receiving an 
R-APS (NR), the RPL owner node starts the WTR timer. Other nodes 
determine whether to flush the FDT according to the DNF flag in the R-APS 
(NR) message.
▫
R-APS no request, RPL blocked (NR, RB)
▪
An R-APS (NR, RB) message is a type of R-APS (NR). It is sent by an RPL 
owner to signal that the network is normal and the RPL has been blocked. 
When receiving an R-APS (NR, RB), other nodes on the ring will unblock 
the common ports, and determine whether to flush the FDT according to 
the DNF flag in the message.
▫
NR   ——No Request, The network is in normal conditions
▫
RB    ——An RPL is blocked.
▫
DNF ——Do Not Flush, A flag in the R-APS message fields and indicates whether 
to flush the FDB.

===== PAGE 61 =====
•
Port state
▫
Forwarding : a port can forward data, and receive and send R-APS messages.
▫
Discarding: a port cannot forward data, but can receive and send R-APS, EFM, 
CFM, LLDP, and 802.1x messages.
•
Port roles : The ITU-T G.8032 protocol defines two port roles: ring protection link (RPL) 
owner port and common port. The link where an RPL owner port resides is called a 
ring protection link. The device where an RPL owner port resides is called an RPL 
owner node.
•
RPL owner port
▫
An ERPS ring has only one RPL owner port, which can be configured by users. Loop 
avoidance is achieved on an ERPS ring by blocking the RPL owner port. A blocked RPL 
owner port cannot forward data but can send ring automatic protection switching (R-
APS) messages.
▫
As defined in the IEEE 802.1ag protocol, the down maintenance end point (MEP) on the 
RPL owner port can receive and send Ethernet OAM messages.
▫
When an RPL owner node receives a fault message and learns that a node or link on the 
ERPS ring is faulty, the RPL owner node automatically enables the RPL owner port. Then, 
the RPL owner port resumes traffic reception and transmission, ensuring uninterrupted 
traffic.
•
Common port
▫
On an ERPS ring, all ports except the RPL owner port are common ports. Common ports 
are responsible for monitoring the status of their directly connected links, and notifying 
any link status changes to the ports on other nodes.
•
Timers
▫
Guard timer

===== PAGE 62 =====
▫
After a node on a Layer 2 network running ERPS recovers from a link fault or node fault, 
the node sends an R-APS (NR) message to other nodes on the ring and starts the guard 
timer. The node does not receive R-APS messages before the timer expires, which 
prevents the node from unblocking a recovered port after receiving expired R-APS(SF) 
messages. The unblocking will cause a loop on the network. If the node receives R-APS 
(SF) messages after the timer expires, the node changes its port state to forwarding. 
▫
Holdoff timer
▪
Layer 2 networks running ERPS require different protection switching sequences. 
For example, when a node becomes faulty in multiple-layer service application, 
carriers prefer a duration to rectify the fault and the duration is not perceived by 
subscribers. To meet the carrier requirement, protection switching is not performed 
immediately after the fault occurs. The fault is reported if it is not rectified after 
the holdoff timer expires.
▫
WTR timer
▪
On a Layer 2 network running ERPS, an RPL owner port enters the forwarding 
state when a node or link on the ERPS ring fails. When the node or link recovers, 
some ports on the node or link may still stay in the Down state (that is, not Up 
yet). If the RPL owner port is immediately blocked upon the fault recovery, and 
finds that there are still ports in the Down state, the RPL owner port will be 
unblocked again. To avoid such toggling protection states in the case of 
intermittent faults, the RPL owner port starts a wait-to-restore (WTR) timer when 
receiving the R-APS (NR) message from the other ports.
▪
If the RPL owner port receives the R-APS (SF) message from the other ports before 
the WTR timer times out, the RPL owner port stops the WTR timer and stays in the 
forwarding state.
▪
If the RPL owner port does not receive the R-APS (SF) message when the WTR 
timer times out, the RPL owner port is blocked and sends the R-APS (NR, RB) 
message to other ports. When receiving the R-APS (NR, RB) message, the other 
ports switch to the forwarding state.

===== PAGE 64 =====

▫

▫

===== PAGE 72 =====
•
NGN is the concept of defining and deploying networks. Because an NGN network is 
divided into different layers and planes with open interfaces, NGN provides a platform 
for service providers and carriers to evolve, create, provide, and manage new services.
•
Advantages of the NGN network
▫
NGN adopts the layered architecture.
▫
NGN provides the media access layer, core switching layer, network control layer, 
and service management layer. 
▫
NGN separates control and connection, as well as services and calls.
▫
NGN bearer networks tend to use the unified IP protocol to implement service 
convergence.
▫
NGN is a network based on unified protocols.
▫
NGN supports multiple services, such as voice, data, and video services.
▫
NGN has advantages in access and coverage.
▫
NGN features low construction and maintenance costs.
•
Compared with the PSTN, the NGN has the following improvements: 
▫
Hierarchical architecture and component-based network model: The network 
construction is more flexible and convenient. It is easy to plan and implement the 
rolling construction according to the service development. The networking is 
more flat and the maintenance cost is reduced. 
▫
IP bearer and multi-service unified platform: The voice, data, and video services 
are carried on a unified platform, which facilitates the convergence of various 
services and the interworking between the fixed network and the mobile 3G 
network. Open interfaces and support for third-party service development.

===== PAGE 73 =====
•
Background of NGN
▫
The NGN system divides the switching equipment into components for call 
control and media processing using a standard protocol. The call control is pure 
software running on a universal hardware platform, and media processing 
converts TDM into IP-based media streams.
▫
The Softswitch technology emerges as the core technology of NGN.
•
Functions of each layer in an NGN system
▫
Edge access layer: Use various access methods to connect various to the network 
and convert the information format into a format that can be transmitted on the 
network.
▫
Core switching layer: Uses the packet technology to provide a highly reliable 
integrated transport platform with high QoS and large capacity.
▫
Network control layer: Implements call control. The core technology of the 
network control layer is Softswitch, which implements basic real-time call and 
connection control functions.
▫
Service management layer: Provides additional value-added services and 
operation support after call setup.

===== PAGE 74 =====
•
According to the protocol functions, NGN protocols are classified into the following 
types:
▫
Bearer control protocol
▪
Function: It is used by the media gateway controller (MGC) to control the 
media gateway (MG), such as the access media gateway (AMG) and trunk 
media gateway (TMG).
▪
Example: MGCP, H248
▪
MGCP/H.248 is a media gateway control protocol based on the gateway 
separation structure. It works in master/slave mode.
▫
Call control protocol
▪
Function: Controls the establishment, connection, and termination of a call.
▪
Example: SIP and H.323
▪
Session initial protocol (SIP) is one of the framework protocols of a 
multimedia communication system. It is an application layer protocol used 
to establish, change, or end multimedia sessions.
▪
H.323 is a protocol that provides multimedia information transmission for 
terminals or other entities based on the packet switched network.
▫
Signaling transmission protocol
▪
Function: Provides signaling transmission services for the Softswitch.
▪
Example: SIGTRAN
▪The Signaling Transport (SIGTRAN) protocol stack bears the 
communication between the signaling gateway and the MGC. It 
provides two functions: adaptation and transmission.

===== PAGE 75 =====
•
The SoftX3000 communicates with the trunk gateway through the H.248 protocol. The 
SoftX3000 provides the MGC function to control the integrated services digital network 
user part (ISUP) trunk in the trunk gateway.

===== PAGE 76 =====
•
The difference between the IMS and the Softswitch in the network architecture. The 
Softswitch network system is closely related to the specific access mode based on the 
master-slave control feature. The IMS system supports various access modes because 
the terminal and core side use the IP-based SIP protocol and the IP technology is 
independent of the bearer media, therefore, the application scope of the IMS gradually 
extends from the initial mobile network to the fixed network. In addition, the IMS 
architecture supports mobility management and provides a QoS guarantee mechanism. 
Therefore, compared with the Softswitch technology, the IMS technology has 
advantages in roaming management and QoS guarantee for broadband users.
•
In terms of the network architecture, the fixed NGN and IMS adopt the layered 
network architecture, the network is divided into the service layer, control layer, bearer 
layer, and access layer. The main difference is that the fixed NGN focuses on physical 
implementation and centralizes the functions of the control layer on the soft switch. In 
the fixed NGN, there is only one central control point, that is, the Softswitch. The 
control layer functions of the IMS are implemented by multiple logical functional 
modules. For example, the session control function is implemented by the CSCF entity, 
the interworking function is implemented by the MGCF/MGW, and the subscriber data 
is stored in the HSS. The network is more distributed, and the logical entity may not be 
physically implemented. Modularization of network functions facilitates network 
expansion and service integration, facilitating centralized networking.
•
The IMS provides the IP multimedia service control capability that is based on the IP 
bearer network, uses SIP as the core control protocol, and is irrelevant to access. The 
IMS was first proposed by the 3GPP to provide multimedia services on the mobile 
communications network. It has been widely recognized in the industry and gradually 
expanded from mobile broadband to a unified core control network applicable to fixed 
broadband access.
•
The IMS supports multiple fixed and mobile access modes, achieving the goal of the 
convergent core network for the first time. 

===== PAGE 77 =====
•
As one of the main contents of R5, 3GPP IMS subsystem is the ultimate goal of 3G 
mobile network to realize packet voice and packet data, and to provide unified 
multimedia service and application. 
•
The 3GPP IMS uses the PS domain as the bearer channel for upper-layer control 
signaling and media transmission. The service/control network is separated from the 
bearer network to achieve independent development.
•
The IMS network uses SIP signaling as the call control signaling, supports SIP mobile 
terminals, and implements interworking with H.323, BICC, and ISUP terminals through 
the MGCF and IM-MGW.
•
The IMS provides SIP users with the full-process roaming capability and VHE virtual 
home service environment capability on the entire network, and supports the open 
OSA/VHE structure. 
•
Specify that IPv6 is mandatory and consider the interworking with IPv4. Make full use 
of the enhanced features of IPv6 in terms of QoS, security, mobility, and so on.

===== PAGE 78 =====

===== PAGE 79 =====
•
Transport layer support: SIP protocol hosted on the IP network, network layer protocol 
for IP, transport layer protocol available TCP or UDP, the preferred UDP is 
recommended.

===== PAGE 80 =====
•
Multi-Party Multimedia Working Group put forward two proposals for multimedia 
communication in the Internet in 1996:
▫
Session Initiation Protocol (SIP)
▫
Simple Conference Invitation Protocol (SCIP)
•
At last, the two proposals are unified in Session Initiation Protocol (SIP).
•
SIP is a protocol that is being developed and continuously researched. On the one 
hand, it adopts the design ideologies of other Internet standards and protocols. In style, 
it complies with the principles of simplicity, openness, compatibility, and scalability of 
the Internet, and takes full attention to the security problems in the open and complex 
network environment of the Internet. On the other hand, it also fully considers the 
support of various services on traditional public telephone networks, including IN 
service and ISDN service.
•
In the SIP protocol, a session is created by using a SIP invite message with a session 
description, so that the participant can perform media type negotiation through SIP 
interaction. It requests the current location of a user through proxy and redirection to 
support the mobility of the user. Users can also register their current locations. The SIP 
protocol is independent of other conference control protocols. It is designed to be 
independent of the underlying transport layer protocols. Therefore, other additional 
functions can be flexibly extended.
•
The SIP protocol can be used to initiate a session or invite a member to join a session 
that has been established in another manner.
•
Text-based encoding and decoding.
•
The transaction mechanism is used. Each request triggers the operation method of the 
server. The request and response constitute a transaction. The transactions are 
independent of each other. Independent of the bottom-layer transmission protocol.

===== PAGE 81 =====
SIP and Associated Protocols
•
SDP (Session Description Protocol)
▫
Application layer protocol. 
▫
It’s used to negotiate the multimedia parameters between two ends.
•
RTP (Real Time Transmission Protocol)
▫
It is defined by RFC 3550  for real time data transmission. 
▫
It provide end to end service guarantee through a series of methods, such as 
payload data identify, sequence  number encoding, time stamp , transmission 
monitor and so on . 
•
Sigcomp (Signaling Compression)
▫
It is used to compress message.

===== PAGE 82 =====
•
Redirect server: The redirect server does not accept or reject calls. Instead, it provides 
the routing function and works with the registration process to support the mobility of 
SIP terminals.
•
Proxy server provides the following functions: routing, authentication and 
authorization, charging monitoring, call control, and service provisioning. There are 
three forms: Stateless, Stateful, and Call Stateful.
•
Location Service/Register
▫
A service used by the SIP relocation server or proxy server to obtain the location 
of the called party. 
▫
User agent User agent client (UAC): The party that initiates a request is called a 
UAC. For example, SIP Phone is a UAC.
▫
User agent server (UAS): The party that receives a request is called the UAS. For 
example, the SoftX3000 is a UAS.
•
The SIP proxy server (logical network entity) forwards the request or response on 
behalf of the client. A proxy server can also act as a server. The proxy server provides 
the following functions: routing, identity authentication, charging control, call control, 
and service provisioning. The proxy server attempts to forward requests to multiple 
addresses in multiple modes, such as branching, looping, and recursive queries.
•
The SIP register server receives the registration request and stores the address 
mapping contained in the registration request in the database for subsequent call 
processing and subscriber home address locating.
•
The location server provides the location function. The location server obtains the 
possible called party addresses of the redirection server and the proxy server, and 
provides a mapping list between the recorded address and the contact address.
•
Multiple logical entities can be in one physical entity. In the Huawei solution, the 

===== PAGE 83 =====
SoftX3000 also functions as the redirection server, proxy server, and registrar.

===== PAGE 84 =====
•
Call: For example, all participants of a conference invited by the same source form a 
call. A P2P IP phone session is the simplest session that maps to a single SIP call.
•
Transaction: A normal call usually contains three transactions. The call initiation 
includes two operation requests: an Invite (Invite) request and an ACK (ACK) request. 
The Invite (Invite) request requires a response, and the ACK (ACK) request only 
acknowledges that a final response has been received, and does not require a response. 
Call termination includes an operation request: Bye.
•
SIP indicates that the SIP protocol is used to communicate with the specified end 
system.
▫
The user name can consist of any characters. Generally, the user name can be 
the same as the email user name or the phone number. Currently, the SoftX3000 
uses the phone number as the user name. 
▫
Host can be set to a host domain name or an IPv4 address. 
▫
Port number indicates the number of the port to which the request message is 
sent. The default value is 5060, that is, the public SIP port number. 
▫
The password can be placed in the SIP URL, but it is not recommended because 
the security is poor. 
▫
Transfer parameter indicates whether to use TCP or UDP for transmission. The 
default value is UDP. 
▫
A specific function of the SIP URL is to allow the host type to be an IP phone 
gateway. In this case, the user name can be a common telephone number. The 
BNF syntax cannot distinguish between a phone number and a common user 
name. Therefore, the User Parameter field is added to the end of the domain 
name. The value of this field can be IP or Phone. If the value is Phone, the user 
name is a phone number, and the corresponding terminal system is the IP phone 
gateway.

===== PAGE 85 =====
▫
The Method Parameter indicates the method (operation) used. The lifetime 
parameter indicates the lifetime of a UDP multicast packet. It can be used only 
when the transport parameter is UDP and the server address parameter is a 
multicast address. The Server Address Parameter indicates the address of the 
server communicating with the user, and it overrides the address in the Host field, 
usually a multicast address. The transfer parameter, lifetime parameter, server 
address parameter, and method parameter are URL parameters and can be used 
only in the redirection address, that is, the Contact field

===== PAGE 86 =====

===== PAGE 87 =====
•
Request message: a SIP message sent by the client to the server for activation based 
on a specific operation. 
•
Response message: used to respond to a request message and indicate the call success 
or failure status. 
•
Both request and response messages contain SIP header fields and SIP message fields. 
•
Used by the client to send SIP messages to the server for activation, including INVITE, 
ACK, OPTIONS, BYE, CANCEL, and REGISTER messages.

===== PAGE 88 =====
•
Format of the SIP request command, which consists of the start line, message header, 
and message body. Each parameter line in the message header is distinguished by a 
newline character. Some parameters are optional for different requests.
•
Call-ID：this field is used to uniquely identify a specific invitation or all registrations of 
a user.
▫
A multimedia conference may have multiple calls. Each call has its own Call-ID. 
For example, a user can invite someone to a long-duration conference several 
times. A subscriber may also receive multiple invitations to the same conference 
or call, and the Call-IDs of the invitations are different. The user may determine 
the repetition of these invitations by using identifiers in the session description, 
for example, the session identifier and the version number of the o (source) field 
in the SDP. The format of the Call-ID is Call-ID: local ID@host. The host should 
define a domain name and a global optional IP address globally. In this case, the 
local identifier is composed of URI characters that are unique in the range of 
"host". Otherwise, the local ID must be globally unique to ensure the global 
uniqueness of the Call-ID. Call-ID is case sensitive.
•
To：this field indicates the recipient of the request. The format of this field is the same 
as that of the From field, except that the first keyword is To. All requests and 
responses must contain this field.
•
Cseq：Cseq is called command sequence number. This field must be added to each 
request. It consists of a command name and a decimal sequence number. The 
sequence number is selected by the requester and is unique within the Call-ID range.
•
Via：the Via field indicates the path that the request passes through. It prevents 
routing loops during transmission of request messages and ensures that the response 
and request messages are transmitted over the same path to pass through the firewall 
or meet other routing requirements.

===== PAGE 89 =====
•
Contact：this field is used for INVITE, ACK, and REGISTER requests, their successful 
responses, call progress responses, and redirection responses. The function of the field 
is to provide the address for communicate with users.
•
Max-Forwards：this field specifies the maximum number of transit stations that a 
request can pass through before reaching the destination address. The value of this 
parameter decreases by 1 each time a request passes through a transit station. If the 
value is 0 and the request has not reached the destination address, the server returns a 
483 response and terminates the request.

===== PAGE 90 =====
•
Allow: this field provides the list of all request messages supported by the agent server.
•
Content-Length: size of a message body. The value is a decimal number. The 
application uses this field to indicate the size of the message body to be sent, 
regardless of the media type of the entity. This header field must be used if a flow-
based protocol, such as TCP, is used as the transport protocol.
•
Content-Type: this field indicates the media type of the message body. If the message 
body is not empty, the Content-Type header field must exist. If the message body is 
empty and the Content-Type header field exists, the length of the message body of 
this type is 0 (for example, an empty audio file).
•
Supported: the transmission of the 100 provisional responses defined in the SIP 
protocol is unreliable. That is, the UAC cannot receive the provisional responses sent by 
the UAS.
•
The header field of the User-Agent field includes the information about the user 
terminal initiating a request.
•
The Expires header field specifies how long the message (or message content) will 
time out.
•
The Accept-Language header field is used in a request message to indicate the 
preferred language of the status response content carried in the reason phrase, session 
description, or response message. If the message does not contain the Accept-
Language header field, the server considers that the client supports all languages.
•
The Authorization field contains the authentication certificate of a terminal.

===== PAGE 91 =====
•

Event 1: The SIP phone sends a registration request to the SoftX3000, notifying the 
SoftX3000 that the SIP phone is powered on or restarted.

SoftX3000 requests to authenticate the subscriber. The WWW-Authenticate field 
carries the Digest authentication mode supported by the SoftX3000 and the domain 
name huawei.com of the SoftX3000. The nonce for the authentication is generated, in 
addition, the AAA server sends the parameters to the terminal through the response 
message to initiate the authentication process.

Event 3: The SIP phone sends a registration request to the SoftX3000 again. The 
registration request carries the Authorization field, including the authentication mode 
DIGEST, SIP phone user ID (phone number), SoftX3000 domain name, NONCE, URI, 
and RESPONS, after receiving the 401 Unauthorized response, the server uses a specific 
algorithm to generate the encrypted RESPONSE) field based on the information 
returned by the server and user configuration.
•
Event 4: After receiving a registration request from a SIP phone, the SoftX3000 checks 
whether the NONCE is correct. If the NONCE is the same as that in the 401 
Unauthorized response, the check is passed. Otherwise, a failure message is returned. 
Then, the SoftX3000 uses the same algorithm as the terminal to generate a response 
based on the NONCE, user name, password (the server can obtain the password of the 
user based on the local user information), and URI, and compares the response with 
the response in the request. If they are the same, the user authentication is successful; 
otherwise, the user authentication fails. The SoftX3000 returns a 200 message, 
indicating that the authentication is successful.

===== PAGE 97 =====
•
Step 1: SIP_A picks up the phone and sends an INVITE message to the SoftX3000, 
requesting the SoftX3000 to invite SIP_B to the session. In addition, the SoftX3000 
sends the IP address 191.169.150.101, port number 8766, payload type, and payload 
type code to the SoftX3000.
•
Step 2: The SoftX3000 returns a 100 Trying response to SIP_A, indicating that the 
request message has been received and is being processed.
•
Step 3: The SoftX3000 returns a 407 Proxy Authentication Required response to SIP_A, 
indicating that the SoftX3000 requests to authenticate the subscriber. The Proxy-
Authenticate field in the response carries the Digest authentication mode supported by 
the SoftX3000 and the domain name huawei.com of the SoftX3000 to generate the 
nonce for the authentication, in addition, the AAA server sends the parameters to the 
terminal through the response message to initiate the authentication process.
•
Step 4: SIP_A sends an ACK message to the SoftX3000, indicating that it has received 
the final response to the INVITE message from the SoftX3000.
•
Step 5: SIP_A sends an INVITE message to the SoftX3000. The Proxy-Authorization field 
is carried, the authentication mode includes the authentication mode DIGEST, user ID 
(phone number in this case) of SIP_A, domain name of the SoftX3000, NONCE, URI, 
and RESPONSE. After receiving the 407 response, SIP_A generates an encrypted 
RESPONSE based on the information returned by the server and user configuration 
using a specific algorithm.
•
Step 6: The SoftX3000 returns a 100 Trying response to SIP_A, indicating that the 
request message has been received and is being processed.
•
Step 7: The SoftX3000 sends an INVITE message to SIP_B, requesting SIP_B to join the 
session. The INVITE message carries the session description of SIP_A to SIP_B.
•
Step 8: The SoftX3000 returns a 100 Trying response to SIP_A, indicating that the 
request message has been received and is being processed.

===== PAGE 98 =====
•
Step 9: SIP_B rings and returns a 180 Ringing message to the SoftX3000.
•
Step 10: The SoftX3000 sends a 180 Ringing message to SIP_A. SIP_A hears the ring 
back tone.
•
Step 11: SIP_B picks up the phone and returns a 200 OK response to the SoftX3000, 
indicating that the INVITE request sent by SIP_B has been accepted and processed. In 
addition, the MSC server sends the IP address 191.169.150.100, port number 8788, 
payload type, and payload type code to the SoftX3000.
•
Step 12: The SoftX3000 sends a 200 message to SIP_A, indicating that the INVITE 
message has been accepted. Then, the SoftX3000 processes the INVITE message and 
sends the session description of SIP_B to SIP_A.
•
Step 13: SIP_A sends an ACK message to the SoftX3000, indicating that it has received 
the final response to the INVITE message from the SoftX3000.
•
Step 14: The SoftX3000 sends an ACK message to SIP_B to acknowledge the receipt of 
the final response to the INVITE message from SIP_B.
•
Step 15: SIP_A hangs up and sends a BYE message to the SoftX3000, requesting to end 
the session.
•
Step 16: The SoftX3000 sends a 200 message to SIP_A, indicating that the session ends.
•
Step 17: The SoftX3000 sends a BYE message to SIP_B, indicating that the session is 
ended successfully.
•
Step 18: SIP_B hangs up and returns a 200 OK response to the SoftX3000, indicating 
that the session is ended successfully.

===== PAGE 99 =====

===== PAGE 100 =====

===== PAGE 101 =====
•
GPON ONT voice service: The voice processing and conversion chip inside the ONT can 
convert the analog voice data at the user end into digital signals and packetize the 
NGN protocol. The ONT supports the SIP and MGCP protocols. However, a device can 
use only one protocol at a time. If you need to change the protocol, you need to 
change the software version of the terminal. The ONT can also be connected to the SIP 
phone of the user through the Ethernet port. In this case, the SIP phone uses the SIP 
protocol to complete the packaging of user data, and transparently transmits the data 
to the upper-layer softswitch for processing through the ONT and OLT.
•
GPON ONU voice service: The ONU connects to a common phone through its POTS 
port, converts analog signals into digital signals through internal digital-to-analog 
conversion, and packs protocols. The ONU supports the H.248 and SIP protocols. The 
ONU can also be connected to the SIP phone of the user through the Ethernet port. In 
this case, the SIP phone uses the SIP protocol to complete the packaging of user data, 
and transparently transmits the data to the upper-layer softswitch for processing 
through the ONT and OLT.
•
GPON MDU voice service: The home gateway packs the POTS service of users using 
the SIP/MGCP protocol provided by itself, integrates the Internet access or IPTV service, 
and uploads the service to the MA5616. The MA5616+MA5680T transparently 
transmits all services from the home gateway. The telephone service is encapsulated 
into packets through the protocol of the home gateway and transparently transmitted 
by the GPON system. Then, the packets are sent to the softswitch for processing to 
implement the VoIP function. The MA5616 can also be directly connected to the POTS 
phone and transparently transmitted to the softswitch through the MA5680T to 
implement the VoIP service.

===== PAGE 125 =====
•
F/S/P: Indicates the subrack ID, slot ID, and port ID of a port.
•
PTPSrvState: Indicates the running status of the physical layer of a port. The possible 
query results are as follows: (1) Normal: normal; (2) PowerDeny: low power; and (3) 
Fault: A fault occurs.
•
PTPAdmState: Indicates the physical layer management status of a port. The possible 
query results are as follows: (1) NoLoop, NoTest: No loopback, not tested; (2) Noloop, 
Test: No loopback, test in progress; (3) LLoop, NoTest: Local loopback, not tested; (4) 
LLoop, Test: Local loopback, test in progress; (5) RLoop, NoTest: remote loopback, not 
tested; and (6) RLoop, Test: remote loopback, test in progress.
•
CTPSrvState: Indicates the service running status of a port. The possible query results 
are as follows: (1) Idle, (2) Offhook; (3) Locked; (4) Ringing; (5) Fault; and (6) –
(indicating that no subscriber is configured).
•
CTPAdmState: Indicates the service management status of a port. The possible query 
results are as follows: (1) LBlock: local blocking; (2) RBlock: remote blocking; (3) 
StartSvc: starting Services; and (4) -: no subscriber is configured.
•
LineState: Indicates the subscriber line status.
