===== PAGE 128 =====

===== PAGE 129 =====

===== PAGE 130 =====

===== PAGE 131 =====
•
The NGN is the product of the convergence of the TDM-based PSTN voice network 
and the IP/ATM-based packet network. The NGN can separate the service functions 
from the lower-layer transmission technologies by using multiple bandwidths and 
transmission technologies with the QoS capability. It allows users to freely access 
networks of different service providers and supports general mobility to ensure that 
users use the same services. It makes it possible to provide integrated services such as 
voice, video, and data over the next-generation network.

===== PAGE 132 =====

===== PAGE 133 =====

===== PAGE 134 =====
•
NGN is the concept of defining and deploying networks. Because an NGN network is 
divided into different layers and planes with open interfaces, NGN provides a platform 
for service providers and carriers to evolve, create, provide, and manage new services.
•
Advantages of the NGN network
▫
NGN adopts the layered architecture.
▫
NGN provides the media access layer, core switching layer, network control layer, 
and service management layer. 
▫
NGN separates control and connection, as well as services and calls.
▫
NGN bearer networks tend to use the unified IP protocol to implement service 
convergence.
▫
NGN is a network based on unified protocols.
▫
NGN supports multiple services, such as voice, data, and video services.
▫
NGN has advantages in access and coverage.
▫
NGN features low construction and maintenance costs.
•
Compared with the PSTN, the NGN has the following improvements: 
▫
Hierarchical architecture and component-based network model: The network 
construction is more flexible and convenient. It is easy to plan and implement the 
rolling construction according to the service development. The networking is 
more flat and the maintenance cost is reduced. 
▫
IP bearer and multi-service unified platform: The voice, data, and video services 
are carried on a unified platform, which facilitates the convergence of various 
services and the interworking between the fixed network and the mobile 3G 
network. Open interfaces and support for third-party service development.

===== PAGE 135 =====
•
Background of NGN
▫
The NGN system divides the switching equipment into components for call 
control and media processing using a standard protocol. The call control is pure 
software running on a universal hardware platform, and media processing 
converts TDM into IP-based media streams.
▫
The Softswitch technology emerges as the core technology of NGN.
•
Functions of each layer in an NGN system
▫
Edge access layer: Use various access methods to connect various to the network 
and convert the information format into a format that can be transmitted on the 
network.
▫
Core switching layer: Uses the packet technology to provide a highly reliable 
integrated transport platform with high QoS and large capacity.
▫
Network control layer: Implements call control. The core technology of the 
network control layer is Softswitch, which implements basic real-time call and 
connection control functions.
▫
Service management layer: Provides additional value-added services and 
operation support after call setup.

===== PAGE 136 =====
•
According to the protocol functions, NGN protocols are classified into the following 
types:
▫
Bearer control protocol
▪
Function: It is used by the media gateway controller (MGC) to control the 
media gateway (MG), such as the access media gateway (AMG) and trunk 
media gateway (TMG).
▪
Example: MGCP, H248
▪
MGCP/H.248 is a media gateway control protocol based on the gateway 
separation structure. It works in master/slave mode.
▫
Call control protocol
▪
Function: Controls the establishment, connection, and termination of a call.
▪
Example: SIP and H.323
▪
Session initial protocol (SIP) is one of the framework protocols of a 
multimedia communication system. It is an application layer protocol used 
to establish, change, or end multimedia sessions.
▪
H.323 is a protocol that provides multimedia information transmission for 
terminals or other entities based on the packet switched network.
▫
Signaling transmission protocol
▪
Function: Provides signaling transmission services for the Softswitch.
▪
Example: SIGTRAN
▪The Signaling Transport (SIGTRAN) protocol stack bears the 
communication between the signaling gateway and the MGC. It 
provides two functions: adaptation and transmission.

===== PAGE 137 =====
•
The SoftX3000 communicates with the trunk gateway through the H.248 protocol. The 
SoftX3000 provides the MGC function to control the integrated services digital network 
user part (ISUP) trunk in the trunk gateway.

===== PAGE 138 =====

===== PAGE 139 =====
•
H.248 messages can be transmitted based on UDP/IP. In addition, H.248 messages can 
be transmitted based on other transmission protocols, such as TCP and SCTP carried 
on the IP network. 
•
The H.248 protocol assumes that the lower-layer transport network is unreliable. 
Therefore, the transaction status and reliability are implemented by the H.248 protocol.
•
Media Gateway (MG): An MG converts media provided in one type of network to the 
format required in another type of network.
•
Media Gateway Controller (MGC): It controls the call state pertaining to connection 
control of media channels of MG.

===== PAGE 140 =====
•
The H.248 protocol, also called the MeGaCo protocol, is a media gateway control 
protocol between the media gateway controller (MGC) and the media gateway (MG). 
The H.248 protocol is a new standard developed by the ITU-T and the IETF. At present, 
standardization organizations such as ITU-T, IETF, and Softswitch Forum are stepping 
up efforts to improve the H.248 protocol. Major telecom equipment manufacturers 
also increase their investment in the R&D and application of the H.248 protocol. 
Compared with the MGCP protocol, the H.248 protocol supports more types of access 
technologies and supports the mobility of terminals. In addition, the H.248 protocol 
supports larger-scale network applications and is more convenient to extend the 
protocol. Therefore, the H.248 protocol is more flexible and has gradually replaced the 
MGCP protocol as the standard of the media gateway control protocol. ,

===== PAGE 141 =====
•
A termination is a logical entity of the MG, which can receive and send media streams 
and control streams. The attributes, that is, parameters about the media streams, 
modem, and bearer capability, of a termination can be used to describe the 
termination in commands. Terminal functions: supporting signal, event detection, and 
data statistics.
▫
Terminals are classified into semi-permanent terminals and temporary terminals. 
The semi-permanent termination may represent a physical entity, for example, a 
TDM channel. The temporary termination may represent a temporary 
information flow, for example, an RTP flow. In this case, the termination exists 
only when the MG uses the information flow.
▫
A termination has a unique ID, which is allocated by the MG when the 
termination is created.
•
Root :The root is a special terminal and represents the entire MG. When the root is 
used as an input parameter of the command, the command can be used on the entire 
gateway instead of a terminal.
•
A context is a relationship between a group of terminals. If there are more than two 
terminals in an association, the association describes the topology and media mixing 
and/or exchange parameters between the terminals.
▫
ContextID：Indicates the association ID. It is a 32-bit integer selected by the MG. 
It is unique within the MG range.
▫
Topology structure：The associated topology structure describes the media flow 
direction between terminals in the association. There are three connection values: 
unidirectional, bidirectional, and isolated. Unidirectional media streams are 
unidirectional media streams between two terminals. Two-way media stream 
refers to the two-way media stream between two terminals. Isolation means that 
there is no media stream between two terminals. The topology structure is used 
only to describe the association.

===== PAGE 142 =====
•
Connection Model:
▫
The maximum number of terminals in an association is a feature of the MG. The 
media gateway that supports only point-to-point connections can exist in only 
two terminals in each association. A media gateway that supports conference 
calls allows three or more terminals to exist in one association.

===== PAGE 143 =====

===== PAGE 144 =====
•
H.248 messages can be transmitted based on UDP/IP. In addition, H.248 messages can 
be transmitted based on other transmission protocols, such as Transmission Control 
Protocol (TCP), Stream Control Transmission Protocol (SCTP), M3UA, and Message 
Transfer Part layer 3-broadband (MTP3-B) over ATM.

===== PAGE 145 =====
•
Command is the main content of an H.248 message. It controls the association and 
terminal attributes, including the event detected by the specified terminal, the signal 
and action used by the terminal, and the associated topology structure. A command 
consists of a command header (CMDHdr) and command parameters. In the H.248 
protocol, command parameters are organized into descriptors.

===== PAGE 146 =====
•
A message is an information unit sent or received by the H.248 protocol. In the H.248 
protocol, one ore more commands are encapsulated in a message.
•
A message may be encoded in a binary format or in a text format. In the case of 
binary codes, specifications defined in ITU-T X.680 (ASN.1) are used for description, 
and BER rules defined in X.690 for encoding; in the case of text format, RFC 2234 ABNF 
specifications are followed. MGCs should support both encoding formats. MGs may 
support one of or both formats. 

===== PAGE 147 =====
•
Most of the H.248-defined commands are used by the MGC to control the MG. 
Generally, the MGC serves as the command initiator, and the MG serves as the 
command responder. Except for the Notify and ServiceChange commands. The Notify 
command is sent by the MG to the MGC, and the ServiceChange command can be 
initiated by the MG or the MGC.

===== PAGE 148 =====

===== PAGE 149 =====
•
Dial plan dmap1. [2-9]xxxxxx indicates that the user can dial any seven digits starting 
with a digit from 2 to 9. 13xxxxxxxxx indicates any 11 number starting with 13. 
0xxxxxxxxx indicates any 10-digit number starting with 0. 9xxxx: any five-digit number 
starting with 9; "1[0124-9]x" indicates a number starting with 1 and a decimal number 
other than 3 is any three digits of the second digit. E: indicates the letter E. x is a 
wildcard value, indicating any digit ranging from 0 to 9. A period (.) indicates that the 
number of digits or characters in front of the period (.) can be zero or more. The value 
x.F indicates that a subscriber can dial any digit (zero digit at most) after dialing a 
digit ranging from 0 to 9. The value [0-9EF].L indicates that a subscriber can dial any 
digit starting with E or F after dialing a digit ranging from 0 to 9, and the long timer 
expires. In fact, E and F correspond to * and # in the DTMF code.

===== PAGE 150 =====
•
Transactions are classified into two types: request and response. There are also two 
types of responses: transaction reply and transaction pending.
▫
A transaction reply is a response to a transaction request from a transaction 
recipient. Each transaction requires a reply. If all commands in a transaction 
request are successfully executed, a transaction request is successfully executed. 
A non-optional command in the transaction request fails to be executed.
▫
The transaction pending is executed by the receiver, indicating that the 
transaction is being processed but is not completed. When the command 
processing time is long, the sender can be prevented from resending the 
transaction request.
•
A transaction is represented as a transaction request. The transaction request receiver 
must respond with a transaction reply. Before the transaction, many transaction 
pending responses may be returned.

===== PAGE 151 =====

===== PAGE 152 =====
•
Initialization process: 
▫
The H.248 MG must be registered with the SoftX3000 before it can provide 
services. 
▫
After the MG is registered successfully, the MGC modifies the attributes of all the 
semi-permanent terminations of the MG in the null context. Instructs the MG to 
detect the offhook event of the user. In this case, the terminal can receive or 
initiate a call. 
▪
The MGC performs operations on the terminals in the MG through null 
association. The attributes of the terminals are modified through the 
Modify command. 
▪
After receiving the Modify command, the MG returns a response.

===== PAGE 153 =====
•
The call process is as follows:
▫
Step 1: After detecting that user A picks up the phone, MG A sends a Notify 
message to notify the server. The server replies with a Reply message, indicating 
that the MG A message has been received.
▫
Step 2: The server sends a Modify command to the MG A. The command 
contains the digitmap information, and the MG A plays the dial tone to user A. 
MG A replies with a Reply message, indicating that the command delivered by 
the server has been received.
▫
Step 3: After hearing the dial tone, the user starts dialing. After receiving the digit, 
the MG A compares it with the digitmap rule delivered by the server. If the 
matching succeeds, the MG A sends a Notify message to the server. The server 
replies with a Reply message, indicating that the MG A message has been 
received.
▫
Step 4: The server sends an ADD command to the MG A, instructing the MG A to 
create a new context and add TDM termination and RTP termination to the 
context. The MG A replies with a Reply message, indicating that the command 
delivered by the server has been received.
▫
Step 5: The server analyzes the dialing of user A, finds the called party, and sends 
an ADD command to the called media gateway MG B, instructing it to create a 
new context and add TDM termination and RTP termination to the context. The 
MG B replies with a Reply message, indicating that the command delivered by 
the server has been received.
▫
Step 6: The server sends a Modify command to the MG B to instruct the MG B to 
play the ringing tone to the called party. The MG B replies with a Reply message, 
indicating that the command delivered by the server has been received.

===== PAGE 154 =====
•
Step 7: The server sends a Modify command to the MG A to instruct the MG A to play 
the ring back tone to the calling party. The MG A replies with a Reply message, 
indicating that the command delivered by the server has been received.
•
Step8: After detecting the offhook event of the called party, the MG B sends a Notify 
message to the server. The server replies with a Reply message, indicating that the 
message has been received.
•
Step9: The server sends a Modify command to the MG B to activate the association 
and create a voice channel. The MG B replies with a Reply message, indicating that the 
command delivered by the server has been received.

===== PAGE 155 =====
•
The session termination process is as follows: Assume that user A hangs up first.
▫
Step10: The server sends a Modify command to the MG A to activate the 
association and create a voice channel. The MG A replies with a Reply message, 
indicating that the command delivered by the server has been received.
▫
Step11: After detecting the on-hook time of user A, the MG A sends a Notify 
message to notify the server. The server replies with a Reply message, indicating 
that the message has been received.
▫
Step12: The server sends a Modify command to the MG A to deactivate the RTP 
terminal of user A. The MG A replies with a Reply message, indicating that the 
command delivered by the server has been received.
▫
Step13: The server sends a Subtract command to the MG A, instructing the MG A 
to delete all semi-permanent terminals and temporary RTP terminals associated 
with user A. The MG A replies with a Reply message, indicating that the 
command delivered by the server has been received.
▫
Step14: The server sends a Modify command to the MG B to deactivate the RTP 
terminal of user B and plays the busy tone to user B. The MG B replies with a 
Reply message, indicating that the command delivered by the server has been 
received.
▫
Step15: The server sends a Modify command to the MG A, instructing the MG A 
to continue detecting the offhook event of user A. The MG A replies with a Reply 
message, indicating that the command delivered by the server has been received.

===== PAGE 156 =====
•
Step16: After user B hears the busy tone, the MG B detects the event and sends a 
Notify message to notify the server. The server replies with a Reply message, indicating 
that the message has been received.
•
Step17: The server sends the Subtract command to the MG B to delete all semi-
permanent terminals and temporary RTP terminals associated with user B and delete 
the association. The MG B replies with a Reply message, indicating that the command 
delivered by the server has been received.
•
Step18: The server sends a Modify command to the MG B, instructing the MG B to 
continue detecting the offhook event of user B. The MG B replies with a Reply message, 
indicating that the command delivered by the server has been received.

===== PAGE 157 =====

===== PAGE 158 =====

===== PAGE 159 =====

===== PAGE 160 =====
•
GPON ONT voice service: The voice processing and conversion chip inside the ONT can 
convert the analog voice data at the user end into digital signals and packetize the 
NGN protocol. The ONT supports the SIP and MGCP protocols. However, a device can 
use only one protocol at a time. If you need to change the protocol, you need to 
change the software version of the terminal. The ONT can also be connected to the SIP 
phone of the user through the Ethernet port. In this case, the SIP phone uses the SIP 
protocol to complete the packaging of user data, and transparently transmits the data 
to the upper-layer softswitch for processing through the ONT and OLT.
•
GPON ONU voice service: The ONU connects to a common phone through its POTS 
port, converts analog signals into digital signals through internal digital-to-analog 
conversion, and packs protocols. The ONU supports the H.248 and SIP protocols. The 
ONU can also be connected to the SIP phone of the user through the Ethernet port. In 
this case, the SIP phone uses the SIP protocol to complete the packaging of user data, 
and transparently transmits the data to the upper-layer softswitch for processing 
through the ONT and OLT.
•
GPON MDU voice service: The home gateway packs the POTS service of users using 
the SIP/MGCP protocol provided by itself, integrates the Internet access or IPTV service, 
and uploads the service to the MA5616. The MA5616+MA5680T transparently 
transmits all services from the home gateway. The telephone service is encapsulated 
into packets through the protocol of the home gateway and transparently transmitted 
by the GPON system. Then, the packets are sent to the softswitch for processing to 
implement the VoIP function. The MA5616 can also be directly connected to the POTS 
phone and transparently transmitted to the softswitch through the MA5680T to 
implement the VoIP service.

===== PAGE 181 =====
•
F/S/P: Indicates the subrack ID, slot ID, and port ID of a port.
•
PTPSrvState: Indicates the running status of the physical layer of a port. The possible 
query results are as follows: (1) Normal: normal; (2) PowerDeny: low power; and (3) 
Fault: A fault occurs.
•
PTPAdmState: Indicates the physical layer management status of a port. The possible 
query results are as follows: (1) NoLoop, NoTest: No loopback, not tested; (2) Noloop, 
Test: No loopback, test in progress; (3) LLoop, NoTest: Local loopback, not tested; (4) 
LLoop, Test: Local loopback, test in progress; (5) RLoop, NoTest: remote loopback, not 
tested; and (6) RLoop, Test: remote loopback, test in progress.
•
CTPSrvState: Indicates the service running status of a port. The possible query results 
are as follows: (1) Idle, (2) Offhook; (3) Locked; (4) Ringing; (5) Fault; and (6) –
(indicating that no subscriber is configured).
•
CTPAdmState: Indicates the service management status of a port. The possible query 
results are as follows: (1) LBlock: local blocking; (2) RBlock: remote blocking; (3) 
StartSvc: starting Services; and (4) -: no subscriber is configured.
•
LineState: Indicates the subscriber line status.

===== PAGE 184 =====

===== PAGE 185 =====

===== PAGE 186 =====

===== PAGE 187 =====

===== PAGE 188 =====

===== PAGE 189 =====
•
Unicast: implements point-to-point transmission, with only one sender and one 
receiver.
•
Broadcast: implements point-to-point transmission. There is only one sender and all 
reachable receivers on the LAN.

===== PAGE 190 =====
•
In conclusion, the multicast technology effectively solves the problem of single-point 
sending and multi-point receiving, and implements efficient point-to-multipoint data 
transmission in the IP network.

===== PAGE 191 =====
•
In multicast mode:
▫
The sender of a message is called a multicast source. The receivers of the same 
message form a multicast group, and each receiver is a multicast group member. 
The router that provides the multicast function is called a multicast router.
▫
A multicast source sends multicast data to a specific multicast group, regardless 
of the location of group members.
▫
The multicast router copies the data and forwards it to the network branch that 
requires the data or has multicast receivers.
▫
Hosts join the multicast groups that they are interested in so that they can 
receive the data packets which sent to these multicast groups.
•
The audience can enable or disable the STB and switch between channels at any time, 
indicating that the host dynamically joins or leaves a multicast group.
▫
Note: In common IPTV scenarios, the multicast mode is used for the live TV 
service, and the unicast mode is used for the VOD service.

===== PAGE 192 =====
•
Advantages of multicast:
▫
Improve efficiency: Reduce network traffic and hardware compliance.
▫
Performance optimization: reduces redundant traffic, saves network bandwidth, 
and reduces network load.
▫
Distributed application: Multicast can adapt to distributed applications. When the 
number of receivers changes, network traffic fluctuates stably.
▫
Enhanced security: Compared with broadcast, it improves the security of 
information transmission because the information is sent only to the receivers 
who need the information.
•
Disadvantages of multicast:
▫
Best-effort: Multicast is based on UDP, and packet loss is inevitable. Therefore, 
multicast applications cannot depend on multicast networks to ensure reliability. 
Multicast networks must be specially designed to meet this requirement.
▫
No congestion avoidance mechanism: The default TCP window mechanism and 
slow-start mechanism may cause multicast congestion.
▫
Packet duplication: Some special mechanisms (such as the Assert mechanism and 
SPT switchover mechanism) of multicast protocols may cause occasional packet 
duplication.
▫
Packet out-of-order: The same multicast protocol sometimes causes out-of-order 
packet arrival.

===== PAGE 193 =====
•
The multicast technology effectively solves the problem of single-point sending and 
multi-point receiving, and realizes the efficient point-to-multipoint data transmission in 
the IP network.
▫
Some new value-added services can be conveniently provided by using the 
multicast feature of the network, including information service fields of the 
Internet such as online TV, IPTV, remote education, remote medical treatment, 
network radio, real-time video conference, network game, and data copy (IDC).
•
Multicast is applicable to point-to-multipoint data transmission, including:
▫
Application of multimedia and streaming media;
▫
Network training and communication in joint operations;
▫
Data warehouse and financial applications (stocks)
▫
Nowadays, IP multicast technology has been applied in the Internet information 
service provided by ISP. For example, online live TV, network TV, remote 
education, telemedicine, network radio, and real-time video/audio conference.

===== PAGE 194 =====
•
The preceding figure shows the relationships between basic management objects.
▫
Multicast VLAN: indicates the VLAN carried in the multicast data. It is also called 
MVLAN.Generally, multicast services are classified by ISP. The forwarding plane, 
control plane, and management plane of a VLAN instance are implemented to 
implement the multicast service provisioning without affecting each other on the 
same device. Except for the super VLAN, any VLAN configured on the switch can 
be used as a multicast VLAN.
▫
Multicast program: It is equivalent to a multicast group. The basic attribute of a 
multicast program is the multicast IP address. The device can perform more 
refined management, such as permission control and CAC, through this object. 
Programs are classified into pre-configured programs and dynamic programs 
according to whether the multicast IP address of each program is pre-configured 
before service provisioning.
▫
Multicast upstream port: connects the multicast source and the device, and also 
connects the upper-layer multicast router and the device. Upstream ports are 
classified into manually configured upstream ports and dynamic upstream ports 
based on whether they depend on the link layer loop protocol.
▫
Multicast user: a receiver of multicast data. A service flow carrying multicast 
control packets in the upstream direction must be configured for the multicast 
user (the device can identify the multicast user by traffic classification). Therefore, 
the multicast user corresponds to a unique terminal or a unique user. In addition, 
a multicast VLAN must be specified for the multicast user, that is, the ISP to 
which the user belongs.

===== PAGE 195 =====
•
Notes:
▫
ONTs are deployed in user homes. Currently, one ONT cannot provision multiple 
multicast users. Provisioning only one multicast user does not affect the scenario 
where one ONT is connected to multiple STBs. 
▫
The traffic stream with the QinQ attribute does not support the multicast user.
▫
The traffic streams with double VLAN traffic classification do not support 
multicast users.

===== PAGE 196 =====

===== PAGE 197 =====
•
ASM model:
▫
Any sender can serve as the multicast source to send data to the group. 
▫
The receiver does not know the location of the multicast source in advance. As 
long as the receiver joins the corresponding group, the receiver receives the 
multicast traffic intended for the group. 
▫
In intra-domain multicast, both PIM-DM and PIM-SM support the ASM model. 
•
SFM model:
▫
The SFM is a derivative model of the ASM. The multicast source filtering policy is 
added based on the ASM. Therefore, this document uses ASM to name both SFM 
and ASM. 
▫
SFM extends the source filtering function of the upper-layer protocol module, 
that is, SFM can allow only the multicast data of the specified multicast source to 
pass.
•
SSM model: 
▫
In daily life, users may be interested only in the multicast information sent by 
specific sources and not want to receive information sent by other sources. SSM 
provides a service transmitting model that enables users to specify the 
information source at the user end. 
▫
SSM differs from ASM in that receivers in the SSM model already know the 
specific location of the multicast source by other means. In addition, the SSM 
model has no domains. As long as the receiver knows the information about the 
multicast source, the receiver can directly create the corresponding multicast 
transmission path. 

===== PAGE 198 =====
•
Select a group filtering mode based on the following rules:
▫
Preferentially select ASMSSM for the compatibility with the original VLAN+GMAC. 
▫
Preferentially select ASM ONLY or SSM ONLY if strict requirements are imposed 
on the security filtering of ASM and SSM packets.  
▫
Preferentially use ASMSSM if various STBs on the home network need to be 
compatible to the maximum extent.  
▫
To implement SIP+GIP forwarding, that is, if you do not want to restrict the SIP 
and GIP planning between different ISPs, select SSM ONLY. However, SSM ONLY 
needs to support IGMPv3 on the entire network. 
•
All IGMP versions support the ASM model. IGMPv3 can be directly applied to the SSM 
model, while IGMPv1 and IGMPv2 need to be supported by SSM mapping.
•
Protocol independent multicast (PIM)
▫
In PIM-SM mode, the ASM model is distinguished from the SSM model based on 
multicast addresses in data and protocol packets. 
▪
If the multicast addresses are within the SSM multicast address range, PIM-
SM is implemented in SSM mode. PIM-SSM is not only efficient, but also 
simplifies the multicast address allocation process, especially when a 
specific group has only one specific source. 
▪
If the multicast addresses are within the ASM multicast address range, PIM-
SM is implemented in the ASM mode.
•
ASM: any-source multicast; SSM: source-specific multicast

===== PAGE 199 =====

===== PAGE 200 =====
•
Based on the protocol function scope, multicast protocols are classified into the IGMP 
(used between hosts and multicast routers) and multicast routing protocols (between 
multicast routers). 
▫
Internet Group Management Protocol (IGMP): It is used between hosts and 
routers to define the mechanism for establishing and maintaining multicast 
member relationships between hosts and routers. IGMP includes IGMPv1, 
IGMPv2, and IGMPv3. 
▫
Multicast routing protocols: aim to construct a loop-free distribution tree 
structure, establish and maintain multicast routing, and correctly and efficiently 
forward multicast data. 
▪
Multicast routing protocols are classified into intra-domain multicast 
routing protocols (including PIM-SM, PIM-DM, and DVMRP) and inter-
domain multicast routing protocols (including MBGP and MSDP). 
▫
To effectively prevent the multicast data from diffusing at the Layer-2 network, 
Layer-2 multicast protocols such as IGMP Snooping are introduced. 

===== PAGE 201 =====
•
To enable the communication between a multicast source and its members, a 
network-layer multicast address, that is, a multicast IP multicast, must be available. In 
addition, a technology must also be available for mapping the multicast IP address to 
a link-layer multicast MAC address. 
•
Multicast IP address
▫
As specified by IANA, multicast packets use class-D IP addresses (224.0.0.0–
239.255.255.255) as their destination addresses, and the class-D IP addresses 
must not appear in the source IP address field of the IP packets. The network 
segment from 224.0.0.0 to 224.0.0.255 is reserved for network protocols in the 
local network. The network segment from 224.0.1.0 to 238.255.255.255 is used as 
user multicast addresses, which are valid globally. The network segment from 
239.0.0.0 to 239.255.255.255 is used as local management group addresses
(similar to private addresses). 
▫
Multicast addresses are not allocated to the receiving device or the multicast 
source device for identifying their network location. In the case of the multicast 
source device, the allocated multicast address is used for generating and carrying 
multicast data; in the case of the receiving device, the multicast address is used 
for distinguishing multicast data. 
▫
In an actual multicast application, the multicast address usually does not need to 
be manually input. For example, in the live TV service, a menu GUI is provided. 
When the user demands a program by using a remote controller, the application 
software will automatically obtain the multicast IP address corresponding to the 
program. 

===== PAGE 202 =====
•
Ethernet multicast MAC address
▫
When IP packets are unicast over an Ethernet, the destination MAC addresses 
used are the MAC addresses of receivers. However, in the transmission of 
multicast packets, the transmission destination is no longer a specific receiver. 
Instead, it is a group with uncertain members. In this case, the multicast MAC 
address is used. 
▫
As specified by IANA, the high-order 25 bits of the multicast MAC address are 
0x01005e, and the low-order 23 bits of the MAC address are the low-order 23 
bits of the multicast IP address. 

===== PAGE 203 =====
•
As specified by IANA, the most significant 25 bits of a multicast MAC address are 
0x01005e, and the least significant 23 bits of the MAC address are the least significant 
23 bits of the multicast IP address. 
•
In a multicast IP address, the most significant 4 bits are 1110, indicating the multicast 
identifier. Information about 5 bits of the IP address is lost, because only 23 bits of the 
28 least significant bits of the IP address are mapped to the MAC address. As a result, 
32 multicast IP addresses are mapped to the same MAC address.  

===== PAGE 204 =====

===== PAGE 205 =====
•
Key points of multicast data forwarding:
▫
All multicast routers must know the multicast source and must forward multicast 
packets from the source to the destination. 
▫
Each multicast router maintains a multicast forwarding table (multicast routing 
table).
▫
The multicast routing protocols must determine the upstream port (closer to the 
source).
•
PIM-DM:
▫
PIM-DM forwards multicast packets in push mode and is for use on small-scale 
networks with densely distributed multicast group members. In push mode, it is 
assumed that each subnet in the network has at least one (S, G) multicast group 
receiver. Therefore, the multicast data is pushed to each corner of the network, 
and then the prune operation is performed. The router that does not need 
multicast traffic will prune itself from the multicast distribution tree.  
•
PIM-SM:
▫
PIM-SM uses the pull mode rather than the push mode. In pull mode, it is 
assumed that no receiver exists in the network, except that a device requests to 
be a receiver using the join mechanism that is displayed. 

===== PAGE 206 =====
•
There are 2 types of routing entries in a PIM network: (S, G) entry and (*, G) entry
▫
An (S, G) routing entry knows the location of the multicast source S, and is 
mainly used to set up an SPT on the PIM router, which is applicable to the PIM-
DM and PIM-SM networks. 
▫
A (*, G) entry knows only the multicast group (G) and is often used to set up an 
RPT on PIM routers, which is applicable to the PIM-SM and bidirectional PIM 
networks. 
•
A PIM router may have both (S, G) and (*, G) entries. When a PIM router receives a 
multicast packet with the source address being S and the group address being G and 
the packet passes the RPF check, the router forwards the packet according to the 
following rules: 
▫
If an (S, G) entry exists on the router, the router forwards the multicast packet 
according to the (S, G) entry.
▫
If no (S, G) entry exists but a (*, G) entry exists, the router creates an (S, G) entry 
based on this (*, G) entry, and then forwards the packet according to the (S, G) 
entry.

===== PAGE 207 =====
•
A multicast routing protocol is to set up a distribution tree. A multicast router can 
establish the path in many ways for transmitting data, and the path is called the 
distribution tree. 
•
Classification:
▫
SPT: shortest-path or source distribution trees
▫
RPT: rendezvous point tree

===== PAGE 208 =====

===== PAGE 209 =====
•
IGMP is a protocol used to establish and maintain multicast group memberships 
between hosts and routers. 
▫
IGMP packets are encapsulated in IP mode. The protocol number is 2, and the 
TTL value is usually 1. 
▫
IGMP implements the dynamic join and leaving of members on the host side. 
▫
It manages and maintains the member relationships on the router side and 
exchanges information with the upper layer multicast routing protocols. 

===== PAGE 210 =====
•
IGMP has 3 versions: IGMPv1, IGMPv2, and IGMPv3. 
▫
IGMPv1 is defined in RFC 1112 and is seldom used. 
▫
IGMPv2 is defined in RFC 2236 and adds the mechanism for fast leave of group 
members on the basis of IGMPv1. 
▫
IGMPv3 is defined in RFC 3376. Compared with IGMPv2, IGMPv3 supports batch 
report, maximum query response time, and source filtering. 
▫
On the following slides, we will focus on the principle of IGMPv2. 
•
IGMPv3 is compatible with IGMPv2 in packet processing. If IGMPv3 is enabled on an 
OLT and the upper-layer multicast router switches to IGMPv2, the OLT automatically 
switches to IGMPv2 upon receiving the IGMPv2 packets. If the OLT does not receive 
any more IGMPv2 packets within the preset IGMPv2 timeout time, it automatically 
switches back to IGMPv3. 
•
According to the protocols, IGMPv1 does not support querier election. An IGMPv1 
querier is specified by an upper-layer protocol, such as PIM. Currently, querier election 
can be performed only between multicast devices of the same version on the same 
network segment. To ensure normal operation, configure IGMP of the same version on 
all the multicast devices on the same network segment. 

===== PAGE 211 =====
•
IGMPv1 packet format: 
•
Version: IGMP version. In IGMPv1, the value of this field is 0x1. IGMPv2 does not have 
this field. 
•
Type: IGMP packet type. 
▫
1: General Query
▫
2: Membership Report
•
Unused: unused field. The value of this field must be 0 during packet sending and can 
be ignored during packet receiving. 
•
Checksum: checksum of IGMP messages. This field is set to 0 during verification 
calculation. When a packet is transmitted, the checksum must be calculated and 
inserted into the field. When a packet is received, the checksum must be verified 
before the packet is processed.
•
Group Address: address of a multicast group. When a member relationship is queried, 
this field is set to 0.

===== PAGE 212 =====
•
IGMPv1 manages the multicast group by using the query and response mechanism. On 
the shared network segment with multiple routers, the L3 routing protocol elects the 
unique router for forwarding the multicast information. The unique router also serves 
as the IGMPv1 querier for querying the group member relationships on this network 
segment. 
•
The process of maintaining IGMP group memberships is as follows:
•
The IGMP querier periodically sends a General Query message to query member 
relationships. The destination address of the message is 224.0.0.1, indicating that the 
message will be sent to all hosts and routers on this network segment. 
•
All hosts on the network segment receive the General Query message and have 
different responses. 
▫
A member that is not in the multicast group, such as PC3, does not respond. 
▫
Member of multicast group G1 start their local timers. 
▪
If PC1 does not listen to the Report message sent by other members until 
its timer times out, PC1 sends a Report message carrying the address of 
multicast group G to the querier. 
▪
If PC2 receives a Report message (sent from PC1) before the timer expires, 
PC2 does not send a Report message to the querier, that is, it suppresses its 
response message. 
▪
The initial value of a timer is a random number from 0 to the maximum 
response time. The default value is 10s. 
•
After receiving a Report message, the querier generates a (*, G1) entry for multicast 
group G1 if it finds that group members of G1 exist on the network segment. Once the 
data of the multicast group arrives at the router, the router forwards the data to the 
members on the network segment. 

===== PAGE 213 =====
•
Adding a member to a group:
▫
PC3 joins the multicast group G2 and detects that no multicast data of G2 exists 
on the network. Therefore, PC3 immediately sends a Report message for G2 
without waiting for the Query message.
▫
After receiving the Report message, the IGMP querier learns that members of G2 
exist on the network segment and generates a (*, G2) entry. When multicast 
traffic of G2 exists on the network, the forwards the traffic to the network 
segment.

===== PAGE 214 =====
•
IGMPv1 does not specially define the message for leaving a multicast group. After a 
host leaves a multicast group, it no longer responds to General Query messages sent 
to the group. 
•
When PC1 needs to leave multicast group G1:
▫
If PC1 receives a General Query message from the IGMP querier, it does not send 
a Report message for G1. Because PC2 in multicast group G1 still exists on the 
network segment, PC2 sends a Report message for G1 to the IGMP querier, and 
the IGMP querier does not detect the leave of PC1. 
•
When PC3 needs to leave multicast group G2:
▫
If PC3 receives a General Query message from the IGMP querier, it does not send 
a Report message for G2. The IGMP querier does not receive Report messages 
from G2 members because no other member of group G2 exists on the network 
segment. After a certain period (130s by default), the IGMP querier deletes the 
multicast forwarding entries corresponding to G2. 

===== PAGE 215 =====
•
The IGMPv2 report mechanism and General Query mechanism are the same as those 
of IGMPv1. The following describes only the differences between IGMPv2 and IGMPv1.
•
Compared with IGMPv1, IGMPv2 has the following changes:
▫
IGMPv2 adds 2 types of messages: Leave and Group-Specific Query. 
▫
IGMPv2 has added the Max Response Time field to the General Query message. 
The value of this field can be configured using a command and is used to control 
the speed at which members respond to query messages. The unit is 0.1s. The 
default value is 100, that is, 10s. 
▫
When a General Query message is sent, the group address field is 0.0.0.0. In 
addition, this field has the same meaning as the group address in IGMPv1. 

===== PAGE 216 =====
•
Different from IGMPv1, IGMPv2 has the querier election and leave mechanisms. 
•
IGMPv2 uses an independent querier election mechanism. When multiple multicast 
routers exist on the shared network segment, the router with the smallest IP address is 
elected aa the querier. 
▫
As shown in the preceding figure, IGMPv2 is run on the multicast routers (R1 and 
R2). Both R1 and R2 consider themselves as the querier and send a General 
Query message to the local network segment, and they both receive the General 
Query message from each other. Then, they compare the source IP address in the 
received message with the port address. The GE0/0/0 port address of R1 is less 
than that of G2. Therefore, the port of R1 becomes the IGMP querier on this 
network segment and R2 does not. 
▫
After that, the IGMP querier R1 sends General Query messages to the local 
network segment, and R2 does not send any General Query messages. R2 will 
start a timer (that is, other querier present timer, 125s by default). If the timer 
receives a General Query message from the querier before the timer expires, the 
timer is reset. If the timer does not receive a General Query message from the 
querier before the timer expires, the timer considers that the querier becomes 
invalid and initiates a new querier election process. 

===== PAGE 217 =====
•
In IGMPv2, the process for PC1 to leave multicast group G1 is as follows:
▫
PC1 sends a Leave message for G1 to all multicast routers on the local network 
segment (with destination address 224.0.0.2). 
▫
After receiving the Leave message, the querier sends a Group-Specific Query 
message to G1. The interval for sending messages and the number of sending 
times can be configured using commands. By default, messages are sent once at 
an interval of 1s and the total number of sending times is 2. In addition, the 
querier starts Timer-Membership (Timer-Membership = Sending interval x 
Sending times). 
▫
Another member (PC2) of group G1 exists on the network segment. After 
receiving the Group-Specific Query message sent by the querier, PC2 immediately 
sends a Report message for G1. After receiving the Report message for G1, the 
querier continues to maintain the group membership. 
▫
For G2, after PC3 sends a Leave message, the querier sends a Group-Specific 
Query message for G2; however, no other members of G2 exist on the network 
segment. Therefore, the querier does not receive any Report message for G2. 
After the Timer-Membership expires, the querier deletes the IGMP group entry 
corresponding to (*, G2). When the querier receives multicast traffic from G2, it 
does not forward the multicast traffic to the downstream. 

===== PAGE 218 =====
•
IGMPv3 is compatible with IGMPv2 in packet processing. If IGMPv3 is enabled on an 
OLT and the upper-layer multicast router switches to IGMPv2, the OLT automatically 
switches to IGMPv2 upon receiving the IGMPv2 packets. If the OLT does not receive 
any more IGMPv2 packets within the preset IGMPv2 timeout interval, it automatically 
switches back to IGMPv3. 

===== PAGE 219 =====
•
On an OLT, you can run the igmp mode { proxy | snooping | off } command to set the 
IGMP mode of the MVLAN. A multicast user can watch the programs in the MVLAN 
only when the IGMP mode of the MVLAN is IGMP proxy or snooping. By default, the 
IGMP mode of an MVLAN is off. 
•
The compatibility policies of the IGMP version on access devices vary on the network 
side and the user side. 
▫
The compatibility policies of the IGMP version on the network side are configured 
based on MVLAN. 
▫
The compatibility policies of the IGMP version on the user side can be configured 
based on multicast users. 

===== PAGE 220 =====

===== PAGE 221 =====
•
IGMP proxy
▫
From the perspective of multicast users, an OLT serves as a multicast router that 
implements the functions of the router in the IGMP protocol. Specifically, the OLT 
fixedly functions as an IGMP querier (not supporting querier election for security 
concerns) on the user-side network. The OLT receives and terminates the join 
messages and leave messages of all multicast users, and duplicates the multicast 
program to only the interested multicast users according to the maintained 
group membership table. 
▫
From the perspective of multicast routers, the OLT serves as a multicast host that 
implements the functions of the host in the IGMP protocol. According to the 
changes (addition or deletion) of the record in the group membership table, the 
OLT sends the join or leave message of a program to the upper layer through the 
multicast upstream port. In addition, the OLT responds to the queries of the 
multicast router according to the status of the group membership table. 
▫
It is configurable on the OLT whether to send the IGMP General Query packet to 
all multicast users or to only interested multicast users. 

===== PAGE 222 =====

===== PAGE 223 =====
•
After IGMP snooping is deployed on a switch, the switch listens to IGMP packets 
exchanged between R1 and group members and maintains Layer 2 multicast 
forwarding entries. 
•
After receiving the multicast traffic intended for 224.1.1.1 from the upstream port of 
the switch, the switch queries the multicast group in the Layer 2 multicast forwarding 
entries, and then forwards the traffic to the member port of the corresponding 
multicast group. PC2 and PC3 will not receive the multicast traffic. 
•
Advantages:
▫
Saves network bandwidth and facilitates independent charging for hosts.
▫
Forwards packets independently by each VLAN, improving information security.

===== PAGE 224 =====
•
A device can select the proxy, snooping, or snooping with the proxy function based on 
the MVLAN. 
•
IGMP transparent snooping
▫
Before using this function, run the igmp query-proxy, igmp report-proxy, and 
igmp leave-proxy commands to disable the proxy function of the query, report, 
and leave messages. 
▫
The network-side IGMP version of the device is not affected by the multicast 
router.
•
IGMP snooping with proxy
▫
Receives the query message from a multicast upstream port, constructs a query 
message and sends it to the user (by default, the query proxy is enabled based 
on the MVLAN) or directly forwards the query message (the query proxy is 
disabled based on the MVLAN), and meanwhile responds to the query of the 
multicast router based on the multicast membership table of the device.
▫
Receives the join messages from multicast users and sends only the first join 
message from each multicast user to the MVLAN. Sends only the last leave 
message from each multicast user to the MVLAN. 
▫
Like the IGMP proxy, the network-side IGMP version of a device is affected by the 
multicast router. 

===== PAGE 225 =====

===== PAGE 226 =====
•
Multicast join process [OLT]:
▫
A multicast user switches a channel and sends a join message for demanding a 
new program GIP1. 
▫
The service board receives the join message and enters the IGMP protocol stack 
of the user. After multicast control is performed, the service board creates a 
group member relationship table and creates a multicast forwarding table. Then, 
the service board sends the join message to the control board according to the 
MVLAN1 of the program. 
▫
After receiving the join message, the control board enters the IGMP protocol 
stack of MVLAN1 and generates the following group membership table and 
multicast forwarding table. 
▫
The control board then sends a join message to the multicast router using the 
multicast upstream port of MVLAN1. 
▫
After receiving the multicast stream, the device first duplicates the stream to 
service board 1 according to the multicast forwarding table of the control board, 
and then duplicates the stream to GPON port 1 according to the multicast 
forwarding table of the service board. 
•
Note: Though the SVLAN of a multicast user is different from the MVLAN, the device 
can still implement the mapping to the MVLAN according to the multicast member 
configuration relationship. In this way, cross-VLAN multicast is supported without 
requiring additional configuration. 

===== PAGE 227 =====
•
Normal leave: As defined by IGMPv2, the router must send a Group-Specific Query 
message after receiving the leave message from a host, and it considers that the host 
does not need the data of the group until the query times out. (Similar to IGMPv3)
•
two IGMP packets are transmitted in the case of a channel switching, one for leaving 
the original multicast group and one for joining the new multicast group. Therefore, 
traffic of 2 multicast groups exists on the subscriber line before the original multicast 
group is stopped. If the subscriber line does not reserve sufficient bandwidth for 
carrying the traffic of 2 multicast groups, traffic overflow will occur, resulting in packet 
loss. For example, if video streams are carried, artifacts will occur. 

===== PAGE 228 =====

===== PAGE 229 =====
•
STB silent leave: An STB exits abnormally, for example, the STB is powered off or the 
STB goes offline. 
•
MAC-based fast leave: When generating a group member relationship table, a device 
records multicast users and records the MAC addresses of multicast group members (a 
maximum of 8 MAC addresses can be recorded for each user). 
•
When the device receives a leave message, it first deletes the MAC addresses in the 
multicast group membership table, and stops forwarding the packets of the group only 
when all the MAC addresses of the multicast user are deleted. As shown in the 
preceding figure, the MAC-based quick leave process of IGMPv3 is the same.

===== PAGE 230 =====

===== PAGE 231 =====

===== PAGE 232 =====

===== PAGE 233 =====
•
The first-level duplication is implemented on the control board. By using the 
"VLAN+GIP" index, the control board duplicates multicast data to the service board 
interested in the multicast program in an as-per-requirement manner, effectively 
saving the backplane bandwidth. 
•
The second-level duplication is implemented on the service board. By using the 
"VLAN+GIP" index, the service board duplicates multicast data to the GPON port 
interested in the multicast program in an as-per-requirement manner, effectively 
saving the downstream bandwidth of the GPON port. Then the service board 
encapsulates and transmits the multicast data on the GPON port in the mode of 
multicast GEM port (system-level parameter, configurable, default value: 4095). 
•
The third-level duplication is implemented on the ONT. By using the "VLAN+GMAC" 
whitelist, the ONT filters out unneeded multicast data to avoid bandwidth overflow at 
the downstream ingress (supported only in olt-control mode). Then, by using the 
"VLAN+GMAC" index, the ONT duplicates the multicast data to the ONT ports in an 
as-per-requirement manner (only GMAC forwarding is supported in snooping mode).
•
Note:
▫
For a third-level ONT, it is recommended that you select the chip that supports 
VLAN+GIP forwarding; otherwise, ensure that the mapping between GIP and 
GMAC is not overlapped to avoid artifact caused by overlapping on the same 
ONT. 

===== PAGE 234 =====
•
The first-level duplication is implemented on the control board. By using the 
"VLAN+GIP+SIP" index, the control board duplicates multicast data to the service 
board interested in the multicast program in an as-per-requirement manner, 
effectively saving the backplane bandwidth. 
•
The second-level duplication is implemented on the service board. By using the 
"VLAN+GIP+SIP" index, the service board duplicates multicast data to the GPON port 
interested in the multicast program in an as-per-requirement manner, effectively 
saving the downstream bandwidth of the GPON port. Then the service board 
encapsulates and transmits the multicast data on the GPON port in the mode of 
multicast GEM port (system-level parameter, configurable, default value: 4095). 
•
The third-level duplication is implemented on the ONT. By using the "VLAN+GMAC" 
whitelist, the ONT filters out unneeded multicast data to avoid bandwidth overflow at 
the downstream ingress (supported only in olt-control mode). Then, by using the 
"VLAN+GMAC" index, the ONT duplicates the multicast data to the ONT ports in an 
as-per-requirement manner (only GMAC forwarding is supported in snooping mode). 
•
Note:
▫
Due to the hardware limitations, only the SIPs with fewer than 20 bits are 
distinguished. For example, 1.1.1.1 and 2.1.1.1 are considered as the same SIP by 
the device. 1.1.1.1 and 1.1.1.2 are considered as different SIPs. 
▫
For a third-level ONT, it is recommended that you select the chip that supports 
VLAN+GIP+SIP forwarding; otherwise, ensure that the mapping between GIP and 
GMAC is not overlapped to avoid artifact caused by overlapping on the same 
ONT. 

===== PAGE 235 =====
•
Single-copy duplication
▫
This is the most common duplication mode of GPON multicast (it also refers to 
the duplication mode mentioned in this document unless otherwise stated). This 
mode makes the best of the GPON downstream WDM transmission principle and 
sends multicast data to all ONTs using the non-encrypted GEM port bearer 
channel. Each ONT receives the multicast data according to the multicast 
filtering table. 
•
Multi-copy duplication
▫
The major difference between single-copy duplication and multi-copy duplication 
is as follows: In multi-copy duplication mode, multicast data is duplicated to 
corresponding service ports according to user requirements, encapsulated in the 
encrypted unicast GEM port channel, and then sent to the ONT. 
▫
The multicast forwarding table on the GPON board corresponds to the following 
items: Index = VLAN+GMAC, entry = multicast user list. 

===== PAGE 236 =====
•
CAC is short for call admission control. In this document, it means controlling the setup 
of IGMP sessions. If an IGMP session fails to be set up, a multicast user will fail to 
receive the multicast program demanded. 
•
GPON port bandwidth CAC
▫
GPON single-copy duplication function (default configuration): Under the same 
GPON port, even if multiple multicast users demand the same multicast program, 
the multicast data is duplicated only once and sent to corresponding multicast 
users through the downstream multicast channel. Therefore, this function 
ensures that the downstream multicast bandwidth does not overflow the 
downstream line bandwidth of the GPON port. 

===== PAGE 237 =====
•
Multicast is mainly used in FTTB/C, FTTH, and multicast user access to device ports. 
The following only describes the FTTx scenarios:
•
FTTH: fiber to the home
•
FTTB: fiber to the building
•
FTTC: fiber to the curb
•
AAA: authorization, authentication and accounting

===== PAGE 238 =====
•
Multicast Service Forwarding Process
▫
On a PON port, an OLT distributes multicast content to all ONUs in single-copy 
broadcast mode. 
▫
The OLT uses IGMP proxy and the ONU uses IGMP snooping to control the 
dynamic forwarding of multicast streams. 
▫
The OLT supports the multicast control function and cooperates with the ONU to 
control the multicast permission. 
•
Multicast terms
▫
A multicast group is identified by a multicast IP address. Any host (or other 
receiving device) that joins a multicast group becomes a member of this group 
and can identify and receive the IP packets with the destination addresses being 
the IP multicast address of this group. 
▫
The signal source that takes a multicast group address as the destination address 
to forward IP packets is called a multicast source. A multicast source can send 
data to multiple multicast groups at the same time.
▫
Multicast group member: The members of a multicast group are dynamic. Hosts 
can randomly join and leave a multicast group on a network. Members can be 
widely dispersed across the network. The multicast source cannot be the receiver 
or a member of a group.

===== PAGE 264 =====
•
Command line explanation:
▫
Fast leave: When a multicast user is configured with this attribute, the system 
sends the Group-Specific Query message after receiving the leave message from 
the multicast user. If the system receives no report packets from the multicast 
user within the preset aging time, it assumes that the multicast user has left. The 
default aging time of a multicast user is 0s. That is, the user is regarded as offline 
when the packet of the Group-Specific Query message is not sent. 
▫
IGMP stream ID: ID of the service stream used by IGMP. 
▫
#The display igmp multicast-vlan member command is used to query the 
member information of a multicast user in an MVLAN or query the information 
about the MVLAN corresponding to a multicast user. 

===== PAGE 265 =====
•
Command line explanation:
▫
max-program: maximum number of programs. When you need to configure the 
number of programs that multicast users can watch at the same time, use this 
parameter.
▫
no-limit: indicates that the user can watch 32 programs at the same time. 
▫
The igmp user block command is used to block a multicast user. To temporarily 
stop a user from watching multicast programs without deleting the user, run this 
command. After the command is executed successfully, the multicast user goes 
offline from the current program and cannot demand programs until being 
unblocked. The multicast user, however, is not deleted. 

===== PAGE 266 =====
•
Dynamic ID (program creation mode)
▫
Static: manually added
▫
Dynamic: automatically download based on the configuration of the multicast 
server
•
In program prejoin, the OLT receives in advance the multicast stream of a program to 
the upstream port before a user sends a request to join a program, shortening the 
waiting time of the user for requesting the program. 
▫
The prerequisite to program prejoin is that the program matching mode of the 
MVLAN is set to static configuration mode.

can check whether the multicast service is normal by sending and receiving packets. 
Focus on the number of sent request packets.

This command is used to query the configuration parameters of a specified MVLAN, 
such as the IGMP mode, version, upstream port, and program matching mode. The 
configuration parameter information must be consistent with the actual planning or 
that of the upper-layer device. 
▫
Packet processing enable status
▫
Proxy enable status of the leave message
▫
Log enable status: The system records the logs of multicast users.

uplink-port: To query the real-time traffic statistics of the multicast upstream
