===== PAGE 280 =====

===== PAGE 281 =====
•
The following table lists QoS measurement indicators：
▫
Bandwidth: theoretical transmission capacity of a connection;
▫
Delay: indicates the time taken by a network node to send information to 
another network node. A long delay affects the quality of real-time services (such 
as IP phones).
▫
Jitter: delay change. Jitter can seriously affect the quality of multimedia services, 
such as video on demand (VoD).
▫
Packet loss rate: indicates the percentage of lost packets during network 
transmission.

===== PAGE 282 =====
•
Band width is also called frequency width. In digital devices, the bandwidth is 
expressed in bit/s, that is, the number of bits that can be transmitted per second.
•
The barrel theory: The narrowest section of the barrel determines the size of the object 
that can be passed.
•
The bandwidth determines the data transmission rate. Theoretically, if the bandwidth 
is 100 Mbit/s, the transmission rate can reach 100 Mbit/s.
•
The bandwidth of a transmission channel depends on the minimum bandwidth of all 
links in the channel. As shown in the figure, although the maximum bandwidth in the 
channel is 10 Gbps, the maximum transmission rate from the PC to the server is only 
10 Mbps. The reason is that the minimum channel bandwidth determines the 
maximum transmission bandwidth. Therefore, the minimum bandwidth in the 
transmission channel is an important factor that affects transmission.

===== PAGE 283 =====
•
The delay refers to the time taken by a network node to send information to another 
network node；The unit is second.
•
The end-to-end delay consists of the transmission delay, processing delay, and queue 
delay.
•
Transmission delay is also called serial delay, which depends on the bandwidth to a 
large extent. That is, the transmission delay depends on the physical characteristics 
and transmission distance of the link.
•
The processing delay refers to the time taken by the device to put data packets into 
the queue on the outbound interface. The size of the delay is related to the processing 
performance of the device.
•
Queue latency refers to the time that a data packet stays in the egress queue. The 
queue latency is related to the size and number of data packets in the queue, 
bandwidth, and queue mechanism.

===== PAGE 284 =====
•
Jitter is caused by different end-to-end delays of data packets that belong to the same 
flow. The jitter is the difference between the end-to-end delays of different packets, 
The unit is second.
•
As shown in the preceding figure, the source end sends packets at the same interval. 
Because the end-to-end delay of each packet is different, the packets cannot reach the 
destination end at the same interval. This phenomenon is called jitter.
•
The jitter is directly related to the delay. If the delay is small, the jitter range is small. If 
the delay is large, the jitter range is large.

===== PAGE 285 =====
•
The packet loss rate is used to evaluate the probability of packet loss during end-to-
end packet forwarding.
•
Packet loss may occur during data transmission. For example:
▫
When packets are scheduled to a queue, packet loss occurs because the queue is 
full.
▫
Packets are lost due to link faults or conflicts.
▫
In most cases, packet loss is caused by full queue. When the queue is full, tail 
drop (dropping the last received packet) is used to discard packets.

===== PAGE 286 =====
•
Generally, QoS provides the following three service models:
▫
Best-Effort service (best-effort service model)
▫
Integrated service (IntServ)
▫
Differentiated service (DiffServ) model
•
Now, let's take a look at them one by one.

===== PAGE 287 =====
•
Best-Effort is the simplest service model.
•
The application can send any number of packets at any time without prior approval or 
notification to the network.
•
For the Best-Effort service, the network tries its best to send packets. However, no 
guarantee is provided for performance such as latency and reliability.
•
The Best-Effort service is the default service model of the Internet. It is applicable to 
most network applications, such as FTP and email, and is implemented through the 
FIFO queue.

===== PAGE 288 =====
•
IntServ is a comprehensive service model that can meet various QoS requirements.
•
In this service model, a specific service needs to be applied for from the network before 
packets are sent. The request is implemented through RSVP, which applies for network 
resources for an application before the application starts to send packets.
•
Once the network determines to allocate resources to the application, the network 
maintains a state for each flow (the flow is determined by the IP addresses, port 
numbers, and protocol numbers of the two ends), and performs packet classification, 
traffic policing, queuing, and scheduling based on the state. The application starts to 
send packets only after receiving the acknowledgment information from the network, 
that is, confirming that the network has reserved resources for the packets of the 
application. As long as the packets of the application are controlled within the range 
specified by the traffic parameters, the network promises to meet the QoS 
requirements of the application.
•
For example, if you want to reserve a vehicle, you need to apply for a service in 
advance and reserve resources to meet the requirement when resources are sufficient.
•
However, this also has a drawback, the vehicle service to maintain a large number of 
vehicles for this information.
•
Disadvantage: The implementation is complex. When no traffic is transmitted, the 
bandwidth is exclusively occupied, and the bandwidth usage is low. This solution 
requires that all end-to-end nodes support and run the RSVP protocol.

===== PAGE 289 =====
•
DiffServ is a multi-service model and can meet different QoS requirements. ---
Currently, IP networks are widely used.
•
Before sending a packet, the application does not need to instruct the network to 
reserve resources for the packet. In the DiffServ model, the network does not need to 
maintain the status of each flow. Instead, the network provides specific services based 
on the DiffServ class of each packet (the DiffServ field in each packet header, such as 
the DS field in the IP header).
•
Traffic on the network is divided into multiple classes, and different classes are 
processed differently. That is, the classifies traffic, marks the class in the packet header, 
and sends the packet to the network. Each node on the network only needs to identify 
the class in the packet and process the packet accordingly.
•
For example, if you go to the train, the ticket is used to mark the service you want to 
enjoy, whether it's soft sleeper, hard sleeper, seat or no seat. When you get on the 
train, you can enter the corresponding carriage according to the train ticket and enjoy 
the corresponding service. You can lie down comfortably in the sleeper carriage and 
enter the seat carriage, indicating that you should sit or stand. ---A ticket is like a 
"mark" in the packet header.
•
In addition to traffic classification and marking, the DiffServ model also has a powerful 
queue technology. When the network is congested, that is, the device cannot process 
all the received packets, the packets are queued. ---Queuing analogy with real life。

===== PAGE 290 =====

===== PAGE 291 =====

===== PAGE 292 =====
•
Traffic classification: Packets are classified based on the characteristics of user Ethernet 
packets and certain rules to differentiate services and provide different services.
•
Priority processing: Service flows are marked or re-marked based on priorities. When 
congestion occurs on the device or upstream network, packets can be scheduled based 
on priorities.
•
Traffic policing: Traffic policing is used to limit the traffic and burst of a connection to 
a network. When a packet meets certain conditions, for example, the packet traffic of 
a connection is too heavy, the traffic policing module can take different actions on the 
packet, such as discarding the packet or re-marking the color of the packet (re-setting 
the priority of the packet). In this way, the port rate is relatively stable, and the impact 
on the lower-level device is avoided.
•
Congestion avoidance: When congestion occurs in the queue on the outbound 
interface, the packets that do not meet the requirements are discarded in advance to 
prevent congestion from becoming worse.
•
Congestion management: When congestion occurs in the egress queue of an interface, 
the QoS parameters of high-priority packets can be preferentially guaranteed through 
a proper queue scheduling mechanism.

===== PAGE 293 =====
•
Packets are processed by the control board and then service boards.
•
The service board does not perform traffic classification in the downstream direction. 
Instead, it searches for service flows based on the forwarding mode (VLAN+MAC or S-
VLAN+C-VLAN).

===== PAGE 294 =====

===== PAGE 295 =====
•
Traffic classification is a process in which packets are classified based on the 
characteristics of the packets and certain rules. In this manner, different services are 
differentiated, and different services are provided. For example, if three services, 
namely, HSI, IPTV, and VoIP, are provided for a user, the system divides the data of the 
three services into three flows. The QoS control of the three flows is different.
•
Traffic classification is used to differentiate service flows and provide QoS guarantee 
for various services of users. The system performs service mapping based on service 
flows and prepares for subsequent QoS actions, such as switching from user VLANs to 
network VLANs, upstream and downstream CAR, priority marking, and queuing.
•
Identifying service flows is the basis for the system to further process data.

===== PAGE 296 =====
•
Traffic classification is implemented in a classifier. A classifier consists of a filter and an 
output flow. After Ethernet packets of different services arrive at the device, they are 
classified into different service flows based on traffic classification and then 
differentiated QoS control is performed based on service flows.
•
The object of traffic classification is Ethernet packets. Therefore, for PON access, the 
system segments and reassembles the packets, restores the packets to Ethernet frames, 
and then classifies the packets. Therefore, each GEM port can be considered as a 
logical port that is equivalent to an Ethernet port. The traffic classification is based on 
these logical ports, and therefore these ports are considered to transmit and receive 
Ethernet frames.

===== PAGE 297 =====
•
An S-VLAN is a service VLAN and is used to identify different services. In a service flow, 
an S-VLAN is also called a network-side VLAN or an outer VLAN.
•
A C-VLAN is a customer VLAN and is used to identify different users. In a service flow, 
a C-VLAN is also called an inner VLAN.

===== PAGE 298 =====
•
An S-VLAN is a service VLAN (S-VLAN) and is used to identify different services. In a 
service flow, an S-VLAN is also called a network-side VLAN or an outer VLAN.
•
A C-VLAN is a customer VLAN (C-VLAN) and is used to identify different users. In a 
service flow, a C-VLAN is also called an inner VLAN.

===== PAGE 299 =====

===== PAGE 300 =====

===== PAGE 301 =====

===== PAGE 302 =====
•
Type of service (ToS) is the packet classification in the IP header. The ToS field 
contains eight bits, including the 3-bit IP precedence field, 4-bit ToS sub-field and 1-bit 
unused bit (must be set to 0) The 4-bit ToS indicates the minimum delay, maximum 
throughput, maximum reliability, and minimum cost. Only one of the four bits can be 
set to 1. If all the four bits are 0, the service is a common service.
•
Differentiated Services Code Point (DSCP) is defined in RFC 2474. It is a product of 
IPv4-based type of service (ToS) and IPv6-based traffic class. The six most significant 
bits (bits 7 to 2) of the DS field are used for the DSCP, and the two least significant 
bits (bits 1 and 0) are reserved. The three most significant bits (bits 7 to 6) of the DS 
field are used for the Class Selector CodePoint, CSCP), which indicates a type of DSCP.

===== PAGE 303 =====

===== PAGE 304 =====

===== PAGE 305 =====
•
A common technology for implementing traffic policing is a committed access rate 
(CAR). In a PON network, a DBA (Dynamic Bandwidth Allocation, dynamic bandwidth 
allocation) technology is further used to police upstream traffic of an ONU.
•
Traffic policing is necessary for live network.
▫
To ensure that the incoming traffic of customers complies with the Service Level 
Agreement (SLA).
▫
Adjusts outgoing traffic, suppresses burst traffic, and ensures service quality.
▫
Controls the rate of broadcast packets through packet suppression.

===== PAGE 306 =====
•
Traffic policing is implemented using the CAR technology, which is based on the token 
bucket algorithm.

===== PAGE 307 =====

===== PAGE 308 =====
•
When a packet arrives, the system compares the number of tokens in the bucket with 
the number of tokens in the packet. If there are sufficient tokens, the packet is 
forwarded. Generally, a token is associated with the forwarding permission of a bit. If 
there are insufficient tokens, the packet is discarded or buffered.
•
Tc and Te indicate the number of tokens in the bucket. The initial values of Tc and Te 
are the CBS and EBS, respectively.
•
In color-blind mode, for a single bucket at a single rate:
▫
If the length of the packet does not exceed Tc, the packet is marked green and 
Tc' = Tc – B.
▫
If the packet length exceeds Tc, the packet is marked red and the Tc value 
remains unchanged.
•
Analogy: The car into the high-speed example, there is a pass card on the green light, 
not on the red light.

===== PAGE 309 =====
•
The trTCM algorithm focuses on the rate burst. It first evaluates whether the rate of 
data flows meets the specified burst requirement. The rule is to compare bucket P and 
then bucket C.
•
Tc and Tp are used to indicate the number of tokens in the bucket. The initial values of 
Tc and Tp are the CBS and PBS.
•
In color-blind mode, the following rules apply to the evaluation of incoming packets 
(assuming that the packet size is B):
▫
If the packet length exceeds Tp, the packet is marked red, and Tc and Tp remain 
unchanged.
▫
If the length of the packet does not exceed Tp but exceeds Tc, the packet is 
marked yellow and Tp' = Tp - B.
▫
If the length of the packet does not exceed Tc, the packet is marked green, and 
Tp' = Tp - B, Tc' = Tc - B.
•
Analogy: an example of a car going into high speed.
•
Without a commemorative card, the red light is on. There is no commemorative card 
pass card, yellow light; If you have a commemorative card and a pass card, the green 
light is on.

===== PAGE 310 =====

===== PAGE 311 =====
•
CIR: indicates the committed information rate. This ensures the available bandwidth 
for users even when the system is busy.
•
PIR: indicates the peak information rate. This is the maximum bandwidth available to 
the user when the system is idle. If this parameter is not set, the system obtains the 
value of this parameter based on the formula min(2*cir, 524288).
•
Committed burst size (CBS) Indicates the guaranteed burst rate of packets. If this 
parameter is not set, the system obtains the value of based on the formula 
min(2000+cir*32, 1024000).
•
Peak burst size (PBS) Indicates the maximum burst rate of packets. If this parameter is 
not set, the system obtains the value of this parameter based on the formula 
min(2*cbs, 1024000).

===== PAGE 312 =====

===== PAGE 313 =====
•
The dual-token bucket mechanism of the TrTCM ensures that the CIR is absolutely 
passed and the PIR is fairly passed. The TrTCM, however, marks packets based on the 
bandwidth but not the priority. Therefore, the bandwidth of high-priority services 
cannot be ensured. The enhanced TrTCM that marks packet colors based on the CAR 
threshold solves this problem.
•
Implementation principle:
▫
Set different thresholds for packets with different priorities. Set a high threshold 
for packets with a high priority by running the car-threshold profile command.
▫
When packets with different priorities pass through buckets P and C:
▪
If the number of packets with a certain priority in bucket P exceeds the 
threshold, the packets are discarded. Otherwise, the packet enters bucket C.
▪
If the rate of a packet with a certain priority in bucket C exceeds the 
threshold, the packet is marked yellow. Otherwise, the packet is marked 
green.
▫
After the packets pass, the tokens in each token bucket are reduced by the 
corresponding amount.

===== PAGE 314 =====
•
CAR threshold (unit: percentage), indicating that a packet with a CoS priority can 
obtain a maximum of x% tokens from the CBS and PBS token buckets. When the 
tokens in the token buckets are removed by more than x%, tokens cannot be obtained 
for the CoS priority.

===== PAGE 315 =====
•
Congestion avoidance solves the problem of how to put packets into queues and how 
to discard packets before they enter queues.

===== PAGE 316 =====
•
When tail drop is used, a large number of TCP packets are discarded, causing TCP 
timeout. As a result, the TCP slow start and congestion avoidance mechanisms are 
triggered, reducing the number of TCP packets to be sent. When packets of multiple 
TCP connections are discarded in a queue, these TCP connections enter the slow start 
and congestion avoidance states simultaneously. This process is called TCP global 
synchronization. In this way, the number of packets sent from multiple TCP 
connections to the queue decreases at the same time. As a result, the number of 
packets sent to the queue is less than the rate of packets sent from the line, and the 
line bandwidth usage is reduced. In addition, the traffic volume of the packets sent to 
the queue fluctuates from small to large. As a result, the traffic volume on the line 
fluctuates between small and full. In addition, the delay jitter of specific traffic is 
affected.

===== PAGE 317 =====
•
When the number of packets in a queue is smaller than 40% of the queue depth, 
packets in the queue are not discarded, and packets of all priorities can be added to 
the queue.
▫
When the number of packets in the queue is greater than or equal to 40% of the 
queue depth, the subsequent packets with priority 0 are directly discarded, and 
the packets with other priorities can be added to the queue.
▫
When the number of packets in the queue is greater than or equal to 60% of the 
queue depth, the subsequent packets with priority 1 are directly discarded, and 
the packets with other priorities (except 0 and 1) can be added to the queue.
▫
When the number of packets in the queue is greater than or equal to 80% of the 
queue depth, the subsequent packets whose priorities are 2 and 3 are directly 
discarded. Packets with other priorities (4, 5, 6, and 7) can be added to queues.
▫
When the number of packets in the queue is equal to or greater than 100% of 
the queue depth, the queue tail drop function is enabled. All the packets that 
reach the queue tail drop function are directly discarded.

===== PAGE 318 =====

===== PAGE 319 =====
•
In the system, the drop threshold of yellow packets is 50% and that of green packets is 
100%, which cannot be modified. That is, if the usage of the queue exceeds 50%, the 
yellow packets cannot be added to the queue, but the green packets can be added to 
the queue. The green packets are discarded only when the queue is fully occupied.

===== PAGE 320 =====
•
In the system, the drop threshold of yellow packets is 50% and that of green packets is 
100%, which cannot be modified. That is, if the usage of the queue exceeds 50%, the 
yellow packets cannot be added to the queue, but the green packets can be added to 
the queue. The green packets are discarded only when the queue is fully occupied.

===== PAGE 321 =====
•
Set the lower drop threshold of yellow packets to 40%, the upper drop threshold to 
80%, and the drop ratio of yellow packets to 100%. When the number of yellow 
packets in the queue is smaller than 40% of the queue depth, the packets in the queue 
are not discarded, but the yellow and green packets can be added to the queue.
•
When the number of yellow packets in the queue is greater than or equal to 40% of 
the queue depth, the subsequent yellow packets are discarded based on the discarding 
rate (for example, 25% in the following figure), and the green packets and yellow 
packets can be added to the queue.
•
When the number of yellow packets in the queue is greater than or equal to 80% of 
the queue depth, subsequent yellow packets are directly discarded. The green packets 
can be added to the queue.
•
When the number of packets in a queue is equal to or greater than 100% of the queue 
depth (the queue is completely congested), the queue performs tail drop and all 
subsequent packets are directly discarded.

===== PAGE 322 =====

===== PAGE 323 =====

===== PAGE 324 =====

===== PAGE 325 =====
•
Priority queues are classified into high-priority queues, medium-priority queues, 
normal-priority queues, and low-priority queues in descending order of priority. When 
packets leave a queue, PQ dequeues and sends the packets in the high-priority queue 
until all the packets in the high-priority queue are sent, and then sends the packets in 
the medium-priority queue until all the packets in the medium-priority queue are sent. 
In the same way, PQ dequeues and sends the packets in the normal-priority queue and 
low-priority queue.
•
In this way, packets in the queue with a higher priority are sent first, and packets in the 
queue with a lower priority are processed first when congestion occurs. In this way, 
packets of key services are processed first, and packets of non-key services (such as 
email services) are processed in idle time, this ensures that key services are prioritized 
and network resources are fully utilized.

===== PAGE 326 =====
•
Weighted round robin (WRR) classifies packets and puts them into different queues 
based on packet types.
▫
WRR schedules packets based on the number of packets. Therefore, each queue 
has no fixed bandwidth. With the same scheduling probability, the bandwidth 
obtained by large-sized packets is higher than that obtained by small-sized 
packets. Users are concerned about the bandwidth. When the average packet 
length of each queue is the same or known, you can configure the WRR weight 
to obtain the required bandwidth. However, when the average packet length of a 
queue changes, you cannot obtain the required bandwidth by configuring the 
WRR weight.
▫
Services with low latency requirements (such as voice services) cannot be 
scheduled in a timely manner.
•
The principle of DRR scheduling is similar to that of RR scheduling.
▫
DRR sets a counter Deficit for each queue. The counter is initialized to the 
maximum number of bytes allowed in a scheduling. Generally, the counter is the 
interface MTU. Each time a queue is polled, a packet is output from the queue 
and the packet length is subtracted from the counter value. If the length of a 
packet exceeds the scheduling capability of a queue, DRR allows a negative 
deficit to be generated to ensure that long packets can be scheduled. However, 
the queue will not be scheduled in the next round robin. When the counter value 
is 0 or a negative value, the stops scheduling the queue but continues to 
schedule other queues whose counter values are positive. When the deficits of all 
queues are 0 or negative, the initial value is added to the deficit counter of all 
queues to start a new round of scheduling.

===== PAGE 327 =====
•
Differential weighted round robin (DWRR) scheduling is used to solve the problem 
that DRR weights cannot be set. The DRR scheduling is equivalent to the DWRR 
scheduling with the weight of 1.
•
DWRR sets a counter Deficit for each queue. The initial value of Deficit is Weight x 
MTU.

===== PAGE 328 =====
•
This scheduling mode is more flexible. You can configure PQ scheduling for services 
that must be guaranteed. When there is remaining bandwidth, DWRR scheduling is 
performed for services with low priorities. This ensures that high-priority services can 
be processed and low-priority services can be processed when there is remaining 
bandwidth.

===== PAGE 329 =====

===== PAGE 330 =====
•
The queue depth determines the capability of a queue to process burst packets. The 
deeper the queue depth, the larger the buffer area of the queue, the stronger the 
capability of processing burst packets, and the less the number of lost packets. 
However, the larger the queue depth, the longer the packet delay.
•
The buffer depth of each type of board is fixed and cannot be configured. The 
percentage of the buffer depth of each queue to the total buffer depth can be 
configured.

===== PAGE 331 =====

===== PAGE 332 =====
•
Congestion avoidance is not considered in the animation.

===== PAGE 334 =====

===== PAGE 335 =====

===== PAGE 336 =====

===== PAGE 337 =====

===== PAGE 338 =====

===== PAGE 339 =====

===== PAGE 340 =====
•
End-to-end QoS in the upstream direction:
▫
The QoS policy on the ONT is as follows:
▪
Traffic classification is performed based on the user port or user-side VLAN 
to differentiate services.
▪
Re-marks the 802.1p priority of service packets.
▪
Packets enter queues of different priorities based on packet priorities. PQ 
scheduling is used to ensure that services with high QoS requirements are 
preferentially forwarded.
▫
The QoS policy on the OLT is as follows:
▪
Traffic classification based on the VLAN ID and 802.1p priority of packets;
▪
Trusts user-side priorities or re-marks 802.1p priorities of service packets.
▪
Performs priority scheduling and congestion management based on 802.1p 
priorities.
▪
The DBA is used to limit the upstream bandwidth of the ONU.
▫
The Layer 2 metro network between the OLT and BRAS/SR performs priority 
scheduling based on 802.1p priorities of service packets.

===== PAGE 341 =====
▫
Traffic classification is enabled on the inbound interface of the BRAS/SR to 
identify 802.1p values of different service packets and re-mark priorities based on 
the upper-layer network type.
▪
If the upper-layer network uses native IP, the BRAS/SR maps the 802.1p 
priority to the ToS/DSCP value in the IP header.
▪
If the upper-layer network is an MPLS VPN, the BRAS/SR maps 802.1p 
priorities to MPLS EXP values.
•
The upper-layer network (IP/MPLS) performs traffic classification, congestion 
management, and congestion avoidance based on the ToS/DSCP value in the IP header 
or MPLS EXP priority.

===== PAGE 342 =====
•
Downstream end-to-end QoS:
▫
The broadband Internet access service, VoIP service, and IPTV service are 
transmitted through their egress switches or PEs. The DSCP priority or MPLS EXP 
value is set for each service packet according to the requirement of "different 
services have different priorities". The core/backbone network schedules queues 
based on DSCP priorities or MPLS EXP values.
▫
When a downstream packet reaches the BRAS/SR at the edge of the Layer 3 
network, the BRAS/SR maps the IP DSCP or MPLS EXP of the packet to the VLAN 
802.1p priority. (Optional) The BRAS/SR can also limit the bandwidth of 
downstream user traffic based on service flows.
▪
For the PPPoE broadband access service, the BRAS controls the access 
bandwidth of each broadband user based on the rate authorized by the 
RADIUS server, such as 512 kbit/s, 1 Mbit/s, and 2 Mbit/s.
▪
For IPTV services, queue shaping can be performed on the SR due to server 
or network traffic jitter.
▫
Queue scheduling is performed based on 802.1p priorities of packets on the 
metro network and Layer 2 access network below the BRAS/SR.
▫
The OLT performs traffic classification based on the VLAN ID and 802.1p priority 
of packets, and trusts the network-side priority or re-marks the 802.1p priority of 
service packets. The OLT performs priority scheduling and congestion 
management based on the 802.1p priority and (optional) limits the downstream 
bandwidth based on service flows.
▫
The ONT trusts the network-side 802.1p priority, schedules queues based on the 
802.1p priority, and limits the downstream traffic bandwidth based on the user-
side port (optional).

•
Reference answer:
▫
ABD

•
Network security is a system. Everything on the network may be the target of an 
attack, and everything on the network may become the weapon of an attacker. 
Therefore, the security of the network is not absolute. You need to evaluate the 
security risks of the network, find the shortest plank in security, and improve and 
enhance the security performance.

===== PAGE 356 =====
•
As shown in the preceding figure, two authorized users access the network, and PC3 
obtains an account or IP address for unauthorized access.

===== PAGE 357 =====
•
As shown in the following figure, two authorized users access the network normally. 
PC3 obtains an account or IP address to access the network illegally. To solve this 
problem, enable DHCP option82/PITP/802.1X on the OLT to prevent unauthorized 
users from accessing the network.

===== PAGE 358 =====

===== PAGE 359 =====
•
The DHCP option 82 information is inserted by the DHCP relay agent into the DHCP 
packets sent by the DHCP client to identify the access location of the user terminal. 
Therefore, the DHCP option 82 function is mainly applied to the DHCP relay network.

===== PAGE 360 =====
•
When receiving a packet, the DHCP relay checks whether the packet is a user request 
packet. If the packet is a user request packet and the value of the "giaddr" (gateway IP 
address) field is 0, the DHCP relay fills the IP address of the DHCP relay in the field 
and unicasts the packet to the real DHCP server. In this way, the DHCP packet can 
traverse multiple subnets.
•
When the DHCP relay finds that the response packet is from the DHCP server, it 
broadcasts or unicasts the encapsulated packet according to the broadcast flag in the 
flag field, and then transmits the packet to the DHCP client.
•
The process of exchanging DHCIP Option 82 packets is as follows:
▫
The DHCP client broadcasts a request packet during initialization.
▫
If no DHCP server is available on the local network, the DHCP relay agent 
processes the packet. The DHCP relay agent checks whether the Option 82 field 
exists in the DHCP Request message and processes the message accordingly.
▫
After receiving the DHCP request packet forwarded by the DHCP relay agent, the 
DHCP server records the information carried in the Option 82 field and sends the 
packet with the DHCP configuration and Option 82 information to the DHCP 
relay agent.
▫
After receiving the response packet from the DHCP server, the DHCP relay agent 
processes the Option 82 information in the packet according to the policy and 
then forwards the processed packet to the DHCPv4 client.

===== PAGE 361 =====
•
Note: For all packets between the client and relay, when the IP address is obtained 
from the initial state, the discover and request packets are broadcast. The offer and 
ack packets determine whether to broadcast or unicast based on the broadcast flag bit 
in the request packet. If the flag bit is broadcast, the offer and ack packets are 
broadcast, otherwise, the packet is a unicast packet. So you cann't say it's a broadcast. 
When the lease reaches 50%, the request and ACK packets are unicast.

===== PAGE 362 =====
•
Option field: optional variable-length option field. For BOOTP, this field is also called 
the vendor field. This field contains a large amount of optional terminal initial 
configuration information and network configuration information, such as the IP 
feature configuration information that determines the terminal, domain name 
information, special information that identifies the terminal, default gateway IP 
address of the terminal, IP address of the DNS server, IP address of the WINS server, 
and validity period of the IP address used by the user. The DHCP protocol can provide 
a large amount of configuration information for the client because of this field. This 
field is formed in the format of "CLV", that is, code: identifier number, which uniquely 
identifies the following information and occupies 1 byte. length: indicates the length of 
the following information, which occupies 1 byte. value: indicates the content of the 
information. The length is specified by length, in bytes.
•
Circuit ID (CID): identifies a circuit. The CID is used to identify the information about 
the subscriber access line, such as the shelf ID, slot ID, port ID, and VPI/VCI. The 
operator identifies the user according to the CID and performs access control. The CID 
uses the TLV structure (Type in type-length-value). The Type is fixed to 1.
•
Remote ID (RID): indicates the remote ID. The RID is used to identify the information 
about the remote user, such as the remote flow description, telephone number, and 
user name. The RID uses the TLV structure, and the value of Type is fixed to 2.

===== PAGE 363 =====
•
Raio: The relay agent option (RAIO) encapsulates the obtained user access information 
into different protocol packets in different formats and provides the physical location 
information about the user to the BRAS or DHCP server. In this manner, the service 
operation and control are implemented, and the secure access management of the 
user account is implemented.
•
Splabel: Indicates the description of the service virtual port to which the user belongs. 
You can run the service-port desc command to set this parameter.
•
The RID format is the label of the service virtual port.

===== PAGE 364 =====

===== PAGE 365 =====
•
802.1X defines port-based network access control as follows:
▫
The access device provides the function of authenticating and controlling the 
access port (physical port or logical port).
▫
Before the port passes the authentication, the port is disabled. Therefore, users 
connected to the port cannot obtain network resources.
▫
If the user passes the authentication, the port is enabled and the user can access 
the network. Otherwise, the port is disabled and users cannot access the network.
•
The 802.1X system defines three functional entities: requester system, authentication 
system, and authentication server system.
•
Generally, a digital user terminal functions as a requester system entity. The user 
terminal must be installed with the 802.1X client software to initiate and exit 
authentication.
•
The authentication system authenticates the request of the requester. Generally, the 
authentication system is a network device that supports the 802.lX protocol, and 
provides a service port for the requester. The port can be a physical port or a logical 
port, and implements 802.1X authentication for access users.
•
The authentication server is an entity that provides authentication services for the 
authentication system. Generally, the 802.1X authentication server system resides in 
the AAA center.

===== PAGE 366 =====
•
The ports of the authentication system are classified into controlled ports and 
uncontrolled ports.
▫
A controlled port is used to transmit authenticated service packets. If the user 
passes the authentication, the controlled port is in the authenticated state and 
can transmit service packets. If the user fails to be authenticated, the controlled 
port is in the unauthenticated state and cannot transmit service packets.
▫
An uncontrolled port is always in the bidirectional connection state and can 
transmit authentication protocol packets, regardless of whether the controlled 
port is in the authorized or unauthorized state.

===== PAGE 367 =====
•
The OLT supports the following authentication modes:
▫
In port-based authentication, the port is disabled before the authentication 
succeeds. If the authentication succeeds, the port is enabled and all service virtual 
flows on the port are enabled.
▫
During service flow-based authentication, the service flow is closed before the 
authentication succeeds. After the authentication is successful, the service flow is 
enabled and all user terminals in the service flow can access the network.
▫
In port+MAC address authentication, the port is enabled and only the packets 
whose source MAC address is the authenticated source MAC address can pass 
through.

===== PAGE 368 =====
•
Extensible Authentication Protocol over LAN (EAPoL) When the authentication system 
works in termination mode, the authentication system terminates the EAPoL message, 
converts the message into another authentication protocol (such as RADIUS), and 
sends the user authentication information to the authentication server. Each physical 
port in the authentication system contains controlled ports and uncontrolled ports. An 
uncontrolled port is always in the bidirectional connection state. It is used to transmit 
EAPoL frames and receive EAPoL authentication packets from the authentication 
requester at any time. The controlled port is enabled only when the authentication 
succeeds. The controlled port is used to transmit network resources and services.

===== PAGE 369 =====

===== PAGE 370 =====
•
MAC spoofing is a type of attack in which a malicious user attempts to interfere with 
normal network communication by forging the source MAC address of the malicious 
user as the source MAC address of a normal user or network device. Malicious users 
use MAC address spoofing to threaten network security.

===== PAGE 371 =====

===== PAGE 372 =====
•
Step 1: When user A goes online, the access device monitors the DHCP packet 
exchange between user A and the DHCP server to obtain the MAC address and IP 
address lease time of user A. The system adds a dynamic MAC address binding entry 
with index 50 to record the VLAN, MAC address, and flow ID of user A.
•
step 2, the access device learns the source MAC address (MAC S1) of the DHCP server 
from the response packet of the DHCP server and adds the source MAC address to the 
MAC address filtering entry.
•
Step 3: After the user A goes online, the access device checks validity of the MAC 
address of the data packet sent by the user A according to the dynamic MAC address 
binding entry. Only the data packets with the MAC address of MAC U1 can pass 
through the interface. The data packets with other MAC addresses are discarded.
•
Step 4: After detecting that user A goes offline, the access device deletes the dynamic 
MAC address binding entry of user A.

===== PAGE 373 =====
•
After anti-MAC spoofing is enabled, the MAC address of the IPoE user with a fixed IP 
address needs to be configured statically.
•
The anti-MAC spoofing feature is mutually exclusive with the VMAC, S+C, and PPPoE
SMAC features at the VLAN level.
•
Disabling the anti-MAC spoofing function involves only the deletion of binding entries, 
which does not affect user services or interrupt services on the forwarding plane.

===== PAGE 374 =====
•
IP spoofing attack refers to the attack initiated by a malicious user by sending packets 
with forged IP addresses. Malicious users can forge IP addresses of authorized users to 
damage services of authorized users.
•
Dynamic IP address binding for anti-IP spoofing:
▫
The system disables the dynamic IP address learning function and monitors the 
DHCP online and offline processes of users. When a user goes online, the system 
dynamically obtains the source IP address of the user and binds the source IP 
address to the service flow of the user.
▫
Only the service packets whose source IP address is the IP address bound to the 
service flow can pass through the device.
▫
When the user goes offline, the unbinds the user's source IP address from the 
user service flow.
•
Static IP address binding for anti-IP spoofing:
▫
The MA5680T and MA5683T support binding IPv4 addresses to service ports. 
After the IP address is bound, the service port allows only the IP packets with the 
bound IP address as the source IP address to pass, thus enhancing the system 
security.

===== PAGE 375 =====

===== PAGE 376 =====
•
Dynamic IP address binding for anti-IP spoofing:
▫
The system disables the dynamic IP address learning function and monitors the 
DHCP online and offline processes of users. When a user goes online, the system 
dynamically obtains the source IP address of the user and binds the source IP 
address to the service flow of the user.
▫
Only the service packets whose source IP address is the IP address bound to the 
service flow can pass through the device.
▫
When the user goes offline, the unbinds the user's source IP address from the 
user service flow.

===== PAGE 377 =====

===== PAGE 378 =====
•
For users using fixed IP addresses, if anti-IP spoofing is enabled in the system, you 
must bind static IP addresses to user flows to ensure that user services are normal.
•
When the anti-IP spoofing function is enabled, one MAC address can be bound to only 
one IP address.
•
When the anti-IP spoofing function is enabled, the IP address validity check is 
performed. The check condition is as follows: For the upstream IP packets from the 
user service flow, if the IP anti-spoofing function is enabled for the flow, the packets 
are IPoE packets, and the source IP address is not 0.0.0.0, the check is performed. 
Check whether the source IP address of the packet is one of the IP addresses that are 
manually bound (configured by the user) or dynamically bound for anti-IP spoofing. If 
the source IP address is bound, the packet is allowed to pass. If the source IP address is 
not bound, the packet is discarded.

===== PAGE 379 =====

===== PAGE 380 =====

===== PAGE 381 =====
•
If the number of consecutive DoS attacks detected by a port/ONT/GEM port is equal to 
the number of consecutive DoS attacks detected by the port/ONT/GEM port, the 
port/ONT/GEM port is deleted from the blacklist.

===== PAGE 382 =====
•
When a DoS attack occurs, the system generates a blacklist and the packet processing 
policy is set to deny. In this case, the system forwards the ARP/ND packets to the 
upstream port and discards the packets sent to the CPU.
•
For other protocol packets, the system directly discards the upstream protocol packets 
and the protocol packets sent to the CPU.

===== PAGE 383 =====
•
13 dsl anti dos (VDNF)
•
Upper-layer protocol delivery switch: CORONA_SetAntiDosSwitchRespSrv
•
100 ms timer, Drv_Security_TimeoutProc
•
50 x 100 ms = 5s
•
The system detects attacks for four consecutive times and adds the port to the 
blacklist.
•
The system checks the blacklisted port again 3 minutes later.
•
If no attack is detected for four consecutive times, the device deletes the IP address 
from the blacklist. Drv_Security_ReadyToOpenPort

===== PAGE 391 =====
•
The ACL rules for filtering data packets sent from the IP addresses in the blacklist are 
as follows:
▫
If a packet matches an ACL rule and the ACL rule denies the packet, the packet is 
discarded.
▫
If a packet matches an ACL rule and the ACL rule allows the packet to pass, the 
packet can pass regardless of whether the IP address of the packet is in the 
blacklist.
▫
If the packets do not match any ACL rule, the packets sent by the blacklisted user 
are discarded.

===== PAGE 393 =====

===== PAGE 394 =====

===== PAGE 395 =====

===== PAGE 396 =====
•
In the AES128 encryption system, the MA5600T/MA5603T supports key change and 
switching.
▫
Key replacement: The OLT initiates a key replacement request, and the ONU 
responds to the request and generates a new key.
▫
Due to the limited length of the PLAOM message, the key is sent to the OLT in 
two parts and is sent for three times.
▫
If the OLT does not receive any of the three transmissions, the OLT resends the 
key change request until it receives the same key for three times.
▫
After receiving the new key, the OLT starts to switch the key.
▫
The OLT sends a command to notify the ONU (ONT or MDU) of the frame 
number that uses the new key. This command is generally sent for three times. 
Once the ONU (ONT or MDU) receives the command successfully, it switches the 
check key in the corresponding data frame.
•
The OLT sends a command to notify the ONU (ONT or MDU) of the frame number 
that uses the new key. This command is generally sent for three times. Once the ONU 
(ONT or MDU) receives the command successfully, it switches the check key in the 
corresponding data frame.

===== PAGE 397 =====

===== PAGE 398 =====

===== PAGE 399 =====

===== PAGE 400 =====
•
Check: The N2510 periodically checks whether a rogue ONU exists on a PON port. The 
detection process can only determine whether a continuous-mode rogue ONU exists 
under the PON port, but cannot locate the specific ONU.
▫
The OLT opens an empty window in the PON upstream direction to detect the 
upstream optical signals of the ONU. If the OLT detects that the upstream 
optical signals are still received, the OLT starts the continuous-mode ONU 
troubleshooting process.
•
Detect: The process of detecting an ONU is to determine which ONU is a rogue ONU.
▫
The OLT issues an instruction to enable the upstream optical signal transmission 
of ONU optical modules one by one to detect whether there is an upstream 
optical signal, and determines whether other ONUs go offline after an ONU is 
enabled. If other ONUs go offline after an ONU is enabled, it indicates that the 
ONU is a continuous-mode ONU. The detection process of a continuous-mode 
ONU detects all ONUs connected to the PON port to ensure that all continuous-
mode ONUs are detected.
•
Isolate: The OLT issues an isolation command to the ONU to disable the transmit 
power of the ONU optical module, eliminating the impact of a rogue ONU on other 
ONUs connected to the same PON port.
▫
Once the upstream optical signal transmission of the ONU optical module is 
disabled by the OLT, the disabling is permanent. That is, the upstream optical 
signal transmission of the ONU optical module is also disabled when the ONU is 
reset or powered off and then powered on. This mechanism ensures that the 
continuous-mode ONU is completely isolated unless the OLT issues a command 
to enable the function.

===== PAGE 402 =====

===== PAGE 403 =====

===== PAGE 413 =====
•
The PITP feature provides the physical location information about access users for the 
upper-layer authentication server. After obtaining the physical location information, 
the router binds the user account with the access location information to implement 
authentication, preventing user account theft and roaming.
•
The PITP binds the user physical information to the user account for authentication to 
prevent the user account and password from being stolen.

===== PAGE 414 =====
•
After the PITP P mode is enabled, in the PPPoE discovery phase, the user physical 
location information is added to the PPPoE packet sent from the user side so that the 
user can be authenticated by the upper-layer server. The other processes are the same 
as those of the PPPoE discovery phase.
•
The differences between the PPPoE dialup process in PITP P mode and the PPPoE 
dialup process in PITP P mode are as follows:
•
In the PPPoE discovery phase, the PPPoE packets exchanged between the 
MA5680T/MA5683T and the router carry the user physical location information. The 
MA5680T/MA5683T inserts the user physical information into the received PPPoE 
packet and then forwards the packet to the router. After receiving a PPPoE packet that 
carries the user's physical location information from the router, the strips the 
information and forwards the packet to the user.
•
If the PPPoE user needs to be authenticated by the RADIUS server, the router extracts 
the user physical location information from the PPPoE packet from the 
MA5680T/MA5683T and adds the information to the authentication request packet. In 
this manner, the user physical information is provided for the server authentication.

===== PAGE 415 =====

===== PAGE 416 =====
•
To enable the PITP function, run the following command:
▫
huawei(config)#pitp enable pmode
▫
huawei(config)#pitp port 0/11/0 enable
▫
huawei(config)#pitp service-port 1 enable

===== PAGE 417 =====
•
After the PITP P mode is enabled, in the PPPoE discovery phase, the user physical 
location information is added to the PPPoE packet sent from the user side so that the 
user can be authenticated by the upper-layer server. The other processes are the same 
as those of the PPPoE discovery phase.

===== PAGE 418 =====

===== PAGE 419 =====
•
In PITP V mode, during PPPoE negotiation between an access user and the router, the 
router sends a VBAS-Request packet to the MA5680T/MA5683T/MA5608T, requesting 
the MA5680T/MA5683T/MA5608T to report the physical port information of the 
access user. The MA5680T/MA5683T/MA5608T sends a VBAS-Response packet 
carrying the port information to the router.
•
In PITP V mode, authentication is divided into three phases: discovery, session, and 
session termination.

===== PAGE 420 =====

===== PAGE 421 =====
•
To enable the PITP function, run the following command:
▫
huawei(config)#pitp enable vmode
▫
huawei(config)#pitp port 0/12/0 enable
▫
huawei(config)#pitp service-port 0 enable

===== PAGE 422 =====
•
Discovery phase: When the VBAS function is enabled, the PPPoE discovery phase is 
processed in the same way. After the PPPoE discovery phase ends, the BRAS sends a 
VBAS request packet to the MA5600T/MA5603T/MA5608T. The MA5600T, MA5603T, 
or MA5608T queries the physical information about the port according to the VBAS 
request, and then sends a VBAS response packet to the BRAS. The BRAS parses the 
VBAS response packet and obtains the physical information about the access port.

PON resource management provides the PON device management function, which 
enables you to view the resources and status of a PON device and manage the device. 
The PON resource management has the following benefits:
▫
Helps you learn about the networking of the PON network, status of each node, 
and location and recovery methods when a fault occurs on the network.
▫
Implements maintenance operations such as remote upgrade, configuration 
backup, and restoration on the PON network.
▫
Improves enterprise network O&M efficiency and reduces operation costs.
•
Key indicators: The efficiency of remotely upgrading ONTs in batches is 1500 units per 
hour. Plan the number of devices when planning an upgrade task.

management control interface).

This section describes the knowledge and detailed operations related to eSight PON

===== PAGE 458 =====
•
Zero touch provisioning (ZTP) for PON terminals (including MxUs and ONTs) is 
implemented after OLTs are deployed. The network administrator does not need to 
commission the device software on site. After being powered on, the PON terminal can 
automatically provision service configurations based on the preset zero-configuration 
policy to implement device deployment.
•
Network planning
▫
Plan the device location, upstream port, management IP address, management 
VLAN, and other network and service parameters.
•
Pre-configuration
▫
Configure an ONU zero-configuration policy.
▫
Configure OLT parameters and associate them with the ONU zero-configuration 
policy.
•
Remote device installation and power-on
▫
After an ONU is installed and powered on, the ONU automatically sends a go-
online notification to the OLT upon startup. eSight verifies the device and selects 
deployment information based on the pre-configuration policy, and delivers the 
information to the ONU. After the configuration takes effect, services are 
automatically provisioned.

===== PAGE 459 =====
•
Zero-configuration PON deployment is to deploy devices (including MxUs and ONTs) 
on a PON network after OLTs are deployed. The network administrator does not need 
to commission the software on the installation site. After the PON terminal devices are 
powered on, the service configuration can be automatically provisioned based on the 
preset zero-configuration policy to implement device deployment.

===== PAGE 500 =====

===== PAGE 501 =====
•
NCE is an innovative network cloudification engine developed by Huawei. Positioned as 
the brain of future cloud-based networks, NCE integrates functions such as network 
management, service control, and network analysis. It is the core enablement system 
for network resource pooling, network connection automation and self-optimization, 
and O&M automation.
•
NCE is located at the management and control layer of the cloud network. It manages 
and controls IP, transport, and access devices, and opens capabilities to support 
interconnection and integration with OSSs, BSSs, and service orchestrators to support 
quick customization of the application layer.

===== PAGE 502 =====

===== PAGE 503 =====

===== PAGE 504 =====

===== PAGE 505 =====
Optical-access technologies are generally referred to as fiber to the x (FTTx). FTTx
provides an access solution for all services. It is applicable to the following networking 
scenarios: fiber to the home (FTTH), fiber to the building (FTTB), fiber to the curb (FTTC), 
fiber to the office (FTTO), fiber to the mobile base station (FTTM), and fiber to the Wi-Fi 
(FTTW). FTTx implements the access of various services including voice, data, videos, 
mobile base stations, and hot points. Users can deploy FTTx services, configure services, 
monitor network, and troubleshoot faults on the NCE.
FTTx include OLT NEs and ONU NEs.
•
OLT NEs: MA5600T, MA5603T, MA5608T, MA5680T, MA5683T, MA5606T, MA5603U, 
MA58 series, EA58 series, MA5801 series and EA5801 series. 
•
ONU NEs: 
MA5620/MA5626/MA5628/MA5620E/MA5626E/MA5620G/MA5626G/MA5610/MA561
2/MA5612A/MA5616/MA5651/MA5652/MA5652G/MA5662/MA5621/MA5621A/MA56
22A/MA5623/MA5623A/MA5631/MA5632/MA5635/MA5669/MA5633/MA5658/MA567
1/MA5672M/MA5675M/MA5673/MA5675/MA5694/MA5698/MA5821/MA5822/MA589
8/MA5818/MA5611S/MA5694S/EA5821/MA5621H.
•
DSLAM NE: MA5600V3, MA5600T, MA5603T, MA5608T, MA5606T that are used as 
DSLAM NEs.
•
MSAN NE: UA5000(IPMB), UA5000(PVMV1), MA5600T, MA5603T, MA5608T that are 
used as MSAN Nes.

===== PAGE 684 =====

===== PAGE 685 =====
•
With the continuous increase in the network scale and complexity, O&M complexity is 
intensified. Carriers urgently need to take automatic deployment measures to reduce 
the skill requirements for O&M personnel and effectively control the operating expense 
(OPEX) in a long term.
•
Traditional tier-1 carriers are transforming from copper to optical. This requires 
simplified OSS integration, remote ONT deployment, and intelligent P2MP O&M 
capability to ensure consistent user experience.

===== PAGE 686 =====
•
NM retains the original U2000 functions and provides FCAPS functions such as 
configuration management and alarm monitoring for devices. The MO models are 
subject to NE models. The management scope covers SD-OLTs and traditional OLTs. 
For SD-OLTs, the original U2000 functions are implemented on the devices over the 
SNMP protocol.
•
Traditional OLTs (legacy NEs): support SNMP protocol management only.
•
SD-OLTs (next-generation OLTs): support NETCONF protocol-based NEs and SNMP 
management and inherit the SNMP management capability of traditional OLTs.

of a card to remove it from

displayed on the homepage.

===== PAGE 689 =====
•
Traditional OLT Predeployment Process:
▫
On the EMS, an engineer manually adds an OLT and configures parameters 
such as the IP address, read/write community name, and SNMP parameters.
▫
The engineer installs the OLT and configures parameters on site such as the 
IP address, route, and SNMP parameters to set up a management channel.
▫
On the EMS, the engineer synchronizes device data.
▫
The EMS reports the OLT information to the OSS.
•
OLT ZTD Process:
▫
On NCE, an engineer configures a ZTP policy.
▫
A construction engineer installs an OLT.
▫
The OLT sends a DHCP request to the DHCP server.
▫
The DHCP server allocates an IP address and notifies NCE of this IP address.
▫
The OLT proactively connects to NCE.
▫
NCE automatically verifies the OLT and issues configurations to the OLT.
▫
NCE reports the OLT information to the OSS.

===== PAGE 690 =====
•
Constraints of OLT ZTD:
▫
This feature depends on the third-party DHCP server.
▫
Currently, this feature is only for scenarios with an OSS. The OSS is used to 
allocate NE names and IP addresses, manage NETCONF/YANG-based NEs, and 
deliver JSON scripts.
▫
Only MA5800 V100R019C20 and later versions are supported.

===== PAGE 691 =====
•
OLT ZTD Process:
▫
Import a configuration template: Huawei engineers need to design the OLT go-
online process based on discussions with the customer, and then import the 
design to NCE. Configuration template files (that is, NE initialization scripts) will 
be automatically delivered to NEs during the go-online process of 
NETCONF/YANG-based NEs.
▪
Configure OLT activation parameters: During the go-online process of the 
OLT, a management channel needs to be set up between the OLT and NCE. 
Therefore, you need to preconfigure communication parameters on NCE, 
such as SNMP parameters, default NETCONF username and password used 
for deployment (default username: ncroot, password: NFRoot.123), and 
NETCONF user password used after the OLT goes online.
▪
NOTE: After Call Home is started, the OLT automatically creates a default 
NETCONF administrator. The default NETCONF username and password 
configured for deployment on NCE must be the same as those 
automatically created on the OLT.
▫
Configure the DHCP server: The network center administrator deploys a Dynamic 
Host Configuration Protocol (DHCP) server on the uplink port of the OLT, pre-
allocates the IP address of the OLT in the DHCP server, and configures the IP 
address of the NCE server in Option 125.

===== PAGE 692 =====
▪
NOTE:
−The DHCP server uses Option fields to transmit control information 
and network configuration parameters so that it can dynamically 
allocate IP addresses and provide abundant network configuration 
information for clients.
−A third-party DHCP server used in OLT ZTD must support the 
following options:
−Option 1: subnet mask
−Option 3: gateway
−Option 125: NCE IP address
▫
Install the OLT: The hardware installation engineer installs the OLT on site and 
powers it on.
▫
Send a DHCP request: After being powered on, the OLT automatically sends a 
DHCP request to the DHCP server.
▫
Assign a temporary IP address: The DHCP server responds with a temporary IP 
address. Other necessary parameters (subnet mask, gateway, and NCE IP address) 
are carried in DHCP options for OLT initial configuration.
▫
Send a Call Home request: After being powered on, the OLT initiates a Call Home 
request using the NETCONF Call Home (RFC8071) function to set up a NETCONF 
channel between the OLT and NCE.
▫
Query OLT information: NCE queries OLT information (including NE ID, ESN, 
temporary IP address, subnet mask, and gateway) by using the NETCONF 
channel.
▫
Report OLT information: After receiving a message indicating that the OLT goes 
online, NCE invokes the OSS interface to report information about the new OLT 
to the OSS.
▫
Receive the OSS application configurations: After receiving a message of OLT 
automatic discovery, the OSS authenticates the OLT based on the imported 
equipment serial number (ESN) whitelist. If the OLT's ESN is whitelisted, the OSS 
adds a corresponding NE based on the reported OLT information, and then sends 
the OLT configurations (including the OLT's NE ID, name, real IP address, subnet 
mask, and gateway) to NCE.
▫
Add the OLT to NCE: NCE automatically adds a corresponding NE and delivers 
NE information, including the real IP address, ESN, and NETCONF username and 
its new password, to the OLT. The OLT uses the NETCONF channel to change the 
password of the default NETCONF user.

===== PAGE 693 =====
▪
After the OLT goes online, NCE automatically updates the IP address and 
NE name of the OLT in the database, and then uses the real IP address to 
connect to the OLT.
▪
NOTE: In the activation phase of the go-online process, the OLT obtains an 
IP address in a temporary subnet of the DCN gateway over DHCP. After the 
activation is complete, the OLT no longer uses this IP address.
▫
Automatically deliver the configurations: NCE generates a configuration file and 
sends the file to the OLT using the real IP address.

===== PAGE 694 =====
•
Creating an OLT Replacement Task:
▫
Prerequisites:
▪
The ESN of the device to be replaced and the ESN of the new device have 
been specified.
▫
Context:
▪
In the case of the OLT replacement, the OLT must be replaced only with an 
OLT of the same model with the same type of the control board (or whose 
configuration files are compatible). The number of service boards is not 
limited, but the service board type and slot where the service board resides 
must be the same as that before the replacement. Replacing an OLT with 
that of a different model is not supported, that is, data of an OLT cannot 
be migrated to another OLT of a different model.。
▫
Procedure:
▪
1. Open the Access Controller app. In the Automation Control Center area, 
click the OLT Deployment card.
▪
2. On the page that is displayed, click OLT Replacement.
▪
3. Click Create.
▪
4. In the Create OLT Replacement Task dialog box, set the new ESN of the 
device to be replaced.
▪
5. Click Apply.

===== PAGE 695 =====

===== PAGE 696 =====
•
Pain Points of Traditional ONT Service Provisioning:
▫
Successful service provisioning depends on correct relationships between ONTs 
and PON ports. Incorrect relationships will result in authentication failure and 
therefore reduce the success rate of service provisioning.
▫
Onsite ONT installation and commissioning require high technical skills of field 
engineers and affect service provisioning efficiency and cost effectiveness.
•
Background:
▫
During FTTH service deployment, copper network carriers hope that no 
changes will be made to end users and the CRM for service provisioning. The 
copper-to-optical change for service provisioning is limited between service 
provisioning systems (activating the OSS and NCE). 
▫
ONT ZTP mainly applies to the FTTH scenario where no ONT provisioning 
process is available for Tier 1 carriers. ONT ZTP means that immediately after 
an ONT is powered on, it is authenticated on the cloud, and configurations 
are applied to it if it passes the authentication. Services are provisioned with 
no field visit required. Users can customize the ONT go-online policy.
▫
Two ZTP solutions are provided depending on whether ONTs are planned 
and configured during OLT deployment:
▪
ONT Pre-deployment
▪
On-demand ONT Deployment

===== PAGE 697 =====
•
ONT ZTP has the following restrictions:
▫
The ONT go-online process and service configuration process need to be 
customized by Huawei engineers based on the discussion result with the 
customer and imported them to NCE, after which, the RESTful interfaces can be 
used to provision services.
▫
Only MA5800s are supported.
▫
Only GPON is supported. EPON is not supported. ONTs can only be managed 
using templates.
▫
App files for the service flow orchestration process can be loaded. Currently, only 
one orchestration process is supported on the entire network.
▫
Only Layer 2 channels can be configured. Customized parameters for Wi-Fi, voice, 
and PPPoE services are not supported. You are advised to configure these 
parameters using ACS.

===== PAGE 698 =====
•
Importing the WKE Application:
▫
Prerequisites
▪
A  design package NCEversion_Fan_WorkFlow.zip has been obtained.
•
Procedure:
▫
The BusinessAPI.zip package must be imported to NCE first. The following 
uses BusinessAPI.zip as an example.
▪
1. Decompress the NCEversion_Fan_WorkFlow.zip package to the 
local PC.
▪
2. Log in to the O&M plane at https://IP address of the O&M 
plane:31943.
▪
3. Open the System Settings app and choose System Settings > API 
Orchestration from the main menu.
▪
4. Use FileZilla to upload the design package to the /home/ossadm/ 
directory on the Common_Service node as the ossadm user.
▪
5. Use PuTTY to log in to the Common_Service node as the ossadm
user in SSH mode.
▪
6. Run the su - root command to switch to the root user.
▪
7. Run the following command to copy the design package to the 
/opt/mateinfo/package/designPackageStore/ directory.

===== PAGE 699 =====
# cp /home/ossadm/BusinessAPI.zip      
/opt/mateinfo/package/designPackageStore/
▪
8. Run the following command to switch to the 
/opt/mateinfo/package/designPackageStore/ directory:
# cd /opt/mateinfo/package/designPackageStore
▪
9. Run the following command to change the file owner:
# chown ossuser:ossgroup BusinessAPI.zip
▪
10. Run the following commands to modify the read, write, and execute 
permissions:
# chmod 640 BusinessAPI.zip
▪
11. Choose Deployment from the navigation pane to view the imported 
compressed file.
▪
12. Click the Activate button in the Operation column for a package with 
Package Name of BusinessAPI to activate processes.
▪
13. Click the Publish button in the Operation column for a package with 
Package Name of BusinessAPI to publish the API to the API gateway for the 
upper-layer system to access.
▪
14. (Optional) Import other customized orchestration process packages by 
referring to 4 to 13.

===== PAGE 700 =====
•
Principle description for the ONT ZTP based on ONT service preconfiguration:
▫
Design service flows: Huawei engineers need to design the ONT go-online 
process and service configuration process based on discussions with the customer, 
and then import them to NCE, after which, services can be provisioned using the 
RESTful interfaces.
▫
Import a whitelist: Authenticated ONTs can be imported to NCE in batches as a 
whitelist. After an ONT is powered on for the first time and NCE receives an 
automatic discovery message of the ONT, NCE authenticates the ONT based on 
information in the imported whitelist. If the ONT passes the authentication, NCE 
allows it to go online. Otherwise, NCE does not allow it to go online.
▪
NOTE: A whitelist consists of ONT authentication elements. Currently only 
four types of authentication elements are supported, including the serial 
number (SN), password, MAC address, and LOID.
▫
Configure access profiles: NCE manages ONTs based on profiles only. Therefore, 
access profiles need to be configured on NCE so that orchestrated service flows 
can use them.
▪
Global profiles that need to be configured: DBA profile, GPON line profile, 
GPON service profile, GPON alarm profile, upstream traffic profile, 
downstream traffic profile
▪
Profiles that need to be configured optionally based on actual service 
packages: ONT VAS profile, VLAN service profile, VLAN RAIO profile, 

===== PAGE 701 =====
multicast rights profile

===== PAGE 702 =====
▫
Import an ONT configuration file: Deploy the OLT using the OLT ZTD function of 
NCE.
▪
During the OLT deployment, import an ONT configuration file, which 
contains configurations of all ONTs, to the OLT. In the imported 
configuration file, ONTs are regularly labeled with virtual identifiers.
▪
ONT configurations include the OLT NE ID, virtual port (for example, 
chassis/slot/port/ONT ID), ONT SN, and AccessID (RFS ONT label).
▪
The virtual ONT SN consists of 12 digits and letters, for example, 
zZzZ00100000.
▫
Request for services: A subscriber requests for services at the customer service 
center. Upon the request, service personnel check resource availability on the OSS 
and confirm whether network provisioning conditions are met.
▫
Issue a work order: The CRM issues a work order to the OSS. The work order 
contains the subscriber's address information and service information.
▫
Deliver an ONT: Service personnel provide an ONT to the subscriber at the 
customer service center or mail it to the subscriber's registered address.
▫
Install and power on the ONT: After receiving the ONT, the subscriber installs the 
ONT and connects the drop fiber to the ONT.
▫
The OLT notifies NCE that a new ONT has been discovered: After the ONT is 
powered on, the OLT notifies NCE that a new ONT has been discovered.
▪
The ONT information, including the SN, manufacturer, EquipmentID (type), 
and version, is sent from the ONT to the OLT by the preconfigured virtual 
port. The OLT then sends the ONT information to NCE.
▫
Authenticate the ONT: After receiving the ONT go-online request, NCE 
authenticates the ONT based on information in the imported whitelist. If the 
ONT passes the authentication, NCE allows it to go online. Otherwise, NCE does 
not allow it to go online.
▪
NCE authenticates ONTs on the cloud in a centralized manner, without the 
need to consider binding relationships between ONTs and OLTs. An ONT 
can go online as long as it passes the whitelist authentication. This 
mechanism greatly reduces carriers' planning and management work for 
OLT-ONT binding relationships.
▫
Modify the ONT entry in the configuration file: After the ONT is authenticated, 
NCE finds a virtual ONT (which is labeled with a virtual SN) in the ONT 
configuration file of the OLT, and changes its virtual SN to the SN of the ONT 
that goes online.

===== PAGE 703 =====
▫
Report ONT information to the OSS: NCE reports ONT SN and ONT ID to the 
OSS. The OSS obtains the corresponding ONT alias as the unique identifier and 
associates the ONT alias with the broadband network gateway (BNG) for service 
provisioning.
▫
Verify ONT services: After installing and powering on the ONT, the subscriber 
verifies whether the ONT services run properly.

This is a method of acknowledging whether an alarm is being handled. By using this

===== PAGE 732 =====
•
Optional: If you want to monitor a threshold-cross alarm (TCA), you need to set the 
threshold parameters.
▫
Select the TCA check box.
▫
Configure High Trigger Threshold, High Clear Threshold, and High Alarm Severity 
for TCA with a high threshold.
▫
Configure Low Trigger Threshold, Low Clear Threshold, and Low Alarm Severity 
for TCA with a low threshold.
•
NOTE: 
▫
High Trigger Threshold: When the performance data is greater than the specific 
value, the high TCA alarm is triggered. The alarm severity is same as High Alarm 
Severity.
▫
High Clear Threshold: When the performance data is less than the specific value, 
the high TCA alarm is cleared automatically.
▫
High Clear Threshold must be less than or equal to High Trigger Threshold.
▫
Low Trigger Threshold: When the performance data is less than the specific value, 
the low TCA alarm is triggered. The alarm severity is same as Low Alarm Severity.
▫
Low Clear Threshold: When the performance data is greater than the specific 
value, the low TCA alarm is cleared automatically.
▫
Low Clear Threshold must be greater than or equal to Low Trigger Threshold.

===== PAGE 733 =====
▫
The alarm severity includes the following: Warning, Minor, Major, Critical.
