•
HLD: High level design. It is the design document provided by the pre-sales manager 
for the customers about scenario solutions. An HLD describes in detail the requirement 
analysis, service deployment, and product selection of network construction.
•
HLD can be used to:
▫
Present the detailed network design solution to the customer to help the 
customer fully understand the solution.
▫
Determine the product type, quantity, and deployment location.
▫
Guide the LLD.
•
HLD can be presented in PPT or Word format.
•
In the HLD, service features should be clear; the deployment scheme should be 
complete; configurations can be completed based on the scheme; the specific design 
numbers used by each feature, such as the VLAN ID and IP address, may be uncertain.
•
LLD: Low level design. The output can be in PPT, Excel, Word, or text format.

===== PAGE 758 =====

===== PAGE 759 =====

===== PAGE 760 =====
•
Data center: where servers and application systems are deployed The data center 
provides data and application services for the internal and external users of an 
enterprise.
•
Demilitarized zone (DMZ): Public servers are deployed in this zone to provide access 
services for external visitors (non-employees). The security of this zone is strictly 
controlled.

===== PAGE 761 =====
•
The core layer connects to the network management center, data center, DMZ, the 
aggregation-layer OLT, and the campus egress. The star topology uses the core node 
as the root to ensure a stable architecture, which facilitates expansion and 
maintenance. 
•
There is a clear border between each department or functional system. Changes within 
a module do not affect other modules, simplifying fault locating.
•
The campus egress, core layer, and aggregation layer can use the dual-node 
redundancy design. Key links are trunk links. Type B protection can be used between 
ONUs and OLTs to ensure network reliability.
•
The OLT and core switch can be located in the core equipment room. The floor 
convergence device is replaced by optical splitters. An optical splitter is a passive device 
and has better stability. An ONU can be installed on a desk or in a room information 
box.

===== PAGE 762 =====

===== PAGE 763 =====

===== PAGE 764 =====

===== PAGE 765 =====

===== PAGE 766 =====

===== PAGE 767 =====

===== PAGE 768 =====

===== PAGE 769 =====
•
GPON and 10G GPON use the same ODN. However, GPON and 10G GPON work at 
different wavelengths. They are two independent channels that do not interfere with 
each other. They coexist and work at the same time. GPON and 10G GPON exclusively 
uses its own bandwidth.
•
POL evolution requires new OLT service boards and ONUs.
•
During OLT selection and configuration and ODN planning, sufficient service slots and 
optical path resources must be reserved for network evolution. For details, see POL 
ODN Planning and Design.

===== PAGE 770 =====

===== PAGE 771 =====

===== PAGE 772 =====
•
ONU-side VLAN is C-VLAN, that is, customer VLAN. OLT-side VLAN is S-VLAN, that is, 
service VLAN.
•
Services can be differentiated by single-tagged VLANs or double-tagged VLANs. The 
inner VLAN indicates a specific service or user, and the outer VLAN indicates a service 
or building. This makes planning more flexible and increases the number of available 
VLANs from 4,000 to 4,000 x 4,000.
•
When an OLT is interconnected with the upper-layer core switch and router, the upper-
layer device can see only the S-VLAN of the OLT but cannot see the C-VLAN of the 
ONUs. When the data is transmitted upstream to the OLT, VLAN translation is 
performed once.
•
ONUs can identify IP phones in the following modes and add voice VLAN tags. 1. OUI-
MAC; 2. LLDP-MED

===== PAGE 773 =====

===== PAGE 774 =====

===== PAGE 775 =====

===== PAGE 776 =====

===== PAGE 777 =====
•
ISSU: In-service software upgrade. Online service software upgrade or in-service 
upgrade. 

===== PAGE 778 =====
•
ISSU: In-service software upgrade. Online service software upgrade or in-service 
upgrade. 
•
Downstream traffic switching depends on the ONU agent's gratuitous ARP, while 
upstream traffic switching depends on the gratuitous ARP sent by the new VRRP 
master.
•
A Layer 2 forwarding link is deployed between the master and backup VRRP devices. 
The Layer 3 device in the backup state forwards packets at Layer 2. After the VRRP 
switchover, the new route and route withdrawing information is advertised to the 
upper layer.

===== PAGE 779 =====

===== PAGE 780 =====

===== PAGE 781 =====

===== PAGE 782 =====

===== PAGE 783 =====

===== PAGE 784 =====

===== PAGE 785 =====
•
You are advised to enable the MAC address-triggered ARP entry update function on 
the core switch. Whenever there is upstream data traffic, the downstream ARP 
outbound interface of the switch will be updated to accelerate downstream traffic 
switching.
•
When the ONU is connected to a large number of terminals, the proxy ARP function 
needs to be disabled on the ONU. For example, an ONU is connected to multiple APs, 
and each AP works in local forwarding mode. In this mode, the MAC addresses and IP 
addresses of all Wi-Fi terminals are transparently transmitted. As the Wi-Fi terminals 
are mobile, the number of ARP packets sent by the ONU is large, which may exceed 
the specification.
•
If Layer 2 isolation cannot be implemented on the switch, configure the same MAC 
address learning priority for the network side and user side on the OLT. In addition, to 
prevent a large broadcast domain from overwhelming the MAC address entries of the 
ONU, it is recommended that VLANs be divided based on PON ports. If VLANs cannot 
be planned for isolation, you need to configure the OLT to allow only the packets 
whose source MAC address is the MAC address of the gateway to pass. (The 
prerequisite for implementing this configuration is that the Layer 3 gateway of all 
service traffic is terminated on specific Layer 3 interfaces.)

===== PAGE 786 =====
•
Note: When the upstream port is provided by the control board of the OLT and two 
control boards are configured, both control boards should provide the upstream port 
and use the trunk technology to form a logical link. (If only the active control board 
provides the upstream port and the standby control board does not provide the 
upstream port, the following problem may occur: When the active control board of 
OLT1 resets, the standby control board takes over the control only after 
synchronization. Then, because it does not provide the upstream port, a command is 
issued to perform type B dual-homing switching to switch services to OLT2. The 
switching is slow and takes 5 to 6 seconds.)

===== PAGE 787 =====
•
Scenario 2.1: LAG bundles multiple uplinks into a logical link to increase link 
bandwidth and improve reliability. Access devices support the static LACP mode. When 
a link in the link group is faulty, LACP can quickly detect the link fault and switch to a 
normal link. After the link recovers, services can be switched back automatically.
•
Scenario 2.2: The OLT runs LACP. Two Layer 3 devices run E-Trunk and are configured 
with VRRP to work as the user gateways. The two uplinks work in active/standby 
mode.
•
Scenario 2.3: The OLT is dual-homed to two Layer 3 switches. mVRRP runs between 
the two Layer 3 switches to determine the active/standby relationship of the 
corresponding pair of user interfaces. Link BFD runs between the Layer 3 switches and 
the OLT. Peer BFD runs between the Layer 3 switches to perform fast fault detection 
and trigger rapid active/standby switchover. When the upstream link of the OLT or a 
Layer 3 switch is faulty, mVRRP switches the status and sends gratuitous ARP packets 
to the access side. The OLT uses MAC address learning to switch and forward traffic.

===== PAGE 788 =====
•
Wired authentication specifications and solution limitations:
▫
802.1x supports EAP-MD5 termination, EAP relay, guest VLAN, and restricted 
VLAN, as well as EAP-TLS relay, MAB, critical VLAN, and dynamic VLAN delivery. 
The VLAN ID can be changed through CoA packets. Accounting start and stop 
packets are sent when users go online and offline, and accounting packets are 
periodically sent. EAP-PEAP/TTLS relay is supported.
▫
The ONU functions as the 802.1x authentication point. It terminates EAP packets 
and sends RADIUS packets to complete user authentication. The advantage of 
this mode is that user security can be ensured and controlled at the edge. The 
disadvantage is that the number of authentication points may exceed the 
capacity of the RADIUS server.
•
Wireless authentication: The authentication access point is the fit AP that the user 
accesses. EAP packets are sent from the AP to the AC. The AC initiates authentication 
to the RADIUS server. After the authentication is complete, the AC delivers the 
authentication information and encrypted authentication file to the fit AP. The fit AP 
connects to the mobile phones or laptops.
•
Extensible Authentication Protocol (EAP) is an authentication protocol supported by 
Remote Authentication Dial In User Service (RADIUS) and standardized by the Internet 
Engineering Task Force (IETF) in RFC 2284. EAP is a universal protocol used in PPP 
authentication. It is a framework that supports multiple optional identity 
authentication mechanisms for PPP, including cleartext password, challenge-response, 
and arbitrary dialog sequence.

===== PAGE 789 =====
• All authentication requests are sent to the core switch, and the core switch 
sends authentication requests to the RADIUS server.
• Advantages of this solution:
▫
Unified authentication facilitates management and maintenance.
▫
The ONU provides anti-MAC spoofing, port hardware isolation, and AES 
encryption to ensure the security of the Layer 2 line from the core layer to 
the ONU.
• Limitations of this solution:
▫
When 802.1x authentication and type B protection are used together and 
a PON line switchover occurs, when any of the user MAC address, access 
physical port, and access VLAN changes, the switch considers that a user 
migration occurs. In this case, the switch allows the user to go online at a 
new location only after the user entry ages. Enabling MAC address 
migration on the switch allows users to be authenticated again without 
waiting for the original authentication information to age out.
▫
Switch authentication performance: 40 certificate authentication requests 
per second and 150 MD5 authentication requests per second
•
Selecting which solution depends on the customer's requirements on edge 
authentication: whether to simplify management or to provide high authentication 
protection.

===== PAGE 791 =====

===== PAGE 792 =====

===== PAGE 793 =====

===== PAGE 794 =====
•
T-CONT of Type 1 uses fixed bandwidth. It is mainly used for delay-sensitive services 
and services with higher priorities, such as TDM services. 
•
T-CONTs of Type 2 and Type 3 use assured bandwidth, which are applicable to VoIP, 
video, and high-priority data services.
•
T-CONT of Type 4 uses best-effort bandwidth. It is mainly used for data services such 
as Internet and email services, whose priority is low.
•
T-CONT of Type 5 uses all bandwidth types and can carry all services. 

===== PAGE 795 =====

===== PAGE 796 =====
•
Security: Supports security access control (802.1x and port-based static MAC bonding), 
preventing access of unauthorized cameras.
•
Bandwidth: Reserves bandwidth that is 1.5 times of the bandwidth for the CBR video 
streams, ensuring that the reserved bandwidth is not shared by other services.
•
QoS: Type 3 DBA bandwidth can be used in the upstream direction. The assured 
bandwidth is equal to the code stream, and the maximum bandwidth is equal to the 
maximum burst bandwidth.
•
Fault locating: Supports Y.1731-based POL fault locating and SLA evaluation.

===== PAGE 797 =====
•
Power supply: ONU supports power over Ethernet (PoE).
•
Bandwidth: LLDP-MED dynamically configures VLANs and DSCP to ensure voice QoS. 
Alternatively, OUI voice-VLAN can be used to identify voice terminals based on OUI-
MAC addresses and add voice VLAN tags to packets.
•
Service forwarding: Layer 2 forwarding is performed for IP phones, and SIP VoIP 
processing is performed for analog phones.

===== PAGE 798 =====
• The VLAN planning principles in the WLAN carrying solution are similar to 
those in the QinQ private line application scenario. The purpose is to 
transparently transmit WLAN management and data packets.
• Packets in the fit AP solution are divided into 2 types: AP management packets 
and service packets.
▫
AP management packets are encapsulated into CAPWAP control packets 
and transmitted over the control tunnel between an access point (AP) and 
an access controller (AC).
▫
Service packets are forwarded through 2 modes: centralized forwarding 
and local forwarding.
▪Centralized forwarding: In this mode, services packets, like 
management packets, are encapsulated into CAPWAP control 
packets and transmitted over the control tunnel between the AP and 
AC.
▪Local forwarding: service packets are not encapsulated in CAPWAP 
control packets but forwarded as Ethernet packets.

===== PAGE 799 =====

===== PAGE 800 =====
•
*The service VLANs of the previous and new APs are different during roaming. To 
ensure nonstop service transmission, the user VLAN must remain unchanged. That is, 
the VLAN ID in data packets is still the original VLAN but not the new VLAN of a new 
AP.

===== PAGE 802 =====
•
The service isolation for small campuses uses VLAN and ACL technologies.
•
For large campus networks, MPLS VPN can be deployed at the core layer, and VLAN 
isolation can be deployed at the access and aggregation layers. If the aggregation layer 
functions as a Layer 2 or Layer 3 splitting point, VRF (MCE) can be deployed.
•
The VLAN planning principles in the WLAN carrying solution are similar to those in the 
QinQ private line application scenario. The purpose is to transparently transmit WLAN 
management and data packets.
•
Packets in the fit AP solution are divided into 2 types: AP management packets and 
service packets.
▫
AP management packets are encapsulated into CAPWAP control packets and 
transmitted over the control tunnel between an access point (AP) and an access 
controller (AC).
▫
Service packets are forwarded through 2 modes: centralized forwarding and local 
forwarding.
▪
Centralized forwarding: In this mode, services packets, like management 
packets, are encapsulated into CAPWAP control packets and transmitted 
over the control tunnel between the AP and AC.
▪
Local forwarding: service packets are not encapsulated in CAPWAP control 
packets but forwarded as Ethernet packets.

===== PAGE 803 =====
•
The VLAN planning principles in the WLAN carrying solution are similar to those in the 
QinQ private line application scenario. The purpose is to transparently transmit WLAN 
management and data packets.
•
Packets in the fit AP solution are divided into 2 types: AP management packets and 
service packets.
▫
AP management packets are encapsulated into CAPWAP control packets and 
transmitted over the control tunnel between an access point (AP) and an access 
controller (AC).
▫
Service packets are forwarded through 2 modes: centralized forwarding and local 
forwarding.
▪
Centralized forwarding: In this mode, services packets, like management 
packets, are encapsulated into CAPWAP control packets and transmitted 
over the control tunnel between the AP and AC.
▪
Local forwarding: service packets are not encapsulated in CAPWAP control 
packets but forwarded as Ethernet packets.
•
Servers in the large campus networks use static IP addresses, so the terminals do not 
need to initiate authentication and authentication servers do not need to deliver 
VLANs. Servers must add interfaces to corresponding VPNs based on applications and 
access users. MPLS VPN provides an end-to-end service transmission channel between 
users and servers, isolating data from different users and applications, ensuring data 
transmission privacy and security.

===== PAGE 804 =====

===== PAGE 805 =====

===== PAGE 806 =====

===== PAGE 807 =====

===== PAGE 808 =====

===== PAGE 809 =====

===== PAGE 810 =====

===== PAGE 811 =====

===== PAGE 812 =====
•
An FBT splitter has been developed for over 20 years, and the manufacturing process is 
very mature. In addition, the raw materials (quartz substrate, optical fiber, heat shrink 
tube, and stainless steel tube) are cost-effective. Therefore, the FBT aplitter is widely 
used in various PONs, especially for small optical splitting (for example, 1:2 or 1:4).
•
An PLC splitter is an integrated waveguide optical power distribution device based on 
quartz substrates. It is made by using semiconductor technology (such as 
photoetching, corrosion, and development). An optical waveguide array is located on 
an upper surface of the chip which integrates a splitting function. Then, multi-channel 
optical fiber arrays of an input end and an output end are separately coupled and 
encapsulated at two ends. The PLC splitter supports 32 or more channels. The loss is 
insensitive to the optical wavelength and can meet the transmission requirements of 
different wavelengths. It can also evenly split optical signals and distribute them to 
users.
•
When a large split ratio (such as 1:16, 1:32, and 1:64) is required, the FBT splitter is 
obviously disadvantageous because the mature biconical process can only pull the split 
ratio below 1:4 at a time, an FBT splitter with a split ratio of 1:4 or higher is achieved 
when multiple FBT splitters with a split ratio of 1:2 are connected via fiber and 
packaged in a splitter box. While PLC splitters are more suitable to applications with 
larger split ratios.
•
FBT and PLC splitters have their own advantages and disadvantages in PON 
applications. Users can select proper optical splitters according to the actual 
application.

===== PAGE 813 =====
•
Mini splitter: It is small-sized and can be used flexibly. It can come with or without 
connectors to adapt to different scenarios.
•
Box-shaped splitter unit: It uses a modular structure to protect optical components 
from damage. It features tool-free installation and easy installation and maintenance. 
Being small-sized and large-capacity, it is widely used in FDTs, FATs, and closures, 
facilitating centralized goods preparation and subsequent capacity expansion and 
maintenance. It comes with a pigtail and the color of the input and output cables can 
be different, which is convenient for use.
•
Rack-mounted splitter unit: It uses a modular design and is compatible with 19-inch 
and ETSI standard cabinets. It also has a guide rail tray to facilitate maintenance.
•
Slotted splitter unit: It has a modular structure. Optical splitters with different split 
ratios are stacked using the same module. It features tool-free installation and easy 
installation and maintenance. It can work with an FAT to support smooth capacity 
expansion. It comes with a connector.
•
Tray splitter unit: It is installed on guide rails. It is usually used in ODFs and FDTs to 
implement quick installation. The distance between guide rails can be adjusted.
•
Wall-mounted splitter unit: The integrated design meets the requirements of indoor 
and outdoor wall-mounted installation scenarios.

===== PAGE 814 =====

===== PAGE 815 =====

===== PAGE 816 =====
•
During ODN design, the optical power attenuation of each optical path must be within 
the range. For example, for Class B+ standard modules, the range is 13–28 dB.

===== PAGE 817 =====

===== PAGE 818 =====

===== PAGE 819 =====
•
Ferrule connector (FC): The external strength member is a metal sleeve, and the 
connector is fastened using screws. Simple structure and convenient operation. It is 
often used in the ODFs.
•
Square connector (SC): The shell is rectangular, and the connector is fastened by 
inserting and removing latches. Easy insertion and removal, high compressive strength.
•
Straight connector (SC): The shell is round and the connector is fastened in bayonet 
mode. The ferrule protrudes long and may damage the end face of the ferrule. It is 
often used in the ODFs.
•
Lucent connector (LC): It is used to connect SFP modules and is made of the easy-to-
use modular jack (RJ) latch mechanism.
•
Physical connector (PC): The endface is polished with a microsphere. The surface of the 
ferrule is polished into a microsphere. The fiber core is located at the highest point of 
bending, which effectively reduces air gaps between fiber components, reduces 
insertion loss, and ensures stable signals. The return losses of the PC, UPC, and APC are 
different. The return loss specified in the industrial standard of the PC is –35 dB.
•
Ultra physical contact (UPC): The endface is not completely flat. It has a slight radian 
to achieve more accurate interconnection. The UPC optimizes the polishing and 
smoothness of the end face on the basis of the PC. The endface looks more like a 
dome. The return loss specified in the industrial standard is –50 dB. The PC and UPC 
connectors are blue.

===== PAGE 820 =====
•
Angled physical contact (APC): The endface is cleaved to be 8 degree to reduce the 
reflectivity of the fiber end and offer better connection performance. The light is 
reflected to the cladding through the inclined surface instead of directly returning to 
the light source. The industrial standard return loss is –60 dB. Generally it is green.
•
APC can only be connected to APC. The structure of the APC is totally different from 
that of PC. If the APC and UPC/PC are connected using a flange, the fiber endface will 
be damaged. PC can be connected to UPC. The following interconnection relationships 
are allowed: PC-PC, PC-UPC, UPC-UPC, and APC-APC.

===== PAGE 821 =====
•
The splicing reliability is high, the loss of the splicing point is less than 0.1 dB, and the 
price of the splicing protector is low. However, the requirements for construction 
personnel are high.
•
The mechanical splicing process is simple. No power supply is required, the operation 
cost is low, and the requirements for construction personnel are low. However, the 
mechanical splicer is not stable, with a loss of 0.2 dB. Matching liquid is used in the 
mechanical splicer, and the volatilization period is five years.
•
Compared with the fixed connection mode, the cable distribution mode uses extra 
pigtails and adapters, improving the flexibility of connections while increasing the 
number of connection points and optical attenuation.
•
Field-mountable optical connector (FMC) represents an improved mechanical splicing 
technology.
▫
FMC is a connector that can be made on site. It does not require a dedicated 
crimping tool. Rather, it can be crimped by using the crimping tool delivered with 
it. After the assembly is complete, the crimping tool can be removed to quickly 
and simply terminate the optical fiber.
▫
FMC adopts the push-pull design, which can fix the bare fiber and the coated 
layer respectively, thereby enhancing the anti-tension and -bending capacities.
▫
FMC uses a unique clip to to clamp the terminal flexible optical cable.
▫
FMC is fully compatible with standard SC adapters and common prefabricated 
connectors.
▫
FMC does not require dedicated tools (splicing machine) or power supply, which 
saves time and costs. The FMC can be reused.

===== PAGE 822 =====

===== PAGE 823 =====

===== PAGE 824 =====
•
ODF: optical distribution frame. An ODF or optical distribution cabinet is mainly used 
in backbone, metro, and access networks. It provides the functions of connecting, 
terminating, distributing, splitting, scheduling, and winding feeder cables.
•
FDT: fiber distribution terminal. An FDT is mainly used on the connection points 
between the feeder cables and distribution cables to secure, protect, and terminate 
optical cables. It also protects splicing points and the devices inside, distributes and 
schedules optical fibers, and houses other value-added modules.
•
FAT: fiber access terminal. An FAT is deployed at the user access node on the ODN to 
connect distribution optical cables to drop optical cables, allow optical fibers to pass 
through, and also distribute, branch, and protect drop optical cables.
•
ATB: access terminal box. An ATB is a passive device used to connect the optical cable 
in the access section to the ONU. It is installed near the ONU and provides an optical 
port for the ONU.
•
Closure: optical cable connecting box.

===== PAGE 825 =====
•
The optical cable entry unit leads in, leads out, and secures optical cables, and grounds 
the strength member of optical cables.
•
The integrated splicing and termination unit splices and distributes optical fibers.
•
The optical splitter unit is configured to encapsulate the optical splitter.
•
The fiber spool unit is used to store the coiled redundant fiber patch cords used for 
inter-port connections.
•
Different configuration modes are recommended for different scenarios.
▫
When the ODF is used at the integrated convergence point with large access and 
splitting capacities, it is recommended that two ODFs be installed side by side. 
ODF 1 have only optical splitter units for the splitting purpose. ODF 2 have only 
the integrated splicing and termination units to lead in and fasten optical cables 
and also to splice and distribute optical fibers. Patch cords led out of the OLT are 
inserted into the input ports of optical splitter units. Then, the output ports of 
optical splitter units connect to the adapters of integrated splicing and 
termination units using patch cords. Finally, the pigtails are spliced with the 
external optical cables.

===== PAGE 826 =====
▫
When the ODF is used at the integrated convergence point with small splitting 
capacity, an ODF that has both optical splitter units and integrated splicing and 
termination units is generally used. This can save the CO space. Such an ODF can 
achieve the same functions as those provided by the above-mentioned two ODFs. 
Patch cords led out of the OLT are inserted into the input ports of optical splitter 
units. Then, the output ports of optical splitter units connect to the adapters of 
integrated splicing and termination units using patch cords. Finally, the pigtails 
are spliced with the external optical cables.
▫
When the ODF is used at the integrated convergence point with small access 
capacity, or when the ODF is used at the information aggregation point or 
information distribution point, an integrated splicing and termination unit can be 
used to lead in and fix optical cables and splice and distribute optical fibers.
•
U is a unit used to measure the external dimensions (height or thickness) of a server. It 
is an abbreviation for unit. 1 U = 4.445 cm.

===== PAGE 827 =====
•
Splitting and splicing closure (SSC): optical cable connecting box.

===== PAGE 828 =====

===== PAGE 829 =====
•
IP rating consists of the letter IP followed by two digits. The first digit indicates the 
level of protection provided against the intrusion of solid objects and dust in electrical 
enclosures, and the second digit indicates that against moisture and water in electrical 
enclosures. The larger the digit, the higher the protection level.
•
In IP65:
▫
Waterproof test: Spray water to all possible directions and surfaces of the 
sample. After the test, the water ingress cannot affect the normal operation and 
damage safety of the equipment. The water does not accumulate and can be 
drained through the drainage hole without damaging the equipment.
▫
Dust-proof test: Perform negative pressure on the cabinet and place the cabinet 
in a floating device. After the test, no dust enters the cabinet.

===== PAGE 830 =====

===== PAGE 831 =====

===== PAGE 832 =====

===== PAGE 833 =====

===== PAGE 834 =====
•
Principles of stepped fiber transmission:
▫
A beam of light forms a θ1 angle with the fiber axis and is incident to the center 
of the core area. The light is refracted on the interface between the optical fiber 
and the air, and is bent towards the normal direction. The refraction angle θ2 is 
determined by the Snell's law: n0sinθ1=n1sinθ2 (n0 and n1 are the refractive 
indexes of the air and fiber core, respectively). When refracted light arrives at the 
interface between the core and cladding, if the incident angle θ3 meets the 
formula sinθ3<n2/n1 (n2 is the cladding refractive index), refraction occurs again. 
If the incident angle is greater than the critical angle θc, total reflection occurs on 
the fiber core and cladding. Sinθc = n2/n1. The total reflection occurs on the 
entire fiber. All the light that meets the θ3>θc condition is limited to the fiber 
core. This is the basic mechanism for fiber constraint and light guiding.
•
In a single-mode or multi-mode optical fiber, "mode" indicates the transmission path 
of light entering the optical fiber at a certain angular speed. The number of 
transmission modes supported by an optical fiber varies depending on the fiber 
diameter. When the diameter of the fiber core is much larger than that of the optical 
wavelength, there may be dozens of or more transmission modes. Such a fiber is 
referred to as an MMF. Different transmission modes have different speeds and 
phases. After long-haul transmission, problems such as delay, spectrum broadening, 
and large dispersion occur. Therefore, MMFs are applicable only to low-rate and short-
distance transmission. When the core diameter is small or similar to that of the optical 
wavelength, there is only one mode (basic mode). Such a fiber is called an SMF, 
especially suitable for large-capacity and long-haul transmission.

===== PAGE 835 =====
•
Currently, POL deployment uses SMFs.
•
SMFs and MMFs cannot be used together or interconnected. The two types of fibers 
have different fiber core sizes and transmit different optical modules. If they are used 
together or interconnected, a large amount of optical loss will be caused, resulting in 
link jitter or faults. Generally, an SMF uses a yellow outer jacket, and an MMF uses an 
orange or light green outer jacket. Do not mix different types of cables.
•
Currently, POL deployment uses SMFs.
•
SMFs and MMFs cannot be used together or interconnected. The two types of fibers 
have different fiber core sizes and transmit different optical modules. If they are used 
together or interconnected, a large amount of optical loss will be caused, resulting in 
link jitter or faults. Generally, an SMF uses a yellow outer jacket, and an MMF uses an 
orange or light green outer jacket. Do not mix different types of cables.

===== PAGE 836 =====
•
Dispersion indicates the optical pulse broadening caused by different group velocities 
of different wavelengths in a fiber.
•
CD coefficientis the dispersion value per unit length of fiber and per unit of spectral 
width, with the unit of ps/(nm*km).
•
CD limits the transmission capacity and distance. The smaller dispersion, the longer 
transmission distance without relay.
•
Zero dispersion wavelength is the wavelength with zero dispersion. Currently, the zero-
dispersion wavelength of Huawei G.652D fibers ranges from 1300 nm to 1324 nm. An 
SMF selects the window of zero dispersion wavelength to achieve larger capacity and 
higher rate.

===== PAGE 837 =====
•
Optical fibers with a small bending radius are applicable to intra-MDF routing, inter-
MDF routing, and indoor routing. For example, patch cords and pigtails.

===== PAGE 854 =====
•
The mini or small optical splitter with a connector can be used. The optical splitter, 
OLT, and ONU are installed in the same cabinet.

===== PAGE 855 =====
•
The OLT, rack-mounted optical splitter, and integrated splicing and termination ODF 
are installed in the same cabinet. Patch cords are deployed on the OLT to connect to 
the input end of the optical splitter, and patch cords are deployed on the output end of 
the optical splitter to connect to the port of the ODF adapter. In the integrated splicing 
and termination ODF, the indoor terminal flexible optical cable is stripped and spliced 
with the patch cord. The spliced connector is connected to the ODF adapter. The 
terminal flexible optical cable is deployed in the ONU information box, and the pigtail 
is spliced in the information box. The spliced connector can be directly connected to 
the ONU or connected through an adapter. The adapter in the ONU information box is 
used to flexibly adjust the optical fibers connected to the ONU when extra optical 
fibers are available. In this case, the deployed terminal flexible optical cable is called 
drop cable (access cable) in the ODN structure.

===== PAGE 856 =====
•
The number of ONUs on a small-or medium-sized campus network is limited. In this 
case, you can deploy frame-shaped ODFs in the CO equipment room. Vertical optical 
cables are deployed in weak-current wells of buildings. If there are multiple buildings 
in the campus and outdoor cabling is required, outdoor optical cables can be used 
between the CO equipment room and each building. In the floor weak-current room 
with FATs deployed, fibers at the distribution section are split in the FAT, the rest of 
the distribution fibers straightly pass through. After fiber splitting, flexible terminal 
optical cables are connected to each ONU through the optical splitter. The FAT can be 
an integrated device with a built-in optical splitter or an independent optical splitter. 
The information box can also be installed in the weak-current well and contains the 
fiber splicing and termination unit, optical splitter, and fiber distribution unit.

===== PAGE 857 =====
•
In large campus and FTTH scenarios, there are a large number of ONUs. In this case, 
cabinet-type ODFs need to be deployed in the CO equipment room.
•
Generally, the optical distribution point/information aggregation point is located 
outdoors. Different types of optical cables can be used as feeder cables in different 
installation modes, such as burial, duct, and aerial.
•
If the optical distribution point/information aggregation point is located outdoors, the 
outdoor FDT can be used. If the point is located inside a building, the cabinet-type ODF 
can be used.
•
In FTTH scenarios, two-level optical splitting is usually used. Level-1 optical splitters are 
deployed at the optical distribution point, and level-2 optical splitters are deployed at 
the user access point.
•
In large-scale campuses, you can select the level-1 or level-2 optical splitting as 
required.

===== PAGE 858 =====
•
The ODN is an important part of the POL. The POL service planning and OLT&ONU 
planning are the input of the ODN planning.
•
During ODN planning, you need to specify the leaf nodes of the ODN tree, that is, the 
type, quantity, and distribution of information points to be accessed on the POL 
network. Then, converge the areas to be covered into several typical scenarios based 
on the type and quantity of information points to be accessed. In a typical scenario, the 
ONU model and deployment location can be determined based on the concurrent 
bandwidth and capacity expansion requirements.
•
Then you need to specify the root nodes, that is, the location of the integrated 
convergence point (OLT installation location) of the POL.
•
After the root and leaf nodes are determined, other nodes in between can be 
determined.
•
Collect network coverage requirements:
▫
POL access services include PC access, AP backhaul, video backhaul, IP voice, and 
IPTV services.
▫
Typical scenarios refer to a certain number of scenarios with similar or the same 
physical structures and services, for example, classrooms, offices, and lecture 
halls in a school.

===== PAGE 859 =====

===== PAGE 860 =====

===== PAGE 861 =====
•
The following describes how to calculate the concurrent bandwidth in the general 
education classroom scenario.
▫
The video surveillance system mainly occupies the upstream bandwidth. 
According to the video surveillance standards of the government's Sharp Eyes 
project, each camera occupies 8 Mbit/s upstream bandwidth.
▫
The recording system occupies upstream and downstream bandwidths. According 
to the industry standards provided by the vendor, the upstream and downstream 
bandwidths are 10 Mbit/s respectively.
▫
The electronic class plate (including facial recognition) mainly occupies upstream 
bandwidth. The upstream bandwidth is 13 Mbit/s (12 Mbit/s + 1 Mbit/s) based 
on the facial recognition standards of the government's Sharp Eyes project. 
▫
The multimedia control system, IP broadcast, and access control have lower 
requirements on bandwidth. The upstream/downstream bandwidth is 1 Mbit/s.
▫
The integrated equipment mainly occupies the downstream bandwidth. The 
downstream bandwidth is 10 Mbit/s based on the industry experience.
▫
The teaching tablet mainly occupies downstream bandwidth. According to the 
industry experience (usually 2 Mbit/s per tablet), 3 Mbit/s downstream 
bandwidth can meet teaching requirements.

===== PAGE 862 =====
•
The following is an example of calculating the concurrent bandwidth in the public area 
(on the same floor of the teaching building).
▫
The video surveillance system mainly occupies the upstream bandwidth. 
According to the video surveillance standards of the government's Sharp Eyes 
project, each camera occupies 8 Mbit/s upstream bandwidth.
▫
Assume that 40 concurrent users access an AP and the upstream/downstream 
bandwidth of each user is 1/3 Mbit/s. Therefore, the upstream and downstream 
bandwidths of each AP are 40 Mbit/s and 120 Mbit/s respectively.
▫
The upstream/downstream bandwidth is 1 Mbit/s for IP broadcast.

===== PAGE 863 =====

===== PAGE 864 =====

===== PAGE 865 =====
•
The type and quantity of splitters vary according to the protection type.
•
The 1:N splitter is used for Type A. Single fibers are used between the splitter and OLT 
and between the splitter and ONU, without any protection. When the upstream fiber 
of the splitter is faulty, manually switch to the standby fiber. In this case, redundant 
fibers are required between the splitter and OLT, so that manual switching can be 
performed when the feeder optical line is faulty.
•
The 2:N splitter is used for Type B. There are two upstream fibers on the splitter. In the 
single-homing scenario, the two upstream fibers are connected to two PON ports on 
the same OLT. In the dual-homing scenario, the two upstream fibers are connected to 
two PON ports on two OLTs. When the upstream active fiber or the OLT port 
connected to the active fiber is faulty, the service is switched to the standby line.
•
The 1:N splitter is used for Type C. In this case, each ONU is connected to two splitters 
in the upstream direction. Each splitter occupies one fiber to the OLT, and each ONU 
occupies two fibers to the splitter. The system automatically switches to the standby 
line no matter whether the active or branch path is faulty.
•
In Type A and Type B scenarios, if customers have high requirements on security 
backup and have sufficient budgets, redundant fibers can be deployed between the 
splitter and ONU so that fibers can be manually switched when the branch path is 
faulty.

===== PAGE 866 =====
•
As shown in the preceding figure, Type B dual-homing protection is used. The feeder 
cable is routed in a ring. The cables are stripped at buildings B, C, and D. After the fiber 
core is cut, the cable is connected to the two input terminals of the 2:N splitter in the 
east and west directions at each access point or to the two fiber cores of the 
distribution optical cable in the building.

===== PAGE 868 =====

===== PAGE 870 =====
•
ONU selection suggestions: Considering future maintenance and spare parts storage, 
and the small number of conference room scenarios, the fewer the ONU models, the 
better. It is recommended to select two GPON ONUs with the PoE function: 8-port GE 
and 24-port GE. In the public area, each unit is configured with one ONU with 24 GE 
ports. In other typical scenarios, each unit is configured with one ONU with 8 GE ports.

===== PAGE 871 =====
•
The installation locations and cable connections of ONUs and information nodes in 
typical scenarios must be clearly planned. A clear and complete generic cabling plan is 
the basis for installation, deployment, and maintenance. The ONU installation position 
is important for ODN planning.

===== PAGE 872 =====

===== PAGE 873 =====
•
Generally, in office campuses, education, and healthcare scenarios, the optical path is 
short and the optical fiber transmission loss is small. The optical attenuation caused by 
splitting does not exceed the maximum optical path attenuation. For small and micro 
POL campuses, you need to consider the case where optical attenuation is too small to 
reach the minimum optical path attenuation. For example, the attenuation of Class B+ 
ranges from 13 dB to 28 dB. For small and micro POL campuses, ensure that the 
optical path attenuation is not less than 13 dB. In the safe city scenario, the optical 
path is long. The optical attenuation caused by splitting may cause the link attenuation 
to exceed the maximum value. For details about how to calculate the optical path 
attenuation, see the preceding slides.
•
When calculating the maximum split ratio in a typical scenario, divide the maximum 
bandwidth by the upstream and downstream concurrent bandwidth, and round down 
the result. For example, in a common classroom, the maximum upstream split ratio is 
11.9 (1250/105), and the maximum downstream split ratio is 11.6 (2500/215), which is 
rounded down to 11/11.
•
The split ratios such as 1:11 and 1:13 are non-standard and need to adapt to the 
typical value. According to the principle of small adaptation, the maximum upstream 
and downstream split ratios 1:11/1:11 are adapted to 1:8/1:8. The 1:8 ratio is used. In 
the teacher's office scenario, the maximum upstream and downstream split ratios 
1:41/1:27 are adapted to 1:32/1:16. The 1:16 ratio is used.

===== PAGE 874 =====
•
The typical split ratios are 1:4, 1:8, 1:16, and 1:32. Classrooms are the majority in 
typical scenarios. Therefore, 1:8 splitters can be connected to classrooms, 
administrative offices, teacher's offices, and conference rooms. The concurrent 
bandwidth in lecture halls and public areas is high, a small split ratio is more suitable. 
Therefore, the ratio can be 1:4 or 1:8. According to the split ratio convergence, the 
maximum concurrent bandwidth in classrooms, offices, and conference rooms is higher 
than that planned, and there is a certain margin for future bandwidth expansion. 
However, lecture halls and public areas just meet the planning requirements. In future 
capacity expansion, measures such as reducing the split ratio can be taken.
•
The optical attenuation of the 1:4 splitter is small. In a small campus, the receive 
optical power of the ONU may not reach the threshold. For example, in the Class B+ 
standard, the minimum/maximum optical power loss of the link is 13/28 dB, and the 
optical attenuation of the 1:4 splitter is 7.4 dB. As a result, the line optical attenuation 
may not reach the minimum value of 13 dB. In this case, if the 1:4 splitter is used, you 
need to add an attenuator.

===== PAGE 875 =====
•
Note: It is recommended that the optical splitting modes be obtained from the Passive 
Optical LAN Engineering Technology Standard (T/CECA20002-2018).
•
Mode 1: When there are a large number of terminals on each floor, use level-1 
splitting. Splitters are deployed in the weak-current room on each floor of the building 
and connected to the ONU on the same floor.
•
Mode 2: When the number of terminals on each floor is small, use level-2 splitting. 
Level-1 splitters and OLTs are deployed in the equipment room of the building, and 
level-2 splitters are deployed in the weak-current room of the floor.
•
Mode 3: When the number of terminals in a building is small, use level-1 splitting. The 
splitters and OLTs are installed in the equipment room of the building.

===== PAGE 876 =====
•
Based on the split ratio corresponding to the typical scenario, map the specific physical 
locations to PON ports. The table lists the split ratio between PON ports and ONUs, 
which can be used to calculate the number of PON ports required. If Type B or Type C 
single-/dual-homing configuration is used, the number of PON ports needs to be 
doubled.

===== PAGE 880 =====
•
Scenario 1: After cables are routed into a building, it is recommended that the cables 
be terminated in the FDT on the 1st floor. 1:32 splitters are used in each floor to 
provide 70 Mbit/s parallel downstream bandwidth. Generally, two splitters are 
deployed in each floor to meet coverage requirements. If the floor is long, it is 
recommended that the splitters be deployed separately to reduce the cable length 
from the splitter to the ONU. On the 1st floor, one splitter can be installed in the FDT, 
and the other splitter can be installed in an independent information box. Two-core 
terminal flexible cables are directly deployed for connection. One 12-core vertical cable 
is routed from the FDT on the 1st floor to other floors. After termination on each floor, 
the 12-core vertical cable is connected to each splitter. Two-core terminal flexible 
cables are used between the splitter and the ONU/ONT. One is active and the other is 
standby. If a single ONU node requires a larger concurrent bandwidth, a splitter with 
smaller split ratio can be used. In addition, more fiber cores can be selected for indoor 
vertical cables and outdoor drop cables.
•
The recommended ODN configurations in scenario 1 are as follows.

Scenario 2: In a scenario with a large floor area, 48-core vertical cables are deployed 
on the 2nd or higher floors. 12-core fibers are pulled and terminated every three floors 
and then connected to the upper, middle, and lower splitters. For example, the fibers 
are pulled out on the 3rd floor. After the termination, two-core terminal flexible cables 
are connected to the splitters on floors 2, 3, and 4. For a scenario with a small floor 
area: 6-core cables are pulled every three floors and connected to the upper, middle, 
and lower splitters.

===== PAGE 884 =====
•
In a ring topology, cables can be routed into a building near the feeder route. It is 
recommended that an FDT be installed on the 1st floor as the information aggregation 
point to branch the optical fibers. Other cables are directly connected. For details, see 
the ODN structure of a small- and medium-sized campus. The branched cables are 
routed as distribution cables in the weak-current well, and FATs are deployed in the 
weak-current well on each floor as the information distribution points. In the FATs, 
fibers are distributed, splitters are connected, and terminal flexible cables are 
connected to ONUs. For a building far away from the feeder route, an outdoor FDT 
can be deployed at the roadside as the optical distribution point. After fibers are 
branched, the distribution cables are connected to the building.
•
If distribution cables are deployed in ring mode and cover multiple buildings, install 
FATs on the 1st floor of each building as information distribution points, and then 
directly route the cables from the FATs to ONUs. Alternatively, install FATs in weak-
current wells on each floor for secondary distribution. If the distribution cables cover 
only one building, the cables can be routed in the weak-current well and the corridor 
to form a ring optical path inside the building.

Multi-fiber push on (MPO) is a multi-core multi-channel pluggable connector.

===== PAGE 897 =====

===== PAGE 898 =====
•
Traditional solution:
▫
Centralized splitting; long-length and large numbers of drop cables.
▫
Splitters are installed in the cable distribution box, occupying large space.
▫
Heavy fiber splicing workload and low efficiency.
▫
The maintenance is difficult skill-required.
•
E2E pre-connection solution:
▫
Distributed splitting reduces the number of drop cables and reduces pipeline 
resources.
▫
Splitters are installed in the pre-connected box, occupying small space.
▫
Easy deployment, 100% splicing-free, no need for professional skills and tools, 
and improved efficiency.
▫
Easy to maintain, no need for fiber splicing, and low maintenance skill 
requirements.

===== PAGE 899 =====

===== PAGE 900 =====

===== PAGE 901 =====
•
Product overview

===== PAGE 902 =====

===== PAGE 903 =====
•
The ODN is an important part of the POL. Therefore, the ODN cannot be planned 
independently. The POL service planning and OLT&ONU planning are the input of the 
ODN planning, and they are dependent on each other.
•
During ODN planning, you need to specify the leaf nodes of the ODN tree, that is, the 
type, quantity, and distribution of information points to be accessed on the POL 
network. Then, converge the areas to be covered into several typical scenarios based 
on the type and quantity of information points to be accessed. In a typical scenario, the 
ONU model and deployment location can be determined based on the concurrent 
bandwidth and capacity expansion requirements.
•
Then you need to specify the root nodes, that is, the location of the integrated 
convergence point (OLT installation location) of the POL.
•
After the root and leaf nodes are determined, other nodes in between can be 
determined.
•
Collect network coverage requirements:
▫
POL access services include PC access, AP backhaul, video backhaul, IP voice, and 
IPTV services.
▫
Typical scenarios refer to a certain number of scenarios with similar or the same 
physical structures and services, for example, classrooms, offices, and lecture 
halls in a school.

===== PAGE 904 =====
•
Note: It is recommended that the optical splitting modes be obtained from the Passive 
Optical LAN Engineering Technology Standard (T/CECA20002-2018).
•
Mode 1: When there are a large number of terminals on each floor, use level-1 
splitting. Splitters are deployed in the weak-current room on each floor of the building 
and connected to the ONU on the same floor.
•
Mode 2: When the number of terminals on each floor is small, use level-2 splitting. 
Level-1 splitters and OLTs are deployed in the equipment room of the building, and 
level-2 splitters are deployed in the weak-current room of the floor.
•
Mode 3: When the number of terminals in a building is small, use level-1 splitting. The 
splitters and OLTs are installed in the equipment room of the building.
