===== PAGE 1007 =====
•
To improve troubleshooting efficiency, make the following preparations before a 
device fault occurs:
▫
Formulate a diagram on the physical connections between onsite devices.
▫
Formulate the communication, interconnection, and permission information 
tables for devices and parts, including the VLAN, IP address, interconnection port 
ID, and firewall configuration information.
▫
Make archives for onsite devices and parts, and record software and hardware 
configurations, software and hardware versions, and any change information.
▫
Periodically maintain spare parts and verify that their hardware configurations, 
software versions, and parameter configurations are the same as those of parts 
used on the live network for quick replacement in case of emergencies.

===== PAGE 1008 =====

===== PAGE 1009 =====

===== PAGE 1010 =====
•
A maintenance engineer simulates a user terminal on an access node to initiate a 
PPPoE dialup.
▫
Set parameters such as the user name, password, service flow ID, authentication 
mode, and timeout interval.
•
The access node interacts with the BRAS through PPPoE.
▫
PPPoE is divided into the discovery and session stages.
▪
PPPoE discovery stage: The source host discovers the MAC address of the 
destination host (BRAS), and sets up a point-to-point PPP session.
▪
PPPoE session phase: A point-to-point connection has been set up and 
carries PPP packets for link negotiation, as shown in the figure.
•
PPPoE dialup simulation is stopped on the access node.

===== PAGE 1011 =====
•
Here PPPoE dialup simulation on an MDU is used as an example to describe how to 
perform PPPoE dialup simulation.
•
The current PPPoE dialup simulation must be stopped before the next PPPoE dialup 
simulation is started.

===== PAGE 1012 =====
•
In call simulation, the system simulates actions on a user port, including offhook, 
onhook, dialup, and ringing detection. In this way, the calling and called parties can be 
simulated. The figure shows the call simulation principle. The phone used for the test is 
placed in the access node equipment room. The users configured on the phone and 
access node can be used for the call simulation test. This method can remotely check 
whether the voice service is normal.

===== PAGE 1013 =====
•
Prerequisite: The voice service has been configured, and a phone is available.
•
Run the pots emulational config command to configure call simulation test 
parameters. 
▫
To implement an automatic call simulation test, configure an alert tone type and 
DTMF numbers before starting a call simulation test.
▫
By default, the alert tone is of the voice prompt type.

===== PAGE 1014 =====
•
A maintenance engineer remotely logs in to the access device and simulates a user to 
start multicast simulation.
▫
The engineer sets parameters such as the user information, program IP address, 
and multicast VLAN ID.
•
The access device constructs and sends a Join packet to the multicast router to request 
for joining a multicast group.
•
The multicast router determines whether a multicast program stream exists.
▫
If yes, the multicast router directly sends the multicast program stream to the 
access device.
▫
If no, after the multicast router interacts with the multicast source, the multicast 
source sends the multicast program stream to the access device through the 
multicast router.
•
The maintenance terminal queries the status of the multicast simulation user and the 
real-time traffic of the multicast program.
▫
It queries the status of the multicast simulation user. It determines whether the 
user has successfully ordered the multicast program based on the online status 
of this user.
▫
It queries the real-time traffic of the multicast program. It determines whether 
the communication between the access device and the multicast source is normal 
based on the queried real-time traffic of the multicast program. 
•
After multicast simulation is complete, the maintenance engineer manually stops 
multicast simulation through the CLI to release resources in a timely manner.

===== PAGE 1015 =====
•
Prerequisites: The data configuration of the multicast port is normal, and the multicast 
user for the multicast simulation test has permission to watch the configured multicast 
program.

===== PAGE 1021 =====
•
By default, the ranging compensation distance of a GPON port is 0–20 km.
•
According to protocols, the maximum ranging compensation distance of a GPON port 
cannot exceed 60 km, and the difference between the minimum and maximum 
ranging compensation distances cannot exceed 20 km.
•
By default, the laser on a GPON port is enabled.

===== PAGE 1022 =====
•
Method of cleaning a fiber connector endface: Use a dry lint-free wipe (two-layer) to 
clean the fiber connector endface along one direction once. If the connector is severely 
dirty, use a lint-free wipe (two-layer) dipped with little cleanser to clean the connector 
endface along one direction once. Then use another dry lint-free wipe to clean it again 
along one direction once to ensure than the connector endface is dry Before the 
operation, disconnect both ends of the fiber. During the operation, dustproof caps and 
both ends of the fiber must be protected against dust. A lint-free wipe can be used 
only once. Use the portion of the lint-free wipe that is not touched by hands. For 
details, see the related operation guide.

▫
If one or more ONUs alternatively go online and offline, the distribution fiber and

===== PAGE 1040 =====

===== PAGE 1041 =====

===== PAGE 1042 =====

===== PAGE 1043 =====
•
If the ONU SN in the query result is different from the actual ONU SN, the ONU will 
fail to register with the OLT.
•
If the Control flag is deactive, the ONU is not allowed to register with the OLT. In this 
case, run the ont activate command in GPON mode to activate the control flag.
•
If Control Flag is active:
▫
If Run state is online, the user goes online and passes the authentication.
▫
If Run state is offline, the user is offline.
•
If Config state is not normal, the ONU configuration restoration fails. The possible 
cause is that an incorrect line profile has been bound to the ONU. You can issue the 
configuration command again or restart the ONU to rectify the fault.
•
If Match state is mismatch, the service profile configuration is inappropriate.

===== PAGE 1093 =====

===== PAGE 1094 =====
•
The OLT implements MAC anti-spoofing based on the following schemes:
▫
After MAC anti-spoofing is enabled, the board stops dynamic MAC address 
learning, captures DHCPv4 and PPPoE packets from both the user and network 
sides, dynamically obtains the source MAC address of the user, and sets the 
binding relationship between the source MAC address and the user port or 
service flow.
▫
After MAC anti-spoofing is disabled, the board resumes dynamic MAC address 
learning and no longer captures PPPoE or DHCPv4 packets from the user or 
network side. The board also deletes its MAC address binding entries.

===== PAGE 1100 =====
•
Check whether WAN connections for the voice service on the ONT are normal and 
whether they have obtained IP addresses.
•
Check whether the voice user configured on the ONT is registered with the MGC/IMS. 
If the voice user is not registered with the MGC/IMS, the link from the ONT to the 
MGC/IMS is faulty or voice parameters are incorrectly configured for the MGC/IMS.
•
Rectify the fault on the link from the ONT to the MGC/IMS. Check the quality of the 
link from the ONT to the MGC/IMS by performing a ping operation. If packets are lost 
or delay is very large, capture packets in an exclusive way to locate the device that 
drops the packets or incorrectly forwards packets. Then rectify the fault.
•
Voice parameters of the ONT or MGC/IMS are incorrectly configured.

===== PAGE 1101 =====
•
Check whether users on the same OLT can call each other normally. 
•

and then run the arp proxy command to enable the ARP proxy globally and ARP proxy 
of the S-VLAN interface of the voice service. Check whether the users can call each 
other normally. 
•
Check whether ACL rules are correctly configured on the router of the IP bearer 
network. 
•
Check whether users on the same ONT can call each other normally. 
•
On the OLT, run the display acl all command to check whether ACL rules are used to 
filter upstream or downstream voice media streams. 
•
On the ONT, check whether IP filtering and MAC address filtering functions are 
configured to filter upstream or downstream voice media streams.

===== PAGE 1102 =====
•
Check the fault scope. 
•
Rectify the packet loss issue on the bearer network and eliminate EMI (such as 
interference brought by a broadcast tower or high voltage cable) in the environment 
of the OLT.
•
Eliminate EMI (such as interference brought by a radio or stereo) in the environment 
of the ONT and the lines between the ONT and the phones.
•
Replace the ONT or power adapter which may be faulty.
•
The subscriber cable may be connected incorrectly.
•
Check the phone number settings on the softswitch and ONT, compare the settings of 
the phone number that encounters the fault with the settings of a correct number, and 
modify the settings of the faulty number according to the settings of the correct 
number.

===== PAGE 1103 =====
•
Run the ont pots-test command to perform a voice loop line test on the ONT, and 
rectify the fault based on the test result.
•
Rectify network faults such as loose fiber connection.
•
Connect the phone that encounters the fault to another POTS port of the ONT.
•
Check whether the 802.1p priority of the voice service is configured too low on the 
ONT. 
•
Replace the ONT, Make a call again to check whether the fault is rectified.

===== PAGE 1104 =====

===== PAGE 1105 =====
•
Power feed is a process in which an AG device provides a terminal device (such as a 
phone) with voltage and current so that the terminal device works properly and the 
AG device can detect terminal device status such as offhook or onhook.
•
POTS boards provide power feed for phones and detect phone status through the 
subscriber line interface circuit (SLIC). After a phone is connected to a POTS port, the 
SLIC provides constant current for the phone and determines the phone status based 
on the feed current.
▫
When the phone is in onhook state, because the resistance between the SLIC and 
the phone is large, it can be considered that the feed current is 0. In this case, the 
SLIC determines that the phone is in onhook state.
▫
After the phone is picked up, the resistance between the SLIC and the phone 
changes to a specific value. In this case, the SLIC provides a specific current for 
the phone and determines that the phone is in offhook state.
•
#pots loop-line-test command for a loop line test:
▫
This command is used to simulate a subscriber loop line test. The qualitative loop 
line test conclusion includes the following: normal, A/B wire touching the power 
line, shorted A wire with wire of an adjacent twisted pair, shorted B wire with 
wire of an adjacent twisted pair, shorted A and B wires between twisted pairs, 
grounded A wire, grounded B wire, grounded A and B wires, shorted wires within 
the same twisted pair (low resistance or self-touched), poor insulation between A 
and B wires, A wire leakage to the ground, B wire leakage to the ground, leakage 
to the ground of A and B wires, leakage of A and B wires, missed calls, phone set 
not connected, off hook, and unknown.
▫
The loop line test is used to check voice faults caused on the subscriber line, such 
as noise and no dial tone.

===== PAGE 1106 =====
•
The check position is the same as that in the scenario of "MxU Connected to a Phone: 
No Power Feed After Offhook".
•
#pots loop-line-test:
▫
This command is used to simulate a subscriber loop line test. The qualitative loop 
line test conclusion includes the following: normal, A/B wire touching the power 
line, shorted A wire with wire of an adjacent twisted pair, shorted B wire with 
wire of an adjacent twisted pair, shorted A and B wires between twisted pairs, 
grounded A wire, grounded B wire, grounded A and B wires, shorted wires within 
the same twisted pair (low resistance or self-touched), poor insulation between A 
and B wires, A wire leakage to the ground, B wire leakage to the ground, leakage 
to the ground of A and B wires, leakage of A and B wires, missed calls, phone set 
not connected, off hook, and unknown.
▫
The loop line test is used to check voice faults caused on the subscriber line, such 
as noise and no dial tone.

===== PAGE 1107 =====

===== PAGE 1108 =====
•
Normally, when a user picks up the phone, the user hears the beep tone and is 
prompted to dial a number. The user hears the busy tone (played by the DSP daughter 
board) in either of the following situations:
▫
The called party is occupied: After dialing a number, the calling party hears the 
busy tone because the called party is in a call or occupied.
▫
One party onhook: When a call ends and one party hangs up, the other party 
hears the busy tone indicating that the peer party hangs up.
▫
No answer: After the calling party dials up, the called party does not answer the 
call. When the phone rings for a period, the calling party hears the busy tone, 
indicating that the other party does not answer the call.
▫
No dialup after offhook for a long time: After picking up a phone, the calling 
party hears the busy tone if the calling party does not dial any number within a 
long period of time.
•
Possible causes of busy tone after offhook are as follows:
▫
The user data configuration is incorrect.
▪
No user data is configured on the POTS port.
▪
User data is not configured on the softswitch/IMS.
▪
The user data configured on the POTS port is different from that 
configured on the softswitch/IMS.
▫
The DSP resources of the voice system are insufficient.
▫
The link between the phone and the softswitch/IMS is faulty.

===== PAGE 1109 =====

===== PAGE 1110 =====
•
In the case of one-way audio, the dialup and ringing on the device are normal. 
Therefore, it can be determined that the signaling flow is normal. One-way audio may 
be caused by incorrect route configuration on the ONU or on the bearer network.

===== PAGE 1111 =====
•
Noise during a voice call mainly refers to the strong current and broadcast voice, 
rather than the noise in the surroundings of the calling party and the called party. 
Voice calls are mainly carried by media streams. If noise occurs during a call, the link 
that the media streams pass through is affected by interference and noise is 
introduced. Possible causes of noise are as follows:
▫
External electromagnetic interference
▫
Power supply or grounding noise
▫
Device line failure

===== PAGE 1112 =====
•
The voice call content is carried by media streams. The root cause of the intermittent 
interruption is that packet loss occurs in media packets. The link from the phone to the 
ONU transmits analog signals, while the link from the ONU to the peer AG device 
transmits VoIP packets. When packet loss occurs on the network, some call content is 
missing. As a result, calls are interrupted intermittently. The local media stream link is 
from the phone to the bearer network. In this segment, packet loss may occur, 
resulting in intermittent call interruptions.

===== PAGE 1128 =====
•
When a multicast user is in offline state on an FTTH network, you need to find out the 
cause why the multicast user goes offline. Enable multicast debugging for the OLT, 
and check whether the OLT receives a report packet from the multicast user for 
ordering a specified program.
•
When a multicast user is in offline state on an FTTB or FTTC network, you need to find 
out the cause why the multicast user goes offline. Enable multicast debugging for the 
MDU, and check whether the MDU receives a report packet from the multicast user for 
ordering a specified program.
•
In config mode, enable multicast debugging as follows:
•
terminal monitor   //*Enable a terminal to display debugging information.
•
terminal debugging       //*Enable the terminal to output debugging information.
•
debugging igmp service-port index  //* Enable the multicast user diagnosis function. 
index indicates the index of a multicast user.

===== PAGE 1129 =====
•
To check whether the multicast user is in block state, run the display igmp user 
service-port index command to query the status of the multicast user.
•
To check whether the user encounters a bandwidth check failure:
▫
Run the display igmp user user-index command to query the maximum 
bandwidth (User MaxBandWidth) of the user.
▫
Run the display igmp program name command to query the bandwidth of the 
ordered program.
▫
Check whether the maximum bandwidth of the user is less than the bandwidth 
of the program ordered by the user.
•
To check whether the number of specific programs that can be ordered by the user 
reaches the maximum, run the display igmp user extended-attributes user-index
command to query the maximum number of programs at each level that the user can 
watch.
•
To check whether the number of programs that can be ordered by the user reaches 
the maximum, run the display igmp user service-port index command to query the 
number of watched programs and the maximum number of programs that can be 
watched by the user.
•
To check whether the user has permission to watch the program, run the display igmp
service-port 100 command to query the permission profile bound to the multicast user 
(service port 100 in this example). If the number of bound profiles is 0, the user has 
not bound to a permission profile and therefore the user cannot order any programs.

===== PAGE 1130 =====

===== PAGE 1131 =====

===== PAGE 1132 =====

===== PAGE 1134 =====

===== PAGE 1135 =====

===== PAGE 1136 =====
•
Based on the encapsulation characteristics of program data packets, there are two 
types of IPTV applications:
▫
Program packets are multicast packets. In this case, program data is replicated 
on an OLT or MxU. Packet forwarding relies on a multicast forwarding table.
▫
Program packets are unicast packets. In this case, program data is replicated on 
the BRAS. Packet forwarding is the same as that for common Internet access 
services.
•
Regardless of the IPTV application type, when the IPTV STB restores program data 
packets (UDP packets) to video information, the STB compares received packets with 
packets sent by the multicast server. If packet loss occurs, video restoration may be 
distorted, resulting in artifacts or frame freezing.

▫
the user has no right

the number of program that the user is allowed to watch has reached maximum
▫
the number of the grade program that the user is allowed to watch has reached 
maximum
▫
the BTV user fails to join the program
▫
the user fails to pass bandwidth CAC
▫
Multicast group IP address conflict

===== PAGE 1139 =====

===== PAGE 1140 =====

===== PAGE 1141 =====

===== PAGE 1142 =====

===== PAGE 1143 =====

===== PAGE 1144 =====
•
If the prejoin function is enabled, an MxU sends report packets to the upstream when 
a program is added. Regardless of whether users are watching programs, multicast 
streams are diverted to the ONU upstream port in advance. In this way, the OLT 
allocates an assured bandwidth of 5 Mbit/s to each program by default.

===== PAGE 1145 =====

===== PAGE 1146 =====
•
The ONU forwards multicast data according to the multicast MAC address. That is, 
programs 1.2.3.4 and 11.11.11.1 share the same channel during ONU forwarding. 
When two users of the ONU watch programs 1.2.3.4 and 11.11.11.1 at the same time, 
the ONU forwards data of the two programs to the user port. As a result, the STB 
receives streams of the two programs at the same time. The STB determines required 
multicast traffic according to the multicast IP address and discards undesired traffic. 
However, the size of the buffer on an STB is limited. If the buffer is insufficient, the STB 
will discard some packets of the two programs, and therefore frame freezing occurs in 
a program.

===== PAGE 1147 =====

===== PAGE 1148 =====

===== PAGE 1149 =====

===== PAGE 1150 =====

===== PAGE 1151 =====

===== PAGE 1152 =====

===== PAGE 1153 =====

===== PAGE 1154 =====
•
If the upstream optical power difference is used for calculating the attenuation of an 
optical splitter, only the ONU to be tested needs to be powered on. That is, lasers on 
ONU ports connected to other ONTs on the same optical splitter are powered off to 
ensure accurate optical power calculation.
•
For example, if method 1 is used, only the laser on ONU 0, ONU 1, or ONU 2 needs to 
be powered on, and the lasers on other two ONUs are powered off.
•
Measurement of downstream optical power is recommended because it is simple.
•
If the optical power cannot be measured on site, it can be queried by running a 
command. The optical power of an optical splitter, however, cannot be queried by 
running a command. For details, see the description of the method of running a 
command to query optical power. When a large volume of data is transmitted, optical 
power values queried by running a command are accurate and stable. When a small 
volume of data is transmitted, however, the queried values may differ from the actual 
values by 2 dB at the maximum. Therefore, when a small volume of data is 
transmitted, it is not recommended that optical power value be obtained by running a 
command.
•
For the theoretical value of an optical attenuation, see the POL ODN Planning and 
Design.

===== PAGE 1155 =====

===== PAGE 1156 =====
•
When a large volume of data is transmitted, optical power values queried by running a 
command are accurate and stable. When a small volume of data is transmitted, 
however, the queried values may differ from the actual values by 2 dB at the 
maximum. Therefore, when a small volume of data is transmitted, it is not 
recommended that optical power value be obtained by running a command.
•
If the MDU or ONU supports optical power query, you can query the TX/RX optical 
power by running MDU commands or on the ONT web page.

===== PAGE 1157 =====
•
Using an optical power meter to measure the upstream optical power will interrupt 
services carried on the link.
•
Do not look into the optical ports or fiber connectors without eye protection. Take 
protection measures to ensure personal safety and avoid personal injury. Never put an 
optical port towards the flammables.
•
Before and after measuring optical power, clean fiber connectors. This is because a 
dirty fiber connected to a normal fiber connector will contaminate the normal fiber 
connector, leading to abnormal attenuation and reflection and therefore affecting the 
fiber link quality.
•
A normal ONU does not proactively send optical signals. A common optical power 
meter can measure only the upstream or downstream optical power at one time by 
interrupting the link. Therefore, a burst optical power meter is required for measuring 
the upstream optical power.

===== PAGE 1158 =====

===== PAGE 1159 =====
•
Using an optical power meter to measure the upstream optical power will interrupt 
services carried on the link.
•
Do not look into the optical ports or fiber connectors without eye protection. Take 
protection measures to ensure personal safety and avoid personal injury. Never put an 
optical port towards the flammables.
•
Before and after measuring optical power, clean fiber connectors. This is because a 
dirty fiber connected to a normal fiber connector will contaminate the normal fiber 
connector, leading to abnormal attenuation and reflection and therefore affecting the 
fiber link quality.

===== PAGE 1160 =====

===== PAGE 1161 =====
•
An optical time domain reflectometer (OTDR) is usually used to locate abnormal 
attenuation on an optical line.
•
For details about how to use an OTDR, see the OTDR user guide.

===== PAGE 1162 =====

===== PAGE 1163 =====
•
Even a dust particle with a diameter of 1 μm on a single-mode fiber can block 1% 
optical signals and leads to 0.05 dB attenuation loss. A dust particle with a diameter of 
9 μm is hard to be seen without a microscope, but it completely blocks the core of a 
fiber. In addition to dust particles, there are other types of contaminants that also need 
to be cleaned, including the following: oil (usually from the hand), condensation 
residue of water vapor in the air; powder (evaporative residue of water or solvent).
•
If these contaminants are not removed, the device may be damaged. These 
contaminants are more difficult to clean than dust.
•
Tools, instruments, and materials:
▫
Optical power meter: used for testing whether the laser on a fiber connector is 
disabled.
▫
Lint-free wipe: a piece of long silk cotton specially used for cleaning a fiber 
connector endface.
▫
Lint-free swab: used for cleaning an optical module port or a fiber adapter. It has 
two specifications: a 2.5 mm diameter and a 1.25 mm diameter. You can select 
one according to the connector type (lint-free swab with a 2.5 mm diameter for 
an SC/FC connector, and that with a 1.25 mm diameter for an LC or MTRJ
connector).
▫
Protective cap: used on a fiber connector, an optical module port, or a fiber 
adapter.
▫
Cleaning tool box: used for placing lint-free wipes and protective caps. Place lint-
free wipes and protective caps separately from other tools.
▫
Cleaning reagent (alcohol): used for cleaning fiber connectors. It is flammable 
and therefore must be safely stored and kept clean.
▫
Fiber endface magnifier: a 400x microscope used for checking whether a fiber 
connector endface is clean and smooth.

===== PAGE 1164 =====
•
A lint-free wipe can be used only once. Use the portion of the lint-free wipe that is not 
touched by hands.
•
You can use a fiber end magnifier to check whether a fiber connector endface is clean 
and smooth.

===== PAGE 1165 =====
•
Use a lint-free swab with a 2.5 mm diameter for a port of the SC or FC type and that 
with a 1.25 mm diameter for a port of the LC or MTRJ type.

===== PAGE 1166 =====
•
Use a lint-free swab with a 2.5 mm diameter for a fiber adapter of the SC or FC type 
and that with a 1.25 mm diameter for a fiber adapter of the LC or MTRJ type.
•
Use an ultrasonic cleaner to clean fiber adapters when there are a large quantity of 
them.
